## 0e. What changed in revision 5 — the D1.5a sync (2026-09-01)

**The ruling.** ADR-072 **D1.5a**: *the memory gate is the ONLY admission control, idle tabs and idle browsers are closed, and every other limit is deleted from the codebase.* Deleted, not merely left unused: `tools.browser.max_tabs` (`pkg/config/config.go:3633`), `tools.browser.max_total_tabs` (`pkg/config/config.go:3678`), the `TryOpenTab` / `ReleaseTab` / `reservedTabs` reservation machinery (`pkg/tools/browser/coordinator.go:137`, `:782-813`), and the proposed-but-never-built `max_browsers` and `--renderer-process-limit`.

**This document is lightly affected, and that is the honest answer rather than a shortcut.** The pool has been out of scope at §1 since revision 2, and §14.3's M1/M8/M9 row already recorded a check that found no dependence on it. **Revision 5 changes no FR, no user story, no BDD scenario, no dataset row, no success criterion and no traceability row** — every count in this document is unchanged. It changes four notes, adds one integration boundary, and records one real interaction that nobody had found.

| # | Change | Driven by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Three citations are annotated where D1.5a supersedes what they point at**, rather than left pointing at withdrawn arithmetic: §0d's `D1.1b` row (the renderer bound is gone and admission is memory alone), its `D1.1c` row (the soft cap and its `+1` ceiling are gone; LRU eviction survives as the *response* to pressure, not as a cap), and §1's out-of-scope bullet, which named *"the pool (**cap**, eviction, idle close, crash containment)"*. | ADR **D1.5a** |
| 2 | **One real interaction is found and recorded — the only place the deletion reaches code this spec owns.** A `target="_blank"` click adopts a new tab, and the refusal is rendered to the agent by `applyReconcileOutcome`, a **D2-owned** function whose reason switch is `max_tabs`-shaped today. New §6 bullet, new §12 **B-8**, new sub-item (e) under §15 item 2. **Deliberately not a new FR:** the refusal itself is D1's requirement; what D2 owns is the sentence the agent reads, and §1 keeps the pool out of scope. | ADR **D1.5a** · verified in source |
| 3 | **`--renderer-process-limit` is recorded as *dissolved*, not silently dropped.** D1.6 introduced it to bound per-instance cost for a capacity formula that no longer exists, and it **weakens site isolation** for pages beyond the limit. With capacity governed by live memory there is no reason to set it, so **full site isolation is retained** — a security improvement, and the concern is dissolved rather than mitigated. Nothing in D2 ever read the flag; it is noted so a later reader does not restore it as an oversight. | ADR **D1.5a** |
| 4 | **Per-browser cost has a number and, more importantly, a scope: ≈182 MB, *idle*.** Measured on the operator's own macOS machine against **Chrome for Testing** — the binary Omnipus launches — as 182 MB fixed (125 MB browser+utility, 57 MB GPU) plus ≈30 MB per renderer. A **capturing** browser additionally costs the injected capture extension plus encoding, and that increment is **unmeasured**. §6's CI process-budget bullet is the one place in this document that reasons about browser memory, and it now carries both halves. | ADR **D1.5a** measurement block |
| 5 | **§12 A-19 gains the distinction whoever rules on it needs:** Jim's `browser_evaluate` exposure is **latent, not live** — and the sharper defect is not the grant but the split between the surface that shows it and the control that holds it shut. *(**Superseded within the day by ADR D1.9b ruling 2**, which closed the split by seeding the flag `true`. The exposure is now **live**; the analysis is what the ruling acted on. §0f, FR-042.)* | Operator, 2026-09-01 |
| 6 | **§14.3's "no dependence on the pool" check is re-run and honestly narrowed.** It held for FRs, scenarios and acceptance criteria and still does. It did **not** hold for the agent-facing message, which change 2 corrects. A check that was right about the requirements and wrong about the prose is worth saying out loud. | This sync |

**What was searched for, and found absent.** `max_tabs`, `MaxTabs`, `max_total_tabs`, `TryOpenTab`, `ReleaseTab`, `reservedTabs`, `--renderer-process-limit`, `PER_BROWSER_COST` — **zero occurrences in this document** before this section was written. `max_browsers` appeared exactly twice, and both are descriptions of **D1's** FR-038 rather than a D2 dependency (§9's FR-038 row and §15 item 2's disambiguation box); both are annotated rather than deleted, because the collision they warn about is unaffected by whether the cap survives.

**Nothing here is sized on the old cap of 5.** No dataset row, fixture, arithmetic literal or scenario counts tabs at all; the largest tab count any scenario needs is **three** (S-32, the dialog raised on tab index 2), which was never near the cap and is not near anything now. **The dialog listener is unaffected in mechanism and strengthened in obligation.** Its arming set is a `map[target.ID]struct{}` keyed per tab (§2.1, FR-014, edit site 15), so an unbounded tab set costs one map entry per tab and no rearrangement: FR-013/FR-014 hold verbatim. What D1.5a *does* change is the weight on the eviction half — idle close is now load-bearing rather than housekeeping, and edit site 15 already requires every teardown site (including the reaper and every whole-Chrome teardown D1 introduces) to evict the tab's key, its pending-dialog entry and its `lastActivation`. **More teardowns, more often, against the same requirement.** A missed eviction was always the ADR-041-F3 wedge returning silently; it is now simply a more frequent way to reach it.

**The risk D1.5a accepts, restated for a D2 reader.** With no counter anywhere, a runaway agent opening tabs in a loop is bounded **only** by memory pressure, and idle close plus the pressure gate are the entire defence. That is D1's risk to carry, and D2 neither adds to it nor mitigates it — but it is why §6's new bullet insists the refusal reach the agent as a *sentence it can act on*: an agent that cannot tell "the host is out of memory" from "it could not be adopted" will retry the loop.

**Still open, unchanged: §15 items 1 (A-3), 7 (A-20) and 8 (A-19).** **A-20 remains the expensive one and still ranks first.** An explicit take-control tool takes the surface **11 → 18**, needs an explicit, literal policy entry for **every** seeded agent or **boot aborts** (Hard Constraint #6), moves the Tier-3 fixture **62 → 69** and the arithmetic literal **87 → 94**, and needs its own FR, US, scenario and test. **Deciding it after Stream E1 lands means redoing Stream E1.** D1.5a does not touch it, and this sync does not reduce its cost.

> **⚠️ Superseded the same day — read §0f.** All three were ruled by ADR-072 **D1.9b** on 2026-09-01. **The figures in the paragraph above are the cost of a shape that was NOT chosen and must not be quoted as this spec's arithmetic:** there is no seventh tool, so 18, 69 and 94 never happen. The live numbers are **11 → 17**, fixture **62 → 68**, union **87 → 93**. The paragraph is kept unedited because it is the estimate the ruling was made against, and because A-20's cheap resolution is only legible next to what it avoided.

## 0f. What changed in revision 6 — the D1.9b rulings (2026-09-01)

**Three rulings landed on this document on the day revision 5 shipped, and between them they close every open item it carried.** ADR-072 **D1.9b** (`86666daaa`) plus its **D2.4 correction** (`356e587d3`). D1.9b's fourth ruling — periodic cache trimming to bound profile disk — is **D1's**; nothing in D2 reads profile size, and it is recorded here only so a reader does not go looking for its absorption.

| # | Ruling | What it closes | What it costs this document |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Taking the operator's tab is IMPLICIT — there is no take-control tool.** An agent acquires the shared tab by acting on it. | **§12 A-20** (§15 item 7) | **Nothing, and that is the finding.** Surface stays **11 → 17**; no seventh policy entry, so no `ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` boot-abort risk (Hard Constraint #6); no new row in §11's seeded table or edit sites 1-7; no new FR, US, scenario or test; **Stream E1 is not redone.** The mitigation the ruling names is recorded (`controlledResult`, `pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:962`), **and so is the fact that it does not reach the two tools this spec exempts — new §12 A-22.** |
| 2 | **`browser_evaluate` is ENABLED.** `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` seeded `true`, so the policy surface and the runtime gate finally agree. | **§12 A-19** (§15 item 8) | **The only ruling that adds a requirement.** New **FR-042**, **US-20**, **S-66/S-67/S-68**, **§10 order 4a** — because the seed is a real edit in Stream E1's own file, and revision 5's "no D2 FR changes on either answer" was true of a *revocation* and false of an *enablement*. Every place describing the tool as inert is corrected. The widening is stated once and routed to the **release notes** (US-20/AC5). |
| 3 | **`browser_snapshot` is Tier 3 — searchable only.** ADR-071's previewed set left intact. | **§12 A-3** (§15 item 1) | **No production edit.** Fixture **62 → 68**, union **87 → 93**, previewed unchanged at **7**; edit sites 11 and 12 struck; **Stream E2 unblocked**. **The consequence is carried, not softened:** the snapshot is not a default, D2.4's claim to the contrary was false, and **agents will keep reaching for `browser_screenshot`** — new **§12 B-9**, wired into §4's observable, SC-001 and holdout 1. *(**Revision 7 correction:** this row said the ADR claim "is corrected". It is not — `356e587d3` adds a **banner** and deletes nothing, so D2.4's Decision sentence still says "the default way an agent reads a page", D2.8's table row still says "open question", and ADR §6 still lists the question as open. **§0c AC-5**, round-4 M1.)* |

**Every number above was re-derived in this worktree at revision-6 time, not carried from the estimates.** `pkg/tools/browser/register.go::RegisterTools` issues eleven `RegisterReplacing` calls (`:65-81`), plus this spec's six → **17**. `tier3SearchOnlyToolNames` (`pkg/tools/manifest_test.go:667-681`) holds **62** quoted names, counted → **68**. `TestVisibility_TierArithmetic` (`:694-744`) pins `17 + 7 + 62 + 1 = 87` → `17 + 7 + 68 + 1 = **93**`. **One trap for whoever edits that test:** the `17` in it is `FullManifestToolNames`, unrelated to this spec's "11 → 17" browser surface despite the matching digits.

**Two things revision 6 declines to do.**
1. **It does not soften ruling 3's consequence into "the snapshot is available".** The ADR itself states that agents will keep screenshotting; a spec that quietly recorded Tier 3 as a scheduling detail would have hidden the trade the ruling deliberately made.
2. **It does not treat ruling 2 as costless.** The ruling was taken with the widening on the table and it stands — but a seed of `true` on a field carrying `omitempty` creates a defect that a seed of `false` could not (**FR-042/AC4, S-68**), and that is a consequence of the ruling, not an objection to it.

**One thing revision 6 escalates, and it is a narrowing rather than a disagreement.** ADR D1.9b ruling 1 bounds implicit tab acquisition with *"`controlledResult` already defers an agent while a human drives"*. That is true of eleven tools and **false of `browser_snapshot` and `browser_handle_dialog`**, both exempt by this spec's own **FR-038** and **FR-035** — so a human at the wheel of the operator's shared tab does not stop an agent reading that tab's field values or answering a dialog on it. Neither exemption should be withdrawn. **§12 A-22, §15 item 11.**

## 0. What changed in revision 4, in one screen

Revision 4 answers the consolidated grill of 2026-09-01 and absorbs the ADR's consolidation, its renumbering, and the **D1.9a** ruling that landed after it.

| # | Change | Driven by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Every ADR citation is re-derived, not renamed.** The ADR was consolidated and renumbered on 2026-09-01; `D1.1a` (×3) and `D1.0a` (×2) no longer exist, and two old numbers now resolve to **different** sections. The full old→new map, and the two that silently mis-resolve, are in **§0d**. | Consolidated grill **M2** |
| 2 | **Four conflicts with the source ADR are recorded in one place (§0c) instead of scattered** — most importantly that the ADR still asserts the ≤150 ms p95 budget **SC-004 withdrew in full**, and that **the ADR's §9 "downstream documents that must change" has no section for this spec at all**, so every conflict this document carries is invisible to an ADR reader. | Consolidated grill **M4** |
| 3 | **The snapshot's one mandated visibility mitigation is corrected where it was silently false: delegated work.** FR-028's chat-thread claim held only for a **parent** turn. A delegated sub-turn's tool calls sit inside a span gated by `shouldRenderSubagentSpan`, which returns `verboseChatEnabled` — default **`false`**. New **FR-039** states the real two-population behaviour and tests both halves; §4, §5, S-43 and order 23 follow. **One ADR claim is narrowed on evidence:** the ActivityPanel *does* carry a **delegated** snapshot (inside its span's step list), under three limits. | Consolidated grill **M5** |
| 4 | **FR-028's reading surface no longer rests on a viewer this spec documents as broken.** The Audit Log viewer blanks on any install carrying a dotted event name — filed as **#667** (OPEN), now cited by number. The audit half's **primary** surface becomes the one that works today (`GET /api/v1/audit-log` and the JSONL file); the viewer is named as the operator-friendly surface **conditional on #667**. | Consolidated grill **M6** |
| 5 | **Jim's `browser_evaluate` is surfaced, not decided.** ADR §6 asks whether the grant survives the sharing ruling; this spec reasoned *from* the ten-allow/one-deny carve-out as settled precedent without noting **D1.10 changed the premise**. New **§12 A-19** records it, and adds the fact the ADR lacks: the tool is gated only by `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` at `Execute` — **outside the tool-policy surface** — so an operator reading policy cannot see the one control that disables it. | Consolidated grill **M11** |
| 6 | **The lease is rescoped to what D1.9a left of it.** *(**Superseded by ADR D1.9c, 2026-09-02: tabs are owned by the SESSION, not the agent.** The rescoping stands — only the ownership key moved. Read "agent" as "session" in this row; §0g.)* Tabs stay per agent; only the operator's tab is shared, so the lease is **agent-vs-agent on the operator's shared tab only** — agents on their own tabs do not contend. §4's tombstone, FR-035 and FR-038 say so. §3's membership rule is replaced with D1's real one (*lease iff `controlledResult`-gated*), not the registry derivation D1 discarded. | ADR **D1.9a** · consolidated grill **M1** |
| 7 | **The `controlledResult` wiring on the four new action tools gets a number.** It was prose only, while D1's test 18 depends on it. New **FR-040**, with US-19, S-64 and a test. The exemptions had full coverage; the inclusions had none. | Consolidated grill **M2 (context)** |
| 8 | **"Take control on request" is specced in both shapes and flagged for ruling** (§12 A-20, §15 item 7). D1.9a leaves open whether it is an explicit tool or an implicit acquisition on first write to the operator's tab. The wording reads explicit; an explicit tool would change this spec's **tool count, tier fixtures and policy seeds**, so it cannot be left implicit-by-default. *(**Superseded by revision 6:** ADR **D1.9b ruling 1** rules it IMPLICIT. No seventh tool, none of the three figures move. §0f.)* | ADR **D1.9a** (since RULED by D1.9b) |
| 9 | **The unattended-`accept:true` gap is answered.** `browser_handle_dialog` is exempt from both gates and the ADR permits it *"even while a human holds control"* — so nothing stated what stops a delegated unattended agent accepting `confirm("Delete this account?")` on the operator's signed-in session. New **FR-041** refuses `accept:true` on a turn that has no one to ask, using the **existing** `AutoDenyAsk` signal; dismissal stays available always, so the unwedge guarantee is untouched. | Consolidated grill, unasked question |
| 10 | **§12 A-16 is RULED, not DECIDED-overrulable.** The ADR erratum this spec asked for **already landed** — D2.9's table reads `deny` for Mia and Ava, with footnote ¹ spelling out `denyAllThenOverride` plus most-restrictive-wins. §15 item 3 is closed. | Consolidated grill **m4** |
| 11 | **Audit is per action for write-class tools, and nothing here assumed otherwise.** Checked: this spec's two audit events (FR-028, FR-031) fire **per invocation**, never first-use-only. ADR **D2.11** rejects first-use auditing by name; no change was needed and the check is recorded so it is not re-run. | ADR **D2.11** |

## 0c. Conflicts with ADR-072 that this spec cannot fix

**This spec does not edit the ADR.** These are recorded here so the conflicts are tracked rather than discovered by whoever implements from the wrong document — the same discipline the ADR's own §9 applies in the other direction.

**And the structural problem first, because it makes the rest invisible.** ADR-072 **§9 ("Downstream documents that must change") names the D1 spec, issue #509, ADR-048 and root `CLAUDE.md` — and does not name this document at all.** So an ADR reader has no way to learn that its D2 acceptance criteria conflict with the spec written to implement them. **The remedy is an ADR §9.5 for this spec, and it is the ADR owner's to write**, not this spec's to insert.

| # | The ADR says | This spec says | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AC-1** | **§3.3 criterion 22** and **§4 ("D2 — lost / risked")**: the actionability pre-check is *"Budgeted at ≤150 ms p95"* on the `performance-2x` profile | **SC-004 withdraws that budget in full**, and replaces it with FR-007's assertable CDP round-trip count plus a **non-gating recorded measurement** | **The ADR is one revision behind this spec, not the other way round.** The withdrawal predates the ADR's consolidation and the consolidation did not absorb it. Four defects, all conceded and none disputed: *(i)* **the statistic was undefined** — p95-of-after minus p95-of-before is not the p95 of paired deltas, and only the second is a per-call budget; *(ii)* **no harness existed**, and a Go benchmark cannot compare two builds; *(iii)* **it was unrunnable in CI**, which this project makes the authority for Go results, so the criterion had no green anyone would ever see; *(iv)* at n=100 on a host **the ADR's own §7 measures at 85–99% utilisation**, the 5th-worst sample's spread swamps 150 ms in both directions. Separately, 150 ms is roughly two orders of magnitude larger than a local CDP round trip, so a gate costing 20× the click it guards would still have passed. **Required of the ADR:** strike criterion 22 and the §4 bullet, or restate them as SC-004's recorded measurement. **Until it does, an implementer reading the ADR will build a gate this spec has no test for.** |
| **AC-2** | **D2.11** names the **ActivityPanel** as unreachable for a snapshot: *"A `browser_snapshot` never appears there"* | True for a **parent-turn** snapshot; **false for a delegated one** | **Narrowed on evidence, in this spec's favour and against it at once.** A delegated sub-agent's span *is* aggregated by the panel and its `steps` *are* rendered (`src/hooks/useRunningActivity.ts:549`, `src/components/chat/ActivityPanel.tsx:161-177`, `src/lib/toolVisibility.ts:254-259`), so a delegated snapshot **is** reachable there — under three limits that stop it being a default surface (FR-039). A parent-turn snapshot is none of the three things the panel aggregates, so for that case the ADR is right. **Required of the ADR:** replace the absolute with the two-population statement. |
| **AC-3** | **D2.11** cites `cdproto/accessibility/types.go:206` for the AX node's `Value` field | That line is `AXPropertySource.Value`, a different struct; the field is `Node.Value` at **`types.go:461`** | Erratum, no design impact. Unchanged from revision 3 (§12 A-14). The ADR's own consolidated D2.11 now cites `:461` correctly — **this row is DISCHARGED** and is kept only so the correction is not re-derived. |
| **AC-4** | **D2.9's table** shows `browser_upload_file` as `ask` for Mia and Ava | They resolve **`deny`** | **DISCHARGED.** The consolidated ADR carries the erratum with footnote ¹, spelling out `denyAllThenOverride` plus most-restrictive-wins. §12 A-16 is now **RULED**. |
| **AC-5** *(new in revision 7 — round-4 **M1**)* | **D2.4's Decision sentence still reads *"as the default way an agent reads a page"***, D2.8's tier table still lists `browser_snapshot` as **"open question — §6"** with the Tier-2 alternative, and **§6's open-question list still asks *"Is `browser_snapshot` Tier 3?"*** | All three were settled by **D1.9b ruling 3**: Tier 3, searchable only, previewed set unchanged at 7 | **THIS SPEC OVERSTATED THE CORRECTION AND THE OVERSTATEMENT IS FIXED HERE.** Revisions 6 said in five places that the claim *"has been corrected in the ADR (`356e587d3`)"*. **Re-derived: `356e587d3` is `8 insertions(+)`, zero deletions** — it adds a **banner above** D2.4 and changes nothing below it. So the ADR now says both things: the banner says "not a default", the Decision sentence eleven lines later still says "as the default way an agent reads a page". **A correction that leaves the corrected sentence in place is a second claim, not a retraction** — and an implementer who reads the Decision line without scrolling up reads the false one. Two further sites the banner does not reach at all: the **D2.8 tier table row** (`browser_snapshot` → *"open question — §6 … Either it earns Tier 2 (and `previewedLazyToolNames` grows from 7 to 8) or D2.4's wording overclaims"*), which contradicts FR-020's *"previewed unchanged at 7"* and this spec's struck edit sites 11-12; and **§6's open-question bullet**, which lists a ruled question as open. **Required of the ADR (owner's call, three edits):** strike or rewrite D2.4's Decision sentence; replace D2.8's row with `3 — search-only`; delete §6's bullet. **What this spec does about it:** every claim of the form "corrected in the ADR" is downgraded to "a correction banner was added; the Decision sentence, D2.8's row and §6's bullet still say the old thing" — §0f row 3, §4's observable, §6's manifest bullet, §12 A-3, §15 item 1 and §15's closing paragraph. §15 item 10 carries the three edits. |

## 0d. ADR-072 was renumbered; every D1 citation here was re-derived

The ADR was consolidated on 2026-09-01 (`22ceff6f`). Its D1 sections were renumbered, and **two old numbers now resolve to a different, wrong section** — so a search-and-replace would have produced citations that look correct and point somewhere else. Every D1 citation in this document was re-derived by reading the current ADR.

| Old citation (revisions 2–3) | Subject | Current section | Trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `D1.0a` | `CaptureSharedContext: true` is the shipped default, so isolation is **off** today | **D1.3** | The ADR no longer poses this as an open operator question. D1.3 records it as today's state and **D1.4 replaces the primitive**, which removes the question rather than answering it. This spec's "(d) D1.0a's `CaptureSharedContext` default" boundary item is rewritten accordingly. |
| `D1.1a` | One Chrome process per workspace, own `--user-data-dir` | **D1.4** | — |
| `D1.1b` | Renderer bound, memory formula, admission gate | **D1.5** (sizing) + **D1.6** (site isolation is a floor, not a knob) | One old section became two — **and both are superseded by D1.5a** (2026-09-01): the renderer bound (`--renderer-process-limit`) is deleted, so site isolation stays whole, and admission is the live-memory headroom check alone. Read D1.5/D1.6 as the record of how the design reached one control, not as the control. |
| `D1.1c` | LRU eviction, no error surface, soft cap with a `+1` hard ceiling | **D1.7** | **The cap half is deleted by D1.5a** — there is no soft cap and no `+1` ceiling, so the "pressure wins over the cap" tie-break has nothing left to tie with. **LRU eviction survives**, as the *response* to memory pressure rather than as the enforcement of a ceiling. |
| `D1.1d` | Idle close, crash containment, boot-marker reconciliation, boot warm — **and the `browser_handle_dialog` exemption** | **D1.8** | The dialog exemption FR-035 rests on sits inside a **pool-lifecycle** section, which is not where a reader looks for it. Cited precisely here for that reason. |
| `D1.2` | Every agent on a workspace shares its browser and logins, **delegated work included** | **D1.10** | **⚠️ The dangerous one.** `D1.2` still exists and is now *"Why the workspace — and why not the agent or the conversation"*. A stale `D1.2` citation resolves cleanly to a real section that says something else entirely. |
| `D1.5` | `ListTabs` must distinguish "no context" from "no tabs" | **D1.12** | **⚠️ The second dangerous one.** `D1.5` is now *"Sizing the pool"*. |
| — *(new, no old number)* | ~~**Tabs stay per agent; the operator's tab is the shared one**~~ **SUPERSEDED by D1.9c** — see the two rows below | **D1.9a** | Landed 2026-09-01, after the consolidation. It **narrows the write lease** to agent-vs-agent on the operator's shared tab. The one question it left open on this spec's surface — §12 A-20 — was **ruled the same day by D1.9a's successor.** |
| — *(new, no old number)* | **Four rulings closing D1's open questions**, of which three land on this document: implicit tab acquisition, `browser_evaluate` enabled, `browser_snapshot` at Tier 3 | **D1.9b** | Landed 2026-09-01 (`86666daaa`), with a **D2.4 correction** in `356e587d3` **that is a banner and not a retraction — §0c AC-5**. Closes **A-20**, **A-19** and **A-3** — every open ruling this document carried. §0f. Its fourth ruling (periodic cache trimming of the profile disk) is **D1's**; nothing in D2 reads profile size. |
| — *(new, no old number)* | **Tabs are owned by the SESSION, not the agent** — supersedes D1.9a | **D1.9c** | Landed 2026-09-02 (`c1f21da69`). **One browser per workspace and one cookie jar are unchanged; only the ownership key moves, agent → session.** The operator's own tab stays workspace-owned and visible to every agent (D1.9a's second half stands), and acquisition stays implicit (D1.9b ruling 1). **D2's surface is untouched** — this document never reads the key — but every sentence that said *"tabs stay per agent"* is now wrong and is corrected: §0 row 6, §1's scope note, §3's interface contract, §4's tombstone, §6's boundary bullet, US-9/AC7, US-10/AC5, FR-035, FR-038, holdout 12 and §15 item 2(d). **The residual is D1's and is named, not assumed away** — §15 item 2(f). |
| — *(new, no old number)* | **The dead `evaluate_enabled` flag is deleted outright** | **D1.9d** | Landed 2026-09-02 (`c1f21da69`). `Tools.Browser.EvaluateEnabled`, its `json:"evaluate_enabled"` tag and `OMNIPUS_TOOLS_BROWSER_EVALUATE_ENABLED` go, so **exactly one switch exists**; the deny-by-default doc comments that describe a posture D1.9b ruling 2 reversed are corrected in the same change. **This one DOES land on D2**, because both halves are edits in Stream E1's own files — **FR-043**, US-21, S-69/S-70/S-71, §10 order 4b, edit sites 2e-2j. §0g. |

`D2.0`–`D2.11` are **unchanged** by the consolidation — same numbers, same subjects. Each of the twelve section titles was re-read at revision-4 time against the current ADR, so every `D2.x` citation in this document still resolves to the subject it names. **No count is claimed**: an exact figure would go stale on the next edit to this file and could not be checked by a reader, which is the kind of unverifiable precision ADR-072 §8 exists to discourage.

## 1. Overview / Actors / Scope

**Problem.** The browser tool surface lets an agent *read* a page and lets it *fail* on one. It ships eleven tools (`pkg/tools/browser/register.go::RegisterTools`). A `<select>` cannot be operated at all. There is no Enter key, no hover, no file attach, and a page that calls `alert()` stops the tab answering CDP with no way out. Targeting is CSS-or-visible-text only; the CDP Accessibility domain is unused (**verified: zero occurrences of `getFullAXTree`/`queryAXTree`/`cdproto/accessibility` across `pkg/` and `src/`**). And the actionability contract is one quarter built: `ClickTool.Execute` and `TypeTool.Execute` prepend `chromedp.WaitVisible`, `GetTextTool.Execute` uses `WaitReady`; nothing anywhere checks enabled-ness, positional stability, or whether a click would actually land on the element rather than an overlay.

**Solution (ADR-072 D2).** Complete the surface along four axes, all additive:

1. **Find** — role + accessible name as a third locator alongside CSS and visible text, sourced from CDP Accessibility, wired into the *same* resolution seam (`text_selector.go::resolveActionSelector`) so every action tool inherits it.
2. **Wait** — finish the actionability gate (visible → stable → enabled → hit-testable) in one shared pre-action path, with an error that names *which* condition failed, a revert switch, and per-condition telemetry.
3. **Act** — six new tools: `browser_select_option`, `browser_press_key`, `browser_hover`, `browser_upload_file`, `browser_handle_dialog`, `browser_snapshot`.
4. **Route** — the `file://` rejection names the supported alternative in the error **and** the agents that receive it can actually call that alternative.

**Actors.** `resolveActionSelector` / `resolvePseudoOnlySelector` (`pkg/tools/browser/text_selector.go`) — the target-resolution seam every action tool already funnels through; the eleven tool structs (`tools.go`, `tabs.go`); `BrowserManager` (session/tab ownership, listener install); `BrowserManager.ValidateURL`; the tool-policy seeding pair (`pkg/config/defaults.go`, `pkg/coreagent/core.go`); the manifest tier authority (`pkg/tools/manifest.go`); the coverage validator (`pkg/config/validate.go::ValidateToolPolicyCoverage`); `pkg/audit::Emit` (new consumer).

**In scope (v1):**
- Role + accessible-name resolution (D2.1), through the existing marker-attribute seam.
- The four-condition actionability gate (D2.2) with a closed, named failure set, a **CDP round-trip budget**, a revert switch and per-condition telemetry.
- The six new tools (D2.3, D2.4) — **except that `browser_upload_file`'s registration is gated on issue #659** (FR-029).
- The `serve_web` pointer in the `file://` error, **and the policy change that makes it reachable** (D2.5 + FR-030).
- Manifest tier assignment + the drift tests it pins, **including a new test that can actually detect Tier-3 drift** (D2.8, FR-036).
- Tool-policy seeding for all six, on **every** seeded agent, so boot does not abort (D2.9, Hard Constraint #6).
- The snapshot's information-disclosure posture (D2.11 bullet 3) **as ruled**: values by default, plus the two mandated mitigations — **and, new in revision 4, an honest statement of where the visible-in-thread mitigation does not reach (FR-039)**, which is delegated work, the population the sharing ruling widened the risk to.
- **The unattended-`accept` guard on `browser_handle_dialog` (FR-041).** Not a new gate and not a policy value — an argument-level refusal on a turn that has nobody to ask, using the existing `AutoDenyAsk` signal. `{accept:false}` is never refused, so the recovery guarantee FR-035 exists for is untouched.
- The **two lease exemptions** — `browser_handle_dialog` (FR-035) and `browser_snapshot` (FR-038) — **and the four inclusions** (FR-040), which had no requirement of their own until revision 4.
- **`controlledResult` on the four new action tools (FR-040).** Separate from the lease and not new machinery: it is the existing human-holds-the-wheel check every action tool already calls. It is numbered here because **D1's own test 18 depends on D2 wiring it**, and prose is not a thing a test can cite.

> **Not in scope, and moved out in revision 2: the per-browsing-context write lease (D2.10).** Revision 2's change 12 relocated it to `browser-workspace-ownership-spec.md` §14 by operator ruling, but the in-scope list, §4's observable contract and §13's holdouts kept describing it as D2 work for a whole revision (round-2 **M4**). It is now removed from all four places. **D2 defines no lease, tests no lease and asserts no lease behaviour** — it only names the two tools that sit outside it and the four that sit inside it.
>
> **And the lease is now much smaller than when it was relocated (ADR D1.9c, 2026-09-02, superseding D1.9a).** The operator ruled that **tabs are owned by the SESSION, not the agent** — *"it should not be per agent but per session, no matter which agent is on it"* — so a tab stays with the chat it was opened in, and a scheduled run gets its own session and its own tabs. **Only a tab the operator opens is shared**, visible to every agent on the workspace and drivable by an agent *on request*. Two **sessions** working on their own tabs therefore **do not contend at all**, and the general "two writers, one browsing context" case the lease was designed for disappears. What is left is **contention on the operator's one shared tab**, which is where D1's lease now lives. D2's obligation is unchanged in shape and narrower in reach: the four action tools take it (FR-040 wires the human-control half; D1 wires the lease itself), and the two recovery/read verbs do not (FR-035, FR-038).
>
> **Why the key moved, and the one thing it does NOT close.** D1.9a's per-agent key had a hole D1.9c removes by construction: **one agent can have two concurrent turns** — a heartbeat while a chat is live, or the same agent dispatched twice by parallel delegation — and under a per-agent key those two shared one tab set with **no arbiter at all**. Different sessions hold different tabs, so they cannot collide. **The residual is stated rather than assumed away, and it is D1's to establish from code:** two concurrent turns *in the same session* would still contend, which is a session-serialisation question, not a browser one. **This document does not answer it and creates no requirement for it** — §15 item 2(f). **Nothing in D2 reads the ownership key**, which is why the whole change costs this document reference updates and no FR.

**Out of scope, and why:**
- **All of D1** — ownership, workspace re-keying, live-panel manager resolution, the workspace-less resolution rule, and the pool (admission on live memory, eviction, idle close, crash containment). Sibling spec. *(Revision 4 wrote "cap" here. **ADR D1.5a deletes every cap**: the memory gate is the only admission control, and idle close — of tabs and now of whole browsers — is load-bearing rather than housekeeping. The bullet's point is unchanged: all of it is D1's.)* **Including the `CaptureSharedContext` default**, which revision 3 cited as an open `D1.0a` question: the consolidated ADR's **D1.3** records `CaptureSharedContext: true` as today's shipped default and **D1.4 replaces the isolation primitive outright**, which removes the question rather than answering it. Nothing in D2 depends on either, because the write lease is keyed by whatever browsing-context key D1 lands on and D2 never reads the key's value.
- **D2.11 bullets 1 and 2** (elevation-of-privilege disclosure in the team-editing UI; the browsing-context-creation audit event). Both act on a *browsing context* whose key D1 decides. Named as an integration boundary in §6. **Note this spec DOES add two audit events of its own** (FR-028, FR-031) — they are keyed by tool invocation, not by browsing context, so they do not fork D1's decision.
- **D2.6's own exclusions**: replacing chromedp with playwright-go (Hard Constraint #1); network interception, frame targeting, drag-and-drop, cookie/storage manipulation.
- **Human-facing dialog UX.** `browser_handle_dialog` is agent-facing. A human who takes the wheel on a tab with an open dialog is wedged today and stays wedged after this work — see §6 and US-12's note. FR-035 removes the *compounding* case the grill found (agent locked out too); it does not give the human a button.

## 2. Existing Codebase Context

### 2.1 Symbols involved

Cited as `file::symbol` (grill m2). Line numbers appear only for immovable fixtures.

| Symbol | Role | Context (verified at revision-2 time) |
|---|---|---|
| `text_selector.go::resolveActionSelector` | **modifies** | The seam. Takes `(selector, text)`, returns `(target, cleanup, err)` where `target` is a CSS string — either the caller's own selector, or the internal marker selector `[data-omnipus-tsel="<tok>"]` (`text_selector.go::textMarkerAttr`). Role/name enters **here** and returns the same marker shape, so every downstream `chromedp` `ByQuery` action is untouched. |
| `text_selector.go::resolvePseudoOnlySelector` | **modifies** | The second entry point — `browser_type` uses this, **not** `resolveActionSelector`, because its `text` arg is the value to type, not a locator. Role/name must be added to **both** or `browser_type` silently misses the new locator. |
| `text_selector.go::wrapTextMatch` | **reuses** | Shared `(marker, err) → (target, cleanup, err)` adapter. The AX path returns through it unchanged. |
| `text_selector.go::displayLocator` / `::scrubMarkerFromError` | **modifies** | Error-surface helpers. `displayLocator` must learn to render `role=button name="Submit"`; `scrubMarkerFromError` needs no change (it scrubs the marker attr, which the AX path also uses). |
| `text_selector.go::removeTextMarker` | **reuses** | Cleanup for the stamped marker. The AX branch returns through it unchanged. |
| `text_selector.go::textResolvePollInterval` | **reuses** | `150 * time.Millisecond`. The gate's retry cadence reuses it rather than inventing a second constant. |
| `tools.go::ClickTool.Execute` | **modifies** | `WaitVisible` → the new actionability gate. |
| `tools.go::TypeTool.Execute` | **modifies** | Same; the `clear` arg's `SetValue`+`SendKeys` sequence must run *after* the gate, not before. |
| `tools.go::GetTextTool.Execute` | **no wait change** | Read-only. `WaitReady` (DOM presence, 8 s budget `tools.go::getTextWaitTimeout`) is deliberate — `<title>` is present but never visible. The gate is for **action** tools; forcing it here would reintroduce the documented ~30 s hang. Gains the role/name locator only. |
| `tools.go::WaitTool.Execute` | **modifies (locator only)** | Gains role/name. Its own `WaitVisible` stays — `browser_wait`'s contract *is* visibility, not actionability. |
| `tools.go::controlledResult` | **reuses + extends** | Returns `{"deferred": true, "reason": …}` as a **non-error** result when a human holds the live view (`mgr.Live().IsControlled`). Its doc comment names seven callers (navigate/click/type/evaluate/switch_tab/close_tab/open_tab); read-only tools (`browser_screenshot`/`browser_get_text`/`browser_wait`) are deliberately ungated. The D1 write lease (`browser-workspace-ownership-spec.md` §14) returns the **same shape** — no prompt rewrite. Every new *action* tool must call it, **except `browser_handle_dialog`** (FR-035) and `browser_snapshot` (read-only). |
| `tools.go::capGetText` / `::maxGetTextChars` | **read, NOT reused** | `maxGetTextChars = config.DefaultBuiltinSuccessCap` = 64,000 (`pkg/config/context_settings.go::DefaultBuiltinSuccessCap`). **Verified: `capGetText` is `text[:maxGetTextChars] + getTextTruncationSuffix`, compared against `len(text)` — an arbitrary BYTE cut that can split a UTF-8 rune and always splits mid-node.** `browser_snapshot` obeys the **same constant** but **not the same mechanism** — see FR-017 and grill M3. |
| `manager.go::BrowserManager.ValidateURL` | **modifies** | `manager.go::blockedSchemes` covers `file`, `javascript`, `data`, `chrome`, `chrome-extension`. **One** format string serves all five: `"browser: %s:// URLs are blocked for security reasons"`. Only `file` has a supported alternative, so the pointer must be scheme-specific. |
| `manager.go::installTargetListenerLocked` | **pattern source, NOT modified** | Installs `chromedp.ListenTarget` on **`se.tabs[0]` only**, deliberately — `Target` discovery is browser-global so one listener suffices. Idempotence key: `sessionEntry.listenerTarget`, compared against the root tab's `targetID`; re-armed per ADR-041 fix F3 because "chromedp.ListenTarget's registration is scoped to the ctx it was given, so closing the tab that ctx belongs to silently ends the listener forever unless something re-installs it". **`Page.javascriptDialogOpening` is per-tab, not browser-global**, so the dialog listener is a SEPARATE installer with its own per-tab key — see Stream C and FR-014. |
| `manager.go::handleTargetEvent` | **reuses (pattern)** | Runs synchronously on the CDP dispatch goroutine and must never block or call `chromedp.Run` inline. Its own doc records that `mgr.Session()` runs a blocking `chromedp.Run` to **recreate a dead tab ctx** — the reason FR-014 needs a re-arm rule. The dialog listener inherits this discipline verbatim. |
| Tab-ctx creation sites | **modifies** | Where a tab ctx is born and the per-tab dialog listener must be armed: `manager.go::createFirstTab` (also the target of `Session`'s crash-recovery recreate), `manager.go::OpenTab`, `manager.go::adoptTarget` / `::adoptTargetWithRetry` (ADR-041 D2 adoption). Named exhaustively so FR-014 is implementable without a hunt. |
| `manager.go::ReapIdleSessions` | **modified by D2 — one eviction, added in revision 3** | Tears down idle browsing contexts and tabs under `m.mu`. Its interaction with the **write lease** is the D1 spec's concern, not this one — see the lease relocation note in §3. **But the dialog state is D2's own new state on `sessionEntry`, so its teardown is D2's (round-2 unasked-question 7).** Revision 2's edit site 15 said "evict at tab teardown" and named no site; the answer is **yes, the reaper is one of those sites**. Whatever tears a tab down must evict that tab's `dialogListeners` key, its pending-dialog entry and its `lastActivation` — the reaper included, alongside explicit `browser_close_tab` and the ctx-recreation path. **Missing the reaper is the ADR-041-F3 failure again:** a reaped-then-readopted `target.ID` whose stale key survives makes the re-arm a no-op, and the tab is wedged with no record. A dialog open **at** reap time simply dies with the tab, which is correct — there is no tab left to unwedge. Edit site 15 now names the reaper. |
| `metadata.go::BrowserBuiltinMetadata` | **modifies** | Eleven metadata-only instances, nil `*BrowserManager`. Feeds `pkg/gateway/gateway.go::buildKnownBuiltinToolNames` → the coverage universe. **Six additions required** (all six names, including `browser_upload_file` — see FR-029 on the difference between *seeded* and *registered*). |
| `register.go::RegisterTools` | **modifies** | Five `RegisterReplacing` calls now, six once #659 lands. `RegisterReplacing`, not `Register` — the hot-reload rationale in its own doc applies identically. |
| `register.go` `EvaluateTool{executeEnabled: …}` | **read; the pattern must NOT be copied — and revision 6 changes the flag's VALUE, not the pattern (FR-042)** | `browser_evaluate` is registered unconditionally and **runtime-gated** by `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled`, independent of tool policy. The gate is at `pkg/tools/browser/tools.go::EvaluateTool.Execute` (`:869-872`, *"This is the SOLE live gate"*), fed at construction from `config.Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:230-235`). **None of the six may replicate this shape** — a tool that is registered-but-inert in a way the policy surface cannot see is exactly the trap; see §5 (grill m1). **What revision 6 changes, and it is only this:** the flag was **unseeded**, so a fresh install was off while Jim's seed (`pkg/coreagent/core.go:1058`) and the global seed (`pkg/config/defaults.go:282`) both said `allow` — an operator reading Agents → Tools saw a capability that was shut. **ADR D1.9b ruling 2 seeds it `true` (FR-042)**, so the two surfaces now agree and the tool is live. **The structural criticism stands unchanged:** the disabling control is still a different key in a different section from the policy an operator inspects, there is still **no SPA control for it** (verified: zero occurrences of `browser_evaluate_enabled` under `src/`), and agents cannot write it (`pkg/sysagent/tools/config.go:322` blocks the whole `sandbox` namespace). Seeding it true removes the *surprise*, not the *split*. §12 A-19. |
| `core.go::allStaticToolNames` (browser block) | **modifies** | Six additions. **Mandatory**: `core.go::validateOverrideKeys` **panics** on an override key absent from this list. |
| `core.go::denyAllThenOverride` | **reuses** | Starts every name at `deny`, then applies overrides. **This is why Mia and Ava need no per-agent edit** — their deny is automatic once the six names are in `allStaticToolNames`. |
| `core.go::tightenGlobalCeiling` (used by `IDWorker`) | **inherits** | Returns a **sparse** map — everything absent inherits the **global** ceiling. So `IDWorker`'s posture for the six is whatever `defaults.go` says, with no Worker-specific edit. Same route gives Worker `serve_web: allow` today (FR-030). |
| Explorer / Researcher browser grants (`core.go` `case IDExplorer:` / `case IDResearcher:`) | **modifies** | **Two seeded agents the ADR's D2.9 table originally omitted.** **Each grants exactly TEN browser tools**, not eleven: `browser_navigate`, `_click`, `_type`, `_screenshot`, `_get_text`, `_wait`, `_list_tabs`, `_switch_tab`, `_close_tab`, `_open_tab`. `browser_evaluate` is **deliberately excluded**, and Explorer's own inline comment says so: *"(NOT browser_evaluate)"*. Ray's block carries the same carve-out with the reason spelled out: *"(NOT browser_evaluate — arbitrary JS)"*. That carve-out — ten allow, one deliberate deny — is the precedent §11's policy table reasons from (grill M11). |
| Ray browser grant (`core.go` `case IDRay:`) | **modifies** | Also **ten**, same carve-out, same inline reason. |
| Jim browser grant (`core.go` `case IDJim:`) | **modifies** | **Eleven** — the only agent granted `browser_evaluate` (`core.go:1058`, inline comment: *"operator-approved for Jim and stays runtime-gated by `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` regardless of policy"*), and the only agent granted `serve_web: allow` (FR-030's whole problem). **⚠️ "Operator-approved" was true of a per-agent browser, and it has been RE-approved against the shared one (ADR D1.9b ruling 2, 2026-09-01).** The original approval was given when a browser was **per agent**. Under **D1.10** the browser is per workspace and carries the operator's live logins for **every site the workspace has visited**, so Jim's arbitrary JS runs against those sessions — the ADR's §6 left that open with *"The grant predates the sharing model and has not been re-examined against it."* **It has now been examined and the ruling is to keep the grant AND make it live** (`sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` seeded `true`, **FR-042**). **So this is no longer a latent exposure: on a fresh install Jim can execute arbitrary JavaScript against the workspace's signed-in browser.** That is the widening, stated once, in the release notes (US-20/AC5), and not re-argued elsewhere in this document. §11(c) and §12 A-4 still reason from the carve-out — which is now **ten allow / one allow-and-live**, not ten allow / one inert — and both arguments survive because they turn on a property of the five new verbs, not on Jim's grant. §12 A-19; §15 item 8 CLOSED. |
| `validate.go::ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` | **no change** | **OR-based per (agent, tool)**: a **global** `sandbox.tool_policies` entry covers **every** agent. A single edit to `defaults.go` therefore closes the boot-abort risk for all agents at once; the per-agent edits are about *posture*, not coverage. |
| Boot / reload / write enforcement | **no change** | Boot abort `gateway.go` (`ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` call site); hot-reload abort; REST 400 in `rest.go`. |
| `manifest.go::ToolManifestTier` | **no change** | **Tier 3 is the residual, not a list** — it returns `ManifestLazy` for any name absent from `infraManifestToolNames` and `fullManifestToolNames`. The six become Tier 3 with **zero production-code edits**. This is also why the existing tier test cannot detect Tier-3 drift — FR-036. |
| `manifest.go::ToolManifestVisibility` / `::previewedLazyToolNames` | **no change — final** | 7 previewed names, `serve_web` among them. **A-3 is RULED Tier 3 (ADR D1.9b ruling 3)**, so all six new tools resolve as the residual and this file is not edited at all. The previewed set stays at seven and ADR-071 is not amended. |
| `manifest.go::previewAllLazy` / `::SetPreviewAllLazy` | **pattern source** | The ADR-071 §4.3.1b time-boxed revert switch: an `atomic.Bool` read inside the single chokepoint, set from live config every turn, no restart. **FR-034's revert switch copies this shape exactly.** |
| `manifest_test.go:667-681` `tier3SearchOnlyToolNames` | **modifies** | Hand-maintained test fixture transcribing ADR-071 §4.1. **62 names, not 63** — `write_agent_metadata` retired. Pinned by hard literals in `TestVisibility_TierArithmetic` (`manifest_test.go:694-744`: `17`, `7`, `62`, `1`, union `87`). Line numbers retained: this is a fixture, and it is the thing being edited. **Re-verified at revision 3 (round-2 m1, REJECTED):** the round-2 grill reported this range as wrong, claiming the literal "runs to ~688". It does not. `var tier3SearchOnlyToolNames = []string{` is at **667** and its closing `}` is at **681**; line 682 is blank and **683-693 are the doc comment of the *next* test**, `TestVisibility_TierArithmetic`, which opens at 694. `667-681` was correct and stands. The only correction taken is the arithmetic test's end bound: its closing `}` is at **744**, not 745. |
| `pkg/gateway/gateway.go::buildKnownBuiltinToolNames` | **reads (new test consumer)** | The registered builtin catalog: general metadata ∪ browser metadata ∪ sysagent tools ∪ the four ADR-052 planning names. FR-036's new test asserts the four-tier partition equals this set. |
| `pkg/tools/web_serve.go::ToolNameWebServe` | **reads** | **`= "serve_web"`.** Corroborated by `previewedLazyToolNames` containing `"serve_web"`. The ADR has since corrected itself (D2.5's inline note); root `CLAUDE.md` still says `web_serve` and is a separate doc fix. |
| `pkg/tools/resolvepath.go::ResolvePath` / `::FSOp` | **reuses** | ADR-046 chokepoint. Verified `FSOp` semantics from the type's own doc: `FSOpRead/List/Send` allowed anywhere except the secret carve-out; **`FSOpWrite/Serve` work-dir-or-mount (`policy.AllowedRoots`) only**. See Stream B — FR-012 selects `FSOpWrite`. |
| `pkg/tools/resolvepath.go::FSOpSend` | **read, REJECTED for this use** | Its own doc: *"It carries NO additional path restriction beyond the open-read rule — the operator explicitly rejected a path-based 'publish' gate for send_file … so the real gate is tool policy."* Revision 1 cited the first half and then built the gate the second half records as rejected (grill M9). Resolved in FR-012. |
| `pkg/config/security.go::Config.SensitiveDataReplacer` | **NOW WIRED IN (FR-027)** | Revision 1 evaluated and declined it. The operator ruling makes it mandatory as defence in depth. See §2.3. |
| `pkg/audit::Emit` | **new consumer** | `Emit(ctx, logger, event, sev, fields)` — the existing structured-audit seam (`pkg/audit/events.go::Emit`), already used by `pkg/tools/memory.go` and others via an injected `*audit.Logger`. **`pkg/tools/browser` does not import `pkg/audit` today** (`capture_session.go`'s header says so explicitly), so FR-028/FR-031 add the first import and a `SetAuditLogger`-style injection mirroring `pkg/tools/library_tool.go::LibraryReadTool.SetAuditLogger`. |
| `src/lib/toolVisibility.ts::shouldRenderToolCall` (`:89`) | **read, must stay unchanged** | Takes `verboseChatEnabled` and short-circuits to `true`. **Verified: zero occurrences of the substring `browser` in the file** — so every browser tool call, including a snapshot, renders in the chat thread today **when the call belongs to the turn the thread is showing**. FR-028's chat half depends on that and §5 forbids changing it. **It is not the whole story, and revision 3 read it as if it were — see the next row.** |
| `src/lib/toolVisibility.ts::shouldRenderSubagentSpan` (`:230-235`) | **read — this is the gate revision 3 missed (consolidated grill M5)** | `return verboseChatEnabled`. Its input's store default is **`false`** (`src/store/chatPreferences.ts:19`). **Verified at three call sites** — `src/components/chat/ChatScreen.tsx:593`, `:941`, `:1480` all filter a message's spans through it. A **delegated** sub-turn's tool calls render only inside its SubagentBlock span, so with default settings **a delegated `browser_snapshot` — field values included — renders nowhere in the parent chat thread** while its full output reaches the 90-day transcript. `shouldRenderToolCall` returning `true` for the name is irrelevant: the span never renders, so the badge inside it is never reached. **FR-039** states this and tests it. |
| `src/components/chat/ActivityPanel.tsx` (`:161-177`) + `src/hooks/useRunningActivity.ts:549` | **read — narrows an ADR claim, on evidence** | ADR D2.11 says *"A `browser_snapshot` never appears there"*. **True for a parent-turn snapshot** (the panel aggregates only subagent spans, background `bash` and judge verdicts — none of which a top-level tool call is). **False for a delegated one:** `useRunningActivity` carries `steps: span.steps` for **native** sub-agents (`:549`), `ActivityPanel` renders each `step.kind === 'tool'` as a `ToolCallBadge surface="panel"` (`:171`), and `shouldRenderToolCallInPanel` (`toolVisibility.ts:254-259`) returns `true` for everything except `ToolSearch` and `load_tool`. **So a delegated snapshot IS reachable in the panel — under three limits that stop it being a default surface:** the operator must open the panel *and* expand that span; the span must still be running or within `RECENTLY_FINISHED_CAP = 8` (`useRunningActivity.ts:148`); and an **external-CLI (3p) sub-agent carries no steps at all** (`:549`, *"empty for 3p"*), so nothing of its browsing is visible anywhere. Recorded precisely because asserting the ADR's absolute would have been a §8-pattern error in this document. |
| `src/hooks/useRunningActivity.ts` | **read — the ADR's claim fails for a PARENT-turn snapshot** | Its own header: the panel aggregates *"subagent delegation spans … and background `bash` sessions"* (plus judge verdicts), capped at `RECENTLY_FINISHED_CAP = 8` (`:148`). A **top-level** tool call is none of those, so ADR D2.11's original "the snapshot must be reachable in the ActivityPanel" is **false for the parent case** — see FR-028 (round-1 grill C2). **Revision 4 correction:** it does **not** follow that a delegated snapshot is unreachable there, which is what revision 3 and the ADR both imply. The row above has the evidence. |
| `src/components/settings/AuditLogViewer.tsx` | **read — this is the audit half's operator surface** | **New in revision 3 (round-2 M6).** Settings → Security → Audit Log. Fetches `GET /api/v1/audit-log` (`pkg/gateway/rest_settings.go::HandleAuditLog`, which streams raw JSONL lines from `$OMNIPUS_HOME/system/audit.jsonl`, last 100, reverse-chronological). **It renders every entry by default** — `eventFilter` starts at `'all'` and an unrecognised event name falls through to `BADGE_FALLBACK` styling rather than being dropped — so a `browser_snapshot` audit record *is* visible to an operator without a code change. **Two real limits, both stated in FR-028 rather than glossed:** (i) its `EVENT_TYPE_OPTIONS` dropdown is a **hardcoded 10-name list** (`tool_call`, `exec`, `file_op`, `llm_call`, `policy_eval`, `rate_limit`, `ssrf`, `startup`, `shutdown`, `security_setting_change`), so an operator can see the new events but cannot *filter to* them; (ii) see the next row. |
| `contracts/components/schemas/AuditEntry.yaml` → `src/lib/api/generated/schemas.ts::AuditEntry` | **read, CONSTRAINS the event name** | **The contract pins `event` to `pattern: '^[a-z_]+$'`** — lowercase letters and underscores, **no dots**. `src/lib/api.ts::fetchAuditLog` passes `AuditLogResponseSchema` to `request()`, whose `performRequest` **throws `ApiSchemaError` on `safeParse` failure** rather than dropping the row — and `entries` is `z.array(AuditEntry)`, so **one** non-conforming entry blanks the entire viewer. **Therefore FR-028's and FR-031's event names are `browser_snapshot` and `browser_upload_file` — underscore form, never `browser.snapshot`.** *(Pre-existing defect, **filed as [#667](https://github.com/elicify-ai/omnipus/issues/667) (OPEN)**, recorded not fixed: `audit.EventChannelPairing = "channel.pairing"` is emitted at `pkg/agent/loop.go:4357` and `audit.EventCliValidate = "cli.validate"` at `pkg/gateway/rest_clivalidate.go:370`, both into the same `audit.jsonl`. On any install where a channel has been paired or a CLI validated, the Audit Log viewer already throws. That is not D2's to fix — see §12 B-6 — but D2 must not add a third, **and FR-028 must not rest its only reading surface on a viewer that is blank on most real installs**, which is what revision 3 did.)* |

### 2.2 CDP / chromedp primitives — all present, no new dependency

`go.mod` pins `chromedp v0.15.1` and `cdproto v0.0.0-20260321001828-e3e3800016bc`. Every primitive D2 needs already ships:

| Need | Primitive | Location |
|---|---|---|
| Role + name query | `accessibility.QueryAXTree()` with `WithRole` / `WithAccessibleName` | `cdproto/accessibility/accessibility.go::QueryAXTree`, `::QueryAXTreeParams.WithRole`, `::WithAccessibleName` |
| Page structure | `accessibility.GetFullAXTree()` | `cdproto/accessibility/accessibility.go::GetFullAXTree` |
| AX → DOM bridge | `accessibility.Node.BackendDOMNodeID` | `cdproto/accessibility/types.go:465` |
| AX ignored flag | `accessibility.Node.Ignored` | `cdproto/accessibility/types.go:455` |
| AX field value | `accessibility.Node.Value` | `cdproto/accessibility/types.go:461` — **note the ADR cites `:206`, which is `AXPropertySource.Value`, a different struct. Minor ADR erratum, recorded so it is not re-derived.** |
| Batched in-page probe | `runtime.Evaluate` via `chromedp.Evaluate` | the gate's fast path (FR-007) |
| Stability (fallback) | `dom.GetBoxModel()` / `dom.GetContentQuads()` | `cdproto/dom/dom.go::GetBoxModel`, `::GetContentQuads` |
| Hit-test (rejected alt.) | `dom.GetNodeForLocation(x, y)` | `cdproto/dom/dom.go::GetNodeForLocation` — see FR-007's rejected-alternative note |
| Scroll-into-view | `dom.ScrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` | `cdproto/dom/dom.go::ScrollIntoViewIfNeeded` |
| File attach | `chromedp.SetUploadFiles(sel, files, opts…)` | `chromedp/query.go::SetUploadFiles` |
| Key events | `chromedp.KeyEvent` / `KeyEventNode` | `chromedp/input.go::KeyEvent`, `::KeyEventNode` |
| Dialog dismiss | `page.HandleJavaScriptDialog(accept)` + `.WithPromptText` | `cdproto/page/page.go::HandleJavaScriptDialog`, `::HandleJavaScriptDialogParams.WithPromptText` |
| Dialog detect | `page.EventJavascriptDialogOpening` | `cdproto/page/events.go::EventJavascriptDialogOpening` |
| Select | `chromedp.SetValue` (+ a dispatched `change`) | `chromedp/query.go::SetValue` |

**The wedge, from CDP's own contract.** `EventJavascriptDialogOpening.HasBrowserHandler` documents it verbatim: *"When browser has no dialog handler for given target, calling alert while Page domain is engaged will stall the page execution. Execution can be resumed via calling Page.handleJavaScriptDialog."* That sentence — not a design intuition — is why acceptance is "the tab still answers CDP", never "the dialog was dismissed".

**Precedent for synthetic key input.** The live-view human path already dispatches real key events (`pkg/tools/browser/live.go`, `input.DispatchKeyEvent`). `browser_press_key` is the agent-facing counterpart, not a new mechanism.

### 2.2a The `Page`-domain question is RESOLVED, not a spike (grill M6, closes A-5)

Revision 1 ordered a spike to find out whether chromedp enables the `Page` domain per target. **It does, and the source says so.** `chromedp@v0.15.1/chromedp.go::Context.attachTarget` builds an action list for every non-worker target and executes `page.Enable()` in it, alongside `log.Enable()`, `network.Enable()`, `inspector.Enable()`, `dom.Enable()`, `css.Enable()`, `target.SetDiscoverTargets(true)`, `target.SetAutoAttach(...)` and `page.SetLifecycleEventsEnabled(true)`. Every tab in `pkg/tools/browser` reaches that path — `createFirstTab` and `OpenTab` via `chromedp.NewContext` + `chromedp.Run`, adopted tabs via `chromedp.NewContext(..., chromedp.WithTargetID(...))` + `chromedp.Run`.

**Three consequences, all load-bearing:**

1. **No per-tab `Page.enable` round trip is needed**, so M6's "no" branch — which would have required an enable inside `installTargetListenerLocked`, a function whose doc states it must be called with `m.mu` held precisely because `ListenTarget` is a lock-free append and never a round trip — **does not arise**. The §5 non-behavior forbidding CDP under `m.mu` is preserved for free.
2. **The listener install stays a lock-free append**, so FR-014's per-tab installer inherits `installTargetListenerLocked`'s discipline exactly.
3. **chromedp does NOT handle dialogs itself.** `chromedp@v0.15.1/target.go`'s page-event switch lists `*page.EventJavascriptDialogOpening` and `*page.EventJavascriptDialogClosed` among its **explicitly ignored** events. So `HasBrowserHandler` is false and the page stalls exactly as CDP documents — this is the mechanism of the wedge, confirmed rather than assumed.

**This is a dependency behaviour, so it must be pinned, not merely noted.** §10 order 0 replaces the spike with `TestChromedpEnablesPageDomainPerTarget` — a real assertion (a fresh tab, an `alert()`, and the assertion that a `ListenTarget` callback observes `EventJavascriptDialogOpening` with no explicit `page.Enable` anywhere in our code) that turns red on a chromedp bump that changes the bring-up list.

### 2.3 The snapshot's disclosure posture — what was verified, and what the operator ruled

**What was verified (unchanged from revision 1, and the ADR now agrees).**

1. **There is no redaction path in this package to inherit.** `RegisterSensitiveValues` / `SensitiveDataReplacer` appear in `pkg/config`, `pkg/tools/list_jobs_row.go`, `pkg/gitevidence`, `pkg/audit/secretscan.go`, `pkg/agent/session_messaging_wire.go` and `pkg/gateway`. **Zero occurrences anywhere in `pkg/tools/browser/`.** `browser_get_text`'s entire output treatment is `capGetText`.
2. **The replacer would not, on its own, do the job the earlier ADR text claimed.** Verified in `pkg/config/security.go::buildAndPopulateSensitiveCache`: it builds a `strings.Replacer` over reflection-walked `SecureString` config fields plus runtime-registered plaintexts, mapping each to `[FILTERED]`, and **skips any value of length ≤ 3**. An account identifier or an arbitrary form value is not a registered secret and passes through untouched.
3. **The risk is a strict widening, not an inheritance.** `browser_get_text` calls `chromedp.Text` — *innerText*, rendered text only. An `<input>`'s value is not innerText. `accessibility.Node.Value` **is** the field's computed value. A snapshot that emits `Value` therefore exposes what a user typed where the existing text tool structurally could not.

**What the operator ruled (ADR D2.11, 2026-08-31, Daniel Piatkowski).**

> **The snapshot returns field values by default.** Omit-by-default with an `include_values` opt-in was **offered and declined**. The rationale: an agent cannot verify a form is correctly filled before submitting it — one of the main things the panel is for — without seeing what is in the fields.

**The accepted risk, stated plainly rather than softened.** A `browser_snapshot` of a signed-in page can carry a **card number, a partially typed password, or an account identifier** into the model's context, into the conversation the operator reads, and into the stored transcript at `sessions/<id>/<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl` — which has a **90-day default retention**. Nothing in this design prevents that. It is accepted because the alternative removes the capability's main purpose, and it is recorded here so it is not rediscovered as a surprise.

**The two mitigations the ruling makes non-optional** are specced as first-class requirements, each with a BDD scenario, a TDD entry and a dataset row:

- **FR-027 — the sensitive-value replacer, wired in as defence in depth.** It does not cover form values and this spec does not pretend it does. It closes the *credential-plaintext* case (a provider key pasted into a form field, a registered secret echoed in an accessible name) at essentially no cost.
- **FR-028 — the capture is visible in two places, and the two answer different questions.** **The ADR's stated mechanism is wrong and the ADR sentence needs correcting.** It says the snapshot "must be reachable in the ActivityPanel / verbose-chat surfaces like any other tool call". Verified against `src/hooks/useRunningActivity.ts`: the panel aggregates subagent delegation spans, background `bash` sessions and judge verdicts — capped at 8 recently-finished — and has **no path that renders an arbitrary tool call**. "Reachable in the ActivityPanel" is false today for *every* browser tool, not just the snapshot.

  **Revision 3 says plainly which half does what (round-2 M6).** Revision 2 called the audit half "operator-inspectable" and named no surface, which left the entire mitigation resting on the chat render — and the chat render *is* the disclosure. Corrected:

  - **(a) The chat thread answers "**what** was captured" — for a snapshot the agent on screen took, and only for that one.** The full result renders inline, by default, for every viewer of the conversation, because `src/lib/toolVisibility.ts` contains zero `browser` references (pinned by S-43). This is the only surface that shows the values, and it shows them *because* they are the tool's output. It is a control in the sense that nothing is hidden; it is **not** a control in the sense of limiting exposure.

    > **⚠️ It does not hold for delegated work, and under the sharing ruling that is now a first-class population — not an edge case (consolidated grill M5; ADR D2.11 and §6 both carry it).** A delegated sub-turn's tool calls render **only** inside its SubagentBlock span, and `shouldRenderSubagentSpan` (`src/lib/toolVisibility.ts:230-235`) returns `verboseChatEnabled`, whose store default is **`false`** (`src/store/chatPreferences.ts:19`) — filtered at `ChatScreen.tsx:593`, `:941` and `:1480`. **So with default settings a delegated `browser_snapshot`, field values included, reaches the 90-day transcript and renders nowhere in the parent thread.** Revision 3 asserted this bullet unconditionally and pinned it with a test (`shouldRenderToolCall(name, …, false) === true`) that is true of the parent thread and **silent on the span gate** — a green that could not have seen the failure. **FR-039 replaces the claim with the two-population statement and tests both halves.**
    >
    > **The ActivityPanel is a partial fallback for the delegated case — and only that case.** Verified rather than assumed (§2.1): a native sub-agent's span carries its tool calls as `steps` (`useRunningActivity.ts:549`), `ActivityPanel.tsx:161-177` renders each as a `ToolCallBadge surface="panel"`, and `shouldRenderToolCallInPanel` (`toolVisibility.ts:254-259`) admits everything but `ToolSearch`/`load_tool`. **Three limits keep it from being a default surface:** the operator must open the panel and expand the span; the span must be running or inside `RECENTLY_FINISHED_CAP = 8` (`:148`); and an **external-CLI sub-agent carries no steps at all**, so nothing of its browsing is visible on any surface. **This narrows ADR D2.11's "a `browser_snapshot` never appears there", which is right for a parent-turn snapshot and wrong for a delegated one** — recorded in §0c AC-2 rather than asserted at the ADR.
  - **(b) The audit event answers "**that** a capture happened, of what shape, from where."** Page origin, node count, output bytes, whether any node emitted a `Value`, whether the output was truncated — and **never the values themselves**, because an audit log that copies the card number has moved the disclosure rather than recorded it. It is the **durable** half: the chat thread is bounded by the session's retention and by whoever reads that conversation; the audit record survives. **It is also the only half that covers delegated work**, since it is emitted by the tool and does not care which thread the call belongs to.

    > **Where it is read — corrected in revision 4, because revision 3 named a surface it had itself documented as broken (consolidated grill M6).** Revision 3 designated Settings → Security → **Audit Log** as *the* operator surface, and, four sections later, recorded that the same viewer **already throws on any install where a channel has been paired or a CLI validated** (§12 B-6). Both statements were true and could not both be relied on. **A mitigation whose only reading surface is blank on most real installs is not a mitigation.** The defect is filed as **[#667](https://github.com/elicify-ai/omnipus/issues/667)** (OPEN — *"Audit Log screen blanks entirely when history contains `channel.pairing` or `cli.validate`"*). Revision 4 therefore names **two** surfaces and says which works today:
    >
    > | Surface | Works today? | For whom |
    > |---|---|---|
    > | `GET /api/v1/audit-log`, and `$OMNIPUS_HOME/system/audit.jsonl` directly | **Yes, unconditionally.** #667 is a **client-side** validation failure — `src/lib/api.ts::performRequest` throws `ApiSchemaError` over `z.array(AuditEntry)`. The endpoint and the file are unaffected by a dotted event name | An on-call operator, and any script. **Not** a surface for a non-engineer, and this spec does not pretend otherwise |
    > | Settings → Security → **Audit Log** (`src/components/settings/AuditLogViewer.tsx`) | **Conditional on #667.** Blank on any install carrying a dotted event name; correct on a clean one | The founder, and anyone who is not going to `grep` a JSONL file |
    >
    > **FR-028 is written against the first and names the second as conditional.** #667 is **not** a blocking prerequisite the way #659 is for FR-029 — D2 can ship a correct, readable audit record without it, and holding this work behind another team's viewer fix would be disproportionate. But **the operator-facing half of the ruled mitigation is not delivered until #667 lands**, and that is stated here rather than left to be discovered. §15 item 9.

  **Two further limits on the viewer, stated rather than glossed** (both verified — see §2.1's rows): its event-type dropdown is a hardcoded 10-name list, so the new events are **visible but not filterable**; and the `AuditEntry` contract pins `event` to `^[a-z_]+$`, so **the names must be `browser_snapshot` / `browser_upload_file`, not dotted** — a dotted name makes `AuditLogResponseSchema.safeParse` fail and `performRequest` throw, blanking the whole viewer. That third limit is exactly #667's mechanism, which is why D2's own two names are asserted against the regex (S-42) rather than merely intended.

  **Recommended ADR amendments (the ADR owner's, not this spec's) — the consolidated ADR has taken some of them.** *Taken:* D2.11 no longer names the ActivityPanel as the durable surface, and now cites `types.go:461`. *Outstanding:* (i) D2.11's *"a `browser_snapshot` never appears there"* is right for a parent-turn snapshot and **wrong for a delegated one** (§0c AC-2, evidence in §2.1); and (ii) the ADR needs a **§9.5** for this document, because §9 currently lists the D1 spec, #509, ADR-048 and root `CLAUDE.md` and **not this spec**, so every conflict recorded in §0c is invisible to an ADR reader.

### 2.4 Impact assessment

| Symbol modified | Risk | Direct dependents (d=1) | Indirect (d=2) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `resolveActionSelector` / `resolvePseudoOnlySelector` | **HIGH** | click, type, get_text, wait + all six new tools | `text_selector_test.go`, `text_selector_e2e_test.go` (2 files, ~59 KB of assertions). **Cost is deliberately UNBUDGETED in v1 and that is now recorded, not omitted (round-2 M10, §12 B-5).** The AX branch issues `QueryAXTree` + `DOM.setAttributeValue` + a cleanup `removeAttribute` per call; the snapshot path issues `GetFullAXTree`, which B-1 already concedes is unbounded. FR-007 budgets the *gate* — the narrower half — and nothing budgets this, the wider one. Revision 2 simply did not mention it. |
| Click/type pre-action wait | **CRITICAL** | Every agent click and keystroke on every page | `execute_e2e_test.go`, `text_selector_e2e_test.go`, `tab_adoption_e2e_test.go`. **Mitigated by FR-034's revert switch** — revision 1 shipped this unconditionally with no way back, which the grill (M13) correctly called out against the ADR-071 `previewAllLazy` precedent. |
| `allStaticToolNames` | **CRITICAL** | `validateOverrideKeys` **panics** on drift; `TestBuildKnownBuiltinToolNames_MatchesCoreagentStaticToolCatalog` | Every seeded agent's policy map; boot |
| `defaults.go` global `tool_policies` | **CRITICAL** | `ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` for **every** agent (OR-semantics) | Boot abort; reload abort; REST 400 |
| `BrowserBuiltinMetadata` | **HIGH** | `buildKnownBuiltinToolNames`; `GET /api/v1/tools` catalog | Coverage universe — a tool in metadata but absent from `allStaticToolNames` fails the sync test |
| `tier3SearchOnlyToolNames` + tier arithmetic literals | **MEDIUM** | `TestVisibility_TierArithmetic`, `TestVisibility_SearchOnlyToolsRemainInSearchIndex` | Build fails until updated — by design (ADR-071 FR-034), **but only for Tier 1/2; FR-036 adds the assertion that makes it true for Tier 3 too** |
| Per-tab dialog listener (new, alongside `installTargetListenerLocked`) | **HIGH** | Every tab's event plumbing; ADR-041 tab adoption | `tabs_test.go`, `tab_adoption_e2e_test.go`, `navigate_stranded_tab_test.go` |
| `sessionEntry` dialog-state teardown | **MEDIUM** | Tab close / ctx recreation must evict the per-tab dialog-listener key | FR-014's re-arm rule (S-33) |
| `sessionEntry.lastActivation` (new per-tab field, round-2 M8) | **MEDIUM** | The FR-013 suspected-dialog fallback reads it; every action tool writes it | Concurrency: written under `m.mu` by the tool *after* its CDP call returns, never from `handleTargetEvent` (whose doc forbids blocking). **Cleared, not preserved, on ctx recreation** — a recreated tab has no completed command of its own. Contract and its consequence are in Stream C gap 3 |
| `ValidateURL` | LOW | `browser_navigate`, `browser_open_tab` | `blocked_schemes_test.go` (asserts the current message) |
| Ray / Explorer / Researcher `serve_web` grant (FR-030) | **MEDIUM** | Three agents gain a tool that writes files and serves them | `TestCoreAgentSeed_*`; a real posture change, argued in §11 |

---

## 3. Implementation Streams (fan-out for parallel agents)

Six streams. **Stream A is the critical path** — it defines the resolution and actionability interfaces every other stream codes against. **Stream E (policy + tier) is independently mandatory and must land in the SAME commit series as any stream that registers a tool**, because a registered tool with no seeded policy aborts boot.

### Shared interface contract (Stream A's first commit — everyone codes against this)

```go
// pkg/tools/browser/target.go (new) — placement: same package as the existing
// text_selector.go seam. NOT a new package: resolveActionSelector, wrapTextMatch,
// textMarkerAttr and scrubMarkerFromError are all unexported and stay that way.

// Locator is the closed set of ways an agent may name an element. EXACTLY ONE
// of {CSS, Text, Role+Name} may be populated. Supplying two is an ERROR that
// names both populated fields — never a silent precedence rule. (Revision 1
// said "honoured in that documented order", which is a different and weaker
// contract than "exactly one"; grill m3.)
type Locator struct {
    Selector string // CSS, optionally with a trailing :has-text()/:text-is() pseudo
    Text     string // visible-text substring (NOT valid on browser_type or browser_press_key)
    Role     string // ARIA/computed role, e.g. "button", "combobox", "link"
    Name     string // computed accessible name, e.g. "Submit"
    Index    *int   // 0-based disambiguator on multi-match; nil = "must be unique".
                    // A pointer, not (int, bool): the two-field form permitted an
                    // Index:3/HasIndex:false state nothing validated (grill m4).
}

// ErrLocatorConflict is returned when more than one locator kind is populated,
// or when a tool receives a locator kind it does not accept. It names the
// offending fields; it never picks a winner.
type ErrLocatorConflict struct{ Fields []string; Tool string }

// resolveTarget is the SINGLE seam. It supersedes resolveActionSelector and
// resolvePseudoOnlySelector as the entry point; both survive as internal
// branches so the existing CSS/text tests keep exercising the same code.
// Returns a CSS string the caller's existing chromedp ByQuery action uses
// unchanged — for the Role+Name branch this is the SAME data-omnipus-tsel
// marker selector the text branch already produces, set via
// DOM.setAttributeValue on the AX node's BackendDOMNodeID. cleanup MUST be
// deferred immediately; it is always safe to call, including on the error path.
func resolveTarget(tabCtx context.Context, toolName string, loc Locator, timeout time.Duration) (target string, cleanup func(), err error)

// ActionCondition is the CLOSED set the actionability gate reports on failure.
// Criterion 7 of the ADR requires the error to name WHICH condition was unmet;
// a closed set is what makes that testable rather than prose.
type ActionCondition string

const (
    CondVisible     ActionCondition = "visible"      // rendered, non-zero box
    CondStable      ActionCondition = "stable"       // two box reads one animation frame apart are identical
    CondEnabled     ActionCondition = "enabled"      // not [disabled], not aria-disabled=true
    CondHitTestable ActionCondition = "hit-testable" // the point-hit node is this node or a descendant
)

// ErrNotActionable is the ONLY error type the gate returns on timeout. Failed
// names the FIRST condition that never became true within the budget — first,
// not last, because the conditions are evaluated in the order above and a
// later one is meaningless while an earlier one is false.
type ErrNotActionable struct {
    Failed  ActionCondition
    Display string // the user-facing locator (text_selector.go::displayLocator)
    Detail  string // e.g. the occluding element's tag+id for CondHitTestable
}
func (e *ErrNotActionable) Error() string // "browser_click: element %q is not actionable: %s (%s)"

// waitActionable runs the four-condition gate. Called by every ACTION tool
// (click, type, select_option, press_key-with-target, hover, upload_file);
// NEVER by a read-only tool (get_text, screenshot, wait, snapshot) and NEVER
// by browser_handle_dialog (FR-035 — it is a recovery verb).
func waitActionable(tabCtx context.Context, toolName, target, display string, timeout time.Duration) error

// THE WRITE LEASE IS NOT DEFINED HERE. It belongs to the D1 spec.
//
// Operator ruling, 2026-08-31: both specs had independently specced a lease
// over the same seven call sites with incompatible signatures. Had both
// landed, the action tools would have taken two unrelated mutexes and mutual
// exclusion would have been LOST for whichever tool took only one — the
```
