- *On its own this scenario is worthless as evidence and is written that way deliberately: it would pass identically on a build with the gate deleted and on a build with no tool policy at all. **S-67 is what gives it meaning.***

**S-67: the same install still refuses the agents policy denies (Security) — US-20/AC2, FR-042** *(new in revision 6; **rewritten in revision 7 — round-4 M2**, because revision 6 asserted a dispatch outcome against a mechanism that observes a policy value, in a stream that owns neither package)*

> **What was wrong with it, stated because the shape recurs.** Revision 6's Then-clauses were *"refused **before `EvaluateTool.Execute` runs**"* and *"the **refusal text** is a policy denial"*, while the test named at order 4a drove `resolveEffectivePolicyWith` — which returns a `config.ToolPolicy` **value** (`pkg/tools/compositor.go:163-167`). A value cannot tell you whether a function ran, and it has no text. **And the deny path produces no refusal text at all in the ordinary case:** `FilterToolsByPolicy` **`continue`s past a deny verdict** (`pkg/tools/compositor.go:436-438`), so the tool is simply **absent from the set sent to the model** — nothing is refused because nothing is offered. A refusal string exists only when a model names a filtered-out tool anyway, and it is produced in a **third** package (`pkg/agent/loop.go:2743` and `:2749`, `name + " — denied by this agent's policy"`). Revision 6 put all of it in Stream E1, which owns `pkg/config` and `pkg/coreagent` and neither of the other two.

**The scenario, restated as three assertions each observable where it is made:**
- **(a) — `pkg/config` + `pkg/coreagent` (Stream E1's own packages).** **Given** the install with `BrowserEvaluateEnabled` seeded `true`, **When** Mia's and Ava's policy for `browser_evaluate` is resolved through the real compositor (`resolveEffectivePolicyWith`, as order 4 already does for uploads), **Then** it is **`ToolPolicyDeny`** for both — via `denyAllThenOverride` plus most-restrictive-wins, the same mechanism §12 A-16 records for `browser_upload_file`. *A policy value is what this layer can observe, and it is all this assertion claims.*
- **(b) — `pkg/tools`.** **Given** the same resolved policy, **When** `FilterToolsByPolicy` runs for Mia and for Ava, **Then** `browser_evaluate` is **not in the returned tool set** and not in the returned policy map. *This is the observable consequence, and it is the one that can fail: it is what makes "seeded true" and "seeded true but policy ignored" different runs.*
- **(c) — `pkg/tools/browser`.** **Given** an `EvaluateTool` constructed with `executeEnabled: true`, **When** `Execute` runs, **Then** it does **not** return `"browser_evaluate: disabled — set sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled=true in config to enable"`. *This is the half that proves the enablement rather than the policy, and it is the only one that can see that string. Asserting it in the same test as (a) is what revision 6 tried to do across three packages at once.*
- *Together these are the pair's oracle: a gate that is on for everyone and a gate that does not exist are indistinguishable from S-66 alone; they differ at (b). **(a) alone is not enough** — a build that resolved `deny` correctly and then sent the tool anyway would pass (a) and fail (b), which is precisely the defect class ADR-037 is this project's cautionary tale for.*
- *One thing this scenario deliberately does **not** assert: that a denied Mia sees a **message**. She does not — the tool is absent, not refused. Writing "the refusal text is a policy denial" as an expectation would have made a test that can only pass by reaching into the load-tool path in `pkg/agent` for a string that the normal path never produces.*

**S-68: an operator's explicit `false` survives a config save — US-20/AC3-AC4, FR-042** *(new in revision 6; **expected RED until the field becomes a `*bool`**)*
- **Given** a `config.json` containing `"sandbox": {"browser_evaluate_enabled": false}` on an install whose `DefaultConfig()` seeds `true`
- **When** the config is loaded, **Then** the flag reads `false` — the explicit value beats the seed (`loadConfig` unmarshals over `DefaultConfig()`, `pkg/config/migration.go:19`)
- **And When** `SaveConfig` (`pkg/config/config.go:4388`) is then called for any unrelated reason, **Then** the written file **still contains** `"browser_evaluate_enabled": false`
- **And When** that written file is loaded again, **Then** the flag still reads `false`
- *Today the second step fails: the field is a plain `bool` with `omitempty` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:235`), so `json.MarshalIndent` (`config.go:4413`) omits a `false` and the next load re-applies the seeded `true`. **The bug is created by the seed, not exposed by it** — with no seed, `false` and absent meant the same thing. The fix is a `*bool`, matching `PathGuardAuditFailClosed` in the same struct (`sandbox.go:228`). This scenario must be RED before the fix and green after; landing it green on the first run means the fix went in without the scenario ever having had the chance to fail.*

**S-69: the decoy switch does not exist any more (Security) — US-21/AC1-AC3, FR-043** *(new in revision 7, ADR D1.9d)*
- **Given** the loaded config tree
- **When** `BrowserToolConfig` is walked by reflection and `env.GetFieldParams(&Config{})` is enumerated
- **Then** there is **no** field with the json tag `evaluate_enabled` and **no** field resolving to `OMNIPUS_TOOLS_BROWSER_EVALUATE_ENABLED`
- **And** the only `EvaluateEnabled` symbol reachable in `pkg/` is `Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled`
- *This scenario asserts an absence, which is the weakest shape of test there is — so it is written against the **reflection/env surface**, not against a `grep`, and it is red today. **What it deliberately does NOT claim:** that behaviour changed. Nothing read the field, so nothing behaves differently; a behavioural oracle here would be unfalsifiable, and saying so is better than inventing one.*

**S-70: the one remaining switch is the one an agent cannot flip (Security) — US-21/AC4, FR-043** *(new in revision 7)*
- **Given** an agent granted the `set_config` tool
- **When** it calls `set_config{key: "sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled", value: true}`
- **Then** the write is refused, through the `sandbox` subtree block (`pkg/sysagent/tools/config.go::blockedConfigKeys`)
- **And** the same call against the deleted `tools.browser.evaluate_enabled` is **not** the assertion this scenario makes, because that key no longer exists
- *This is the scenario that makes the deletion a net gain in coverage rather than a net loss. **Verified before writing it: `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` appears in no test in `pkg/` at all** — the two escalation tests that look like coverage (`config_blocklist_test.go:117`, `config_privilege_test.go:75`) name the dead field. Deleting the field without this scenario would leave the live control with zero tests and two green tests pointing at nothing.*

**S-71: an existing test goes red on purpose, once (Regression) — US-21/AC5, FR-043** *(new in revision 7)*
- **Given** `pkg/config/env_prefix_guard_test.go`'s `want` map, which lists `OMNIPUS_TOOLS_BROWSER_EVALUATE_ENABLED` at `:132`
- **When** the env tag is deleted from `BrowserToolConfig` and the map is **not** amended
- **Then** `TestBrowserToolConfig_EnvKeys_NoDoublePrefix` fails at `:151-155` with *"expected env tag … not found via env.GetFieldParams(&Config{}) — field renamed or removed?"*
- **And When** the map entry is removed in the same commit, **Then** it passes, and every other `OMNIPUS_TOOLS_BROWSER_*` tag still resolves to itself
- *Written as a scenario rather than a footnote because the red is **correct** and will otherwise be read as a break. The test anticipated exactly this deletion in its own failure message; the change is expected to satisfy it, not to silence it.*

**S-72: a human at the wheel does not stop a read of their tab (Security) — US-22/AC1 + AC4, FR-044** *(new in revision 7, round-4 M3)*
- **Given** the operator's shared tab, a human holding the live view (`IsControlled` true), and a signed-in page with a partially filled form
- **When** an agent calls `browser_snapshot` on that tab
- **Then** the snapshot returns, **with field values**, and is **not** `{"deferred": true}`
- **And** in the same fixture, `browser_click` on that tab **does** defer — so the assertion is about the exemption, not about a broken `IsControlled`
- **And** `browser_click` on a **different** tab in the same browser **also** defers, because `IsControlled` is browser-global rather than per-tab (`tools.go:69`, `:963`)
- *The second and third steps are what make this a test. Asserting only that the snapshot returns would pass on a build where the human-control gate does nothing at all.*

**S-73: an attended agent may press OK on a dialog the human is looking at (Security) — US-22/AC2-AC3, FR-044** *(new in revision 7, round-4 M3)*
- **Given** the operator's shared tab showing `confirm("Delete this account?")`, a human holding the live view, and a turn **without** `AutoDenyAsk`
- **When** the agent calls `browser_handle_dialog{accept:true}`
- **Then** the dialog is **accepted** — the recovery verb is exempt from the human-control gate (FR-035) and FR-041's refusal does not apply to an attended turn
- **And When** the same call is made on a turn **with** `AutoDenyAsk` set, **Then** it is refused by name (FR-041) and `{accept:false}` still dismisses
- **And** handling the dialog **acquires nothing**: the tab's ownership is unchanged afterwards, and no subsequent call behaves as though the agent had taken the tab
- *This scenario is uncomfortable on purpose. It is the exposure §12 A-22 routes to the ADR, written so that **narrowing it later is a deliberate edit to a named test** rather than a silent behaviour change — and so that a reviewer who thinks the first step should not be true has something concrete to argue with. **The last step is the one that answers the sibling grill's question about FR-035** (it bypasses ruling 1's mitigation entirely): dialog handling is not an acquisition, so the mitigation's subject and this exemption's subject are different things.*

---

## 9. Traceability matrix (FR ↔ US ↔ BDD ↔ test ↔ ADR/grill)

**41 live FRs** (FR-001…FR-042, FR-023 withdrawn), **65 live BDD scenarios** (S-01…S-68, S-52…S-54 withdrawn), **19 live user stories** (US-1…US-20, US-14 withdrawn). *Revision 4 added FR-039/FR-040/FR-041, S-63/S-64/S-65 and US-19. **Revision 6 adds FR-042, S-66/S-67/S-68 and US-20** — all of it ADR D1.9b ruling 2's, and none of it ruling 1's or ruling 3's, which add no requirement at all. Nothing is renumbered.* The three withdrawn identifiers are tombstoned rather than renumbered — deliberately, so that this document, both grills of it and the D1 spec's §14 cross-reference all keep resolving. A tombstone is not a dangling row: it carries no test **because D2 asserts nothing there**, and it names where the requirement actually lives.

**The coverage claim, stated so it is true (round-2 M3).** Revision 2 claimed *"every live FR has at least one US, one BDD scenario and one TDD entry"* and was contradicted three rows below by FR-025 and FR-026, both carrying `—` in the US and BDD columns. Corrected:

> **Every live FR has at least one TDD entry.** Every live FR **except the two build-gate FRs** (FR-025 `no contracts/ change`, FR-026 `no new runtime dependency`) additionally has at least one US and one BDD scenario. Those two are **build gates, not behaviour**: they have no actor, no observable outcome and nothing a Given-When-Then would add. They trace directly to **SC-007** and **SC-008**, and are marked `— (build gate)` rather than `—`, so the empty cell reads as a decision instead of an omission.

**Three acceptance criteria are deliberately non-automated**, and are likewise marked rather than left looking like gaps: **US-4/AC2** (SC-004's hand-run wall-clock measurement, §10 order 29), **US-17/AC3** (the config key's doc comment naming the removal issue, carried by SC-009), and — new in revision 6 — **US-20/AC5** (the `browser_evaluate` release-note item; no test can read a release note, and saying so is better than inventing an assertion that greps a changelog file).

## 10. TDD plan (ordered; Gate → Unit → Integration → E2E)

| Order | Test | Level | Traces to | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0a** | `TestUploadFile_NotRegistered` | **Gate** | FR-029 | **This is the gate that holds Stream B's upload registration, and it is GREEN, not red.** Asserts `browser_upload_file` is absent from the registry AND present in `buildKnownBuiltinToolNames()`. **No issue-state guard** — a Go unit test cannot observe a GitHub issue's state without a network call, nothing in `.github/workflows/` or the test tree does anything like it, and revision 2 named no mechanism because there is none (round-2 C3). `#659` lives in the test name's doc comment, where human context belongs. **Deleted in the same PR that registers the tool**, which must cite #659 as closed. |
| **0b** | `TestDelegatedSubTurn_AskWithNoApprover_Terminates` | **Oracle, carried by #659 — not a gate in this repo** | FR-029 | The oracle at the right layer: a delegated sub-turn invoking **any** `ask`-policy tool with no approver must **complete with a denial** under a bounded test timeout. **Written against a generic `ask` tool, not `browser_upload_file`** — so it needs no build FR-029 forbids to exist (revision 2's version required exactly such a build, which made it unrunnable as well as red). **Lands `t.Skip`-ped**, skip string `"#659: AutoDenyAsk is not inherited by delegated sub-turns"`; **the PR that closes #659 removes the skip.** A permanently-red committed test is not a gate — it contradicts Hard Constraint #7 and reds `pr.yml` forever, and a gate that can never go green blocks forever. **What actually unblocks Stream B's registration: 0a green + #659 closed + this skip removed and passing.** |
| **0c** | `TestChromedpEnablesPageDomainPerTarget` | Integration | FR-013/FR-014 | **Replaces revision 1's spike** — the question is answered (§2.2a); this pins the answer against a chromedp bump. |
| 1 | `TestValidateOverrideKeys_PanicsOnUnknown` | Unit | FR-022 | Cheapest; proves the Stream E ordering constraint before any seeding |
| 2 | `TestToolPolicyCoverage_SixNewBrowserTools_NoGaps` | Unit | FR-021 | **The boot blocker.** Must be red before Stream E, green after |
| 3 | `TestCoreAgentSeed_BrowserD2Posture` | Unit | FR-021 | Jim/Ray/Explorer/Researcher allow×5; Mia/Ava deny via the default, not a literal; Worker via the global |
| 4 | `TestCoreAgentSeed_UploadIsAskForEveryBrowsingAgent` / `_UploadIsDenyForNonBrowsingAgents` / `_ExplorerResearcherBrowserParity` / `_BrowsingAgentsCanCallServeWeb` | Unit | FR-021, FR-024, FR-030 | **Renamed and split in revision 3 (round-2 C1).** The first asserts `ask` for Jim/Ray/Explorer/Researcher/Worker; the second asserts `deny` for Mia/Ava **through the real compositor** (`resolveEffectivePolicyWith`), not by reading the seed map — the seed map alone would show only the agent-side `deny` and miss the merge that produces it. Together they replace `_UploadIsAskForEveryAgent`, which asserted a state the edit sites cannot produce and which contradicted order 3. `_BrowsingAgentsCanCallServeWeb` **computes** the agent set from resolved policy — the predicate is **`allow` for at least one `browser_*` tool** (round-2 m2), so `ask` never moves the set |
| **4a** | **`TestDefaultConfig_BrowserEvaluateEnabledSeededTrue`** (`pkg/config`) / **`TestEvaluate_PolicyDenyResolvesDeny_MiaAva`** (`pkg/coreagent`) + **`TestFilterToolsByPolicy_OmitsBrowserEvaluate_MiaAva`** (`pkg/tools`) + **`TestEvaluateTool_EnabledDoesNotReturnDisabledString`** and **`TestEvaluateTool_DescriptionDoesNotClaimOffByDefault`** (`pkg/tools/browser`) / **`TestConfig_ExplicitBrowserEvaluateFalseSurvivesSaveRoundTrip`** and **`TestResolveBool_BrowserEvaluateEnabled_NilMeansFalse`** (`pkg/config`) | Unit | **FR-042** | **New in revision 6 (ADR D1.9b ruling 2); the middle group split across packages in revision 7 (round-4 M2).** Sits in Stream E1 because the seed is an edit to `pkg/config/defaults.go`, E1's own file — **but E1 owns `pkg/config` and `pkg/coreagent` only, and revision 6 asked one test there to observe a dispatch outcome in two packages it does not own.** *(a)* The seed test alone passes on a build with the gate deleted *and* on a build with no tool policy, so it is evidence of nothing by itself. *(b)* The meaning comes from the split pair: `resolveEffectivePolicyWith` returns `ToolPolicyDeny` for Mia and Ava (a **value**, which is all that layer can see), **and** `FilterToolsByPolicy` omits the tool from their sent set — the observable consequence, and the one that separates "policy held" from "policy resolved and ignored". *(c)* The `pkg/tools/browser` pair proves the **enablement**: `Execute` no longer returns the `"disabled — set sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled=true"` string, and `Description()` no longer tells the model the tool is off by default (edit site 2j) while still describing the runtime disable. **Nothing asserts a "policy refusal message", because there is none** — a denied tool is filtered out, not refused (S-67's note). *(d)* `..._SurvivesSaveRoundTrip` is **expected RED on the first run** and stays red until `SandboxConfig.BrowserEvaluateEnabled` becomes a `*bool` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:235`; `omitempty` on a plain `bool` drops an explicit `false` at `SaveConfig`, `pkg/config/config.go:4413`). *A green first run there means the `*bool` fix landed without the test ever having had the chance to fail — re-run it against the plain `bool` before believing it.* *(e)* **`..._NilMeansFalse` is round-4 C2's assertion and is not optional:** against a bare `OmnipusSandboxConfig{}` it requires `ResolveBool(cfg.BrowserEvaluateEnabled, false) == false`. The precedent the `*bool` copies resolves nil → **`true`** (`config_b1_test.go:368`), so without this test the fix for (d) can ship arbitrary JavaScript ON in every construction that skips `DefaultConfig()`, with (d) green. **Edit site 2c is the consumer that must be changed for any of this to compile** (`pkg/agent/loop.go:2431`) |

The second is the one that **adds** coverage: it drives `set_config{key:"sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled"}` through the real `validateConfigKey` and asserts refusal, because **that key has no test anywhere in `pkg/` today** (verified) while the two escalation tests that look like its coverage name the field this FR deletes. The third is an **existing** test that goes red the moment the env tag is deleted (`env_prefix_guard_test.go:151-155`, message *"field renamed or removed?"*) — its `want` map entry at `:132` is removed in the same commit; the red is the test working, not a break. **Nothing here asserts a behaviour change, and that is correct:** nothing read the deleted field, so there is none to assert — see S-69's note on why a behavioural oracle here would be unfalsifiable |

It is now explicitly a recorded measurement with no pass/fail, and the assertable part of FR-007 lives at order 14 where CI can run it.

### Regression requirements

**These tests exercise the code paths this work changes and MUST keep passing, by name:**

| Test | File:line | Why it is at risk |
|---|---|---|
| `TestExecute_HappyChain_NavigateWaitClickGetText` | `execute_e2e_test.go:163` | The click path gains the four-condition gate |
| `TestExecute_Type_PersistsInDOM` | `execute_e2e_test.go:892` | The type path's `WaitVisible` is replaced |
| `TestTextSel_Click_HasTextPseudo_ClicksLink` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:109` | Text branch rerouted through `resolveTarget` |
| `TestTextSel_Click_TextParam_ClicksButton` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:151` | Same |
| `TestTextSel_Specificity_ClicksButtonNotWrappingDiv` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:191` | Specificity must survive the new seam |
| `TestTextSel_TypeTool_PseudoSelector_TypesIntoInput` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:379` | `browser_type` moves off `resolvePseudoOnlySelector` |
| `TestTextSel_Specificity_NoExtraProse_ClicksButtonNotDiv` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:511` | Same |
| `TestTextSel_TypeTool_PseudoSelector_TypesIntoContentEditable` | `text_selector_e2e_test.go:878` | Contenteditable + the gate |
| `TestExecute_TargetBlankClick_AdoptsNewTab` | `tab_adoption_e2e_test.go:77` | The per-tab dialog listener installs at `adoptTarget` |
| `TestReconcileTabs_OneClickTwoNewTargets_OneAdoptedOneStranded` | `tabs_test.go:908` | Same tab plumbing |
| `TestListenerReArm_*` (the `se.listenerTarget` bookkeeping tests) | `tabs_test.go:347-413` | The dialog listener adds a **second**, independent per-tab key; these must not start asserting it |
| `TestChokePoint_PerSurfaceCap_B15_GetText` | `per_tool_cap_alignment_test.go:11` | The snapshot must not change `browser_get_text`'s cap or its `capGetText` mechanism |
| `TestRegisterTools_RewireMustApplyNewSecurityState` | `register.go`'s cited regression | Six more `RegisterReplacing` calls on the hot-reload path |
| `TestBuildKnownBuiltinToolNames_MatchesCoreagentStaticToolCatalog` | `pkg/gateway` | Six names must land in metadata **and** `allStaticToolNames` |
| all of `tools_control_test.go`, `shared_control_test.go` | — | `controlledResult` gains the lease as a sibling; the deferral shape must not change, **and these must be extended to assert `browser_handle_dialog` does NOT consult it** (FR-035) |
| all of `blocked_schemes_test.go` | — | Asserts the current `file://` message text; **this test WILL need updating**, and it must be updated to assert the new literal, never deleted |
| all of `text_selector_test.go` | — | Unit-level seam behaviour |

**How to run the browser suite (do not run the full Go suite — OOM):**
`CGO_ENABLED=0 OMNIPUS_BROWSER_E2E=1 go test -tags goolm,stdjson -count=1 -p 1 ./pkg/tools/browser` — matching the CI job. `skipIfNoBrowser` (`browser_e2e_test.go`) skips in CI without that env var, which is exactly how coverage silently went to zero in #615.

Nothing asserted about the post-gate `chromedp.Click` traffic, which issues ≥1 `getBoxModel` and this change does not touch | FR-007 |

| element still animating when `chromedp.MouseClickNode` reads `getContentQuads` (animation resumes after the gate's two probes) | the click may land off-target — **accepted and stated**, not caught. `CondStable` is proven across RT1/RT2 only. No assertion; this row exists so the limit is recorded rather than discovered | FR-005 |
| `<select>` set by label, `change` listener armed | value set **and** listener fired | FR-009 |
| `<select>` set by **`value`** (`{value:"b"}` against `<option value="b">Beta</option>`) | Beta selected **and** listener fired — same outcome as by label | FR-009 |
| `<select>` with **both** `value` and `label` supplied | error naming both fields; nothing selected | FR-009 |
| `<select>` with `{value:"zzz"}` matching no option | same named error a non-matching `label` produces | FR-009 |
| `<select>` with **zero** `<option>` elements | named error | FR-009 |
| `<select multiple>`, labels `["Alpha","Beta","Gamma"]`, only Alpha/Beta exist | error naming `Gamma`; `selectedOptions.length === 0` | FR-009 |
| `key:"Ctrl+Banana"` | error listing accepted keys; input unchanged | FR-010 |
| `key:"Enter"`, nothing focused, no locator | succeeds; `focused_element: null`; **`waitActionable` not entered** (seam assertion); `controlledResult` and the lease **were** taken | FR-010 |
| hover target with a click counter | menu visible, counter `0` | FR-011 |
| upload path **inside** the work dir | attached; audit event `outcome: ok` | FR-012, FR-031 |
| upload path outside `AllowedRoots` | denied by `ResolvePath(FSOpWrite)` **before** `SetUploadFiles`; audit event `outcome: denied` | FR-012, FR-031 |
| upload of a **0-byte** file inside the work dir | attached; `input.files[0].size === 0` — an empty file is a legitimate upload, not an error | FR-012 |
| upload of a file **larger than the page's own limit** (e.g. 100 MB against a 10 MB `accept` limit) | `SetUploadFiles` succeeds — the page's validation is the page's business; the tool reports attachment, not acceptance | FR-012 |
| upload path that is a **symlink** into the work dir | resolved by `ResolvePath`'s realpath check; `RealPath()` TOCTOU window accepted and stated | FR-012 |
| upload path that **does not exist** (inside the work dir) | named error **before** `SetUploadFiles` is reached. **State which layer rejects it:** `ResolvePath(FSOpWrite)` has *write* semantics and may legitimately admit a not-yet-existing path, so the **tool** must `Stat` the resolved path and refuse — otherwise Chrome fails internally and the agent sees an opaque CDP error instead of a named one (round-2 m7) | FR-012 |
| upload path that is a **directory** | named error at the same tool-level `Stat`, before `SetUploadFiles`; `ResolvePath` does not distinguish file from directory (round-2 m7) | FR-012 |
| **browser-capable** agent (Jim/Ray/Explorer/Researcher/Worker), `browser_upload_file` policy | `ask` — never `deny`, never `allow` | FR-021 |
| **non-browsing** agent (Mia/Ava), `browser_upload_file` policy | `deny` — agent-level `deny` from `denyAllThenOverride` merged with global `ask` under deny-wins; asserted through the compositor, not off the seed map; **no per-agent literal** (round-2 C1) | FR-021 |
| delegated sub-turn, no approver, **any `ask`-policy tool** | **turn terminates** with a denial. Deliberately not `browser_upload_file`, whose registration FR-029 holds — the oracle needs no forbidden build (round-2 C3) | FR-029 |

| `alert()` on tab 2, then handle, then `get_text` | identical | FR-014 |

| `alert()` while a blocked click holds the lease | `browser_handle_dialog` runs; the blocked click then returns | FR-035 |
| `alert()` on a tab whose ctx was recreated | recorded; **exactly one** map entry | FR-014 |

| the same typo in `tier3SearchOnlyToolNames` | the partition test **fails** | FR-036 |
| any browser tool on a host with no Chromium | error names the missing browser and the install path | FR-033 |
| `IsControlled = true`, `browser_snapshot` on the tab the human is driving, page with a filled form | snapshot returns **with field values**; never `{"deferred": true}` — while `browser_click` on the same tab defers | FR-044 |
| `IsControlled = true`, `browser_click` on a **different** tab in the same browser | **defers** — `IsControlled(defaultSessionID)` is browser-global, not per-tab (`tools.go:69`, `:963`). The over-deferral is the cost, and it is asserted rather than described | FR-044 |
| `IsControlled = true`, **no** `AutoDenyAsk`, `browser_handle_dialog{accept:true}` on the human's tab | **accepted** — FR-035 exempts the verb from the control gate and FR-041 does not apply to an attended turn. Tab ownership is unchanged afterwards | FR-044 |
| `IsControlled = true`, **with** `AutoDenyAsk`, `browser_handle_dialog{accept:true}` | refused by FR-041; `{accept:false}` still dismisses | FR-044, FR-041 |
| `BrowserToolConfig` walked by reflection, and `env.GetFieldParams(&Config{})` enumerated | **no** field with json tag `evaluate_enabled`; **no** field resolving to `OMNIPUS_TOOLS_BROWSER_EVALUATE_ENABLED` | FR-043 |
| `set_config{key:"sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled", value:true}` from an agent | refused, through the `sandbox` subtree block. **The key has no test at all today** — the two that look like its coverage name the deleted `tools.browser.evaluate_enabled` | FR-043 |
| a bare `OmnipusSandboxConfig{}` (no `DefaultConfig()`), flag resolved | `ResolveBool(…, false)` is **`false`** — **not** `true`, which is what the `PathGuardAuditFailClosed` precedent resolves to | FR-042 |
| `EvaluateTool.Description()` on a stock install | does **not** say the tool is off by default at runtime; **does** still say a runtime disable exists and reads differently from a policy denial | FR-042 |

---

## 11. Functional requirements & success criteria

- **FR-001 … FR-044** as tabulated in §9 (FR-023 tombstoned). All MUST. *(FR-042 is new in revision 6 — ADR D1.9b ruling 2's `browser_evaluate` seed; rulings 1 and 3 add none. **FR-043 and FR-044 are new in revision 7:** FR-043 is ADR **D1.9d**'s deletion of the decoy flag, and FR-044 turns §12 **A-22** from prose into an assertion — round-4 M3. **ADR D1.9c adds no requirement here**, because nothing in this document reads the tab-ownership key.)*

**SC-001 (headline demonstration — NOT an automated criterion).** An agent completes a form containing a text input, a `<select>`, a file attachment and an Enter-key submit, on a page with generated class names, using **only** role + accessible-name locators — end to end, no CSS selector anywhere in the call sequence. This is the single scenario that is impossible on every axis today. **It is a demonstration performed by hand and recorded in the PR, with a named owner; it has no test row and no automation** (grill: SC-001 was previously presented alongside seven automated criteria without saying so). It also **depends on FR-029**: the file-attachment leg cannot be demonstrated until #659 lands, so SC-001 ships in two parts and the PR records which part is done.
  > **The trace begins with a `ToolSearch`, and the demonstration must record whether the agent performed it unprompted (§12 B-9, new in revision 6).** `browser_snapshot` is **Tier 3 — searchable only** (§12 A-3, RULED by ADR D1.9b ruling 3), so it is not in the agent's manifest until it goes looking. **An agent that reaches for `browser_screenshot` instead has not failed this criterion's mechanism — it has demonstrated the tier**, and the PR must say which happened. Prompting the agent to search first is legitimate and makes SC-001 a demonstration of `resolveTarget`; it is **not** a demonstration that the surface is discoverable, and the two must not be reported as one.

**SC-002.** A fresh install boots with all **seventeen** browser tools **seeded** and **zero** `ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` gaps. **Seventeen is re-derived at revision 6, not carried:** `pkg/tools/browser/register.go::RegisterTools` issues eleven `RegisterReplacing` calls (`:65-81`) and this spec adds six — **11 + 6 = 17**, with **no seventh tool**, because ADR **D1.9b ruling 1** rules tab acquisition implicit (§12 A-20). Of the seventeen, **sixteen are registered; one (`browser_upload_file`) is seeded but unregistered while FR-029 holds it.**
  **One caveat is retired here and it is worth saying why rather than deleting it silently.** Revisions 2–5 also carried *"one of which (`browser_evaluate`) is registered-and-runtime-inert unless `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled`"*. **That caveat no longer applies: ADR D1.9b ruling 2 seeds the flag `true` (FR-042), so `browser_evaluate` is registered AND live on a fresh install.** The reason the caveat existed — that a bare count is true and misleading at once (round-2 m5) — is still the right instinct, which is why the remaining exception is still spelled out. **`browser_upload_file` is not a template for anything** — see §5.

**SC-003.** Every actionability failure **on a path through `waitActionable`** is reported with the failing condition named; the failure-message test table covers all four literals, the constant-set test proves there is no fifth, and **no such path emits a bare "timeout"**.
  **Two scope statements, both load-bearing (round-2 M5).** *(i)* The scope is the gate, **and FR-037 extends it forward** through the post-gate `chromedp` dispatch — otherwise the criterion is unachievable while the tools keep `chromedp.Click`: verified, `Click` re-runs its own `NodeVisible` after the gate, `Selector.Do`'s `s.wait` swallows the resulting `ErrNotVisible` as `(false, nil)`, and `retryWithSleep` polls to the deadline and returns a bare `context deadline exceeded`. Revision 2 asserted "no path emits a bare timeout" over everything and would have shipped a criterion the first SPA re-render falsifies. *(ii)* Timeouts on paths that **never enter the gate** — read-only tools, resolution failures, the dialog wedge — are outside this criterion and are governed by FR-013's own wording rules instead.

**SC-004 (recorded measurement, NOT a pass/fail gate).**

> **⚠️ The source ADR still asserts the criterion this withdraws, and the ADR is the document that is behind (consolidated grill M4).** ADR-072 **§3.3 criterion 22** and its **§4** "D2 — lost / risked" bullet both still say the actionability pre-check is *"Budgeted at ≤150 ms p95"*. That text survived the ADR's 2026-09-01 consolidation, which post-dates this withdrawal. **This spec does not edit the ADR.** The conflict is recorded in **§0c AC-1** with all four defects spelled out, and the remedy — strike criterion 22 and the §4 bullet, or restate them as this measurement — is the ADR owner's. **It is not currently tracked anywhere an ADR reader would see it: ADR §9 lists the D1 spec, #509, ADR-048 and root `CLAUDE.md`, and has no section for this document at all.** §15 item 10.

Revision 1's "≤150 ms p95 delta on `performance-2x`" is **withdrawn** — four independent defects, all conceded (grill M2): the statistic was undefined (p95-of-after minus p95-of-before is not p95-of-paired-deltas, and only the second is a per-call budget); no harness existed and a Go benchmark cannot compare two builds; it was unrunnable in CI, and project policy makes CI the authority for Go results, so the criterion had no green anyone would ever see; and at n=100 on a host ADR-072 §7 itself measures at **85–99% utilisation**, the 5th-worst sample's spread swamps 150 ms in both directions. Separately, 150 ms is roughly two orders of magnitude larger than a local CDP round trip, so a gate costing 20× the click it guards would still have passed.

  **Replaced by two things.** The **assertable** part is FR-007: the fast path issues exactly two `Runtime.evaluate` round trips, counted at a seam, host-independent, and runnable in CI (§10 order 14). The **wall-clock** part is this recorded measurement, defined precisely enough to reproduce:
  - **Statistic:** the **paired per-call delta** — for each iteration, `t_after − t_before` for the same click on the same page against the same Chrome instance; report the **median and the p95 of those paired deltas**, plus n.
  - **Harness:** two binaries built from the same tree (one with `actionability_gate: full`, one with `visible_only` — the FR-034 switch makes this a config flip rather than two builds, which is why it exists), **one** Chrome instance, a static local fixture page with an immediately-actionable button, **≥1000** iterations, the first 100 discarded as warm-up, alternating A/B per iteration so drift affects both arms equally.
  - **Comparison:** `benchstat` over the two sample sets.
  - **Host:** whatever machine ran it — **recorded by name in the PR body along with `nproc`, total RAM and the load average at the time**. Not pinned, because nothing in this repo pins one; recorded, because an unattributed number is the failure `docs/internal/false-green-patterns.md` exists for.
  - **Threshold:** none. This is a number the reviewer reads, not a gate a build passes. **If the measured median delta exceeds the round-trip cost of two `Runtime.evaluate` calls by more than 5×, that is a finding to raise** — but a finding, argued on the evidence, not an automatic red.

**SC-005.** After a handled `alert()`/`confirm()`/`prompt()` on **any** tab index, a subsequent `browser_get_text` returns within the normal page timeout — **including when a human holds the live view and when another action tool holds the write lease** (FR-035). Asserted as the tab's continued responsiveness from the wedged state, not from the post-recovery state.

**SC-006.** The `pkg/tools/browser` suite passes with `OMNIPUS_BROWSER_E2E=1`, every test in §10's regression list included, and the CI pass floor at `.github/workflows/pr.yml:481` is raised **to a stated number with stated headroom, not to the measured count**. Verified: the current literal is `-lt 180` and its own comment reads *"Raise this number (never lower it without re-verifying the true count) as more tests land"* — its intent is a **floor with headroom**, and ratcheting to the exact measured count makes the next legitimately-skipped test a red build (grill m6). **The rule: new floor = floor(measured × 0.95), rounded down to the nearest 10.** The PR body must record the measured count, the machine, the date and the resulting floor. *(Illustrative only: if the suite measures 244 after this work, the floor becomes 230 — the actual number comes from the actual run.)*

**SC-007.** `make verify-contracts` green with a zero-line diff under `contracts/`, `pkg/api/generated/` and `src/lib/api/generated/` — the positive evidence for FR-025.

**SC-008.** `gofmt -l . | wc -l` is `0`; `golangci-lint run --build-tags=goolm,stdjson` exits 0; `go.mod` unchanged (FR-026).

**SC-009.** Every one of the three operability signals in §6.1 is emitted and asserted: a `browser_upload_file` audit event on every invocation, a metadata-only `browser_snapshot` audit event — both in underscore form, which SC-009 asserts against the `AuditEntry` contract pattern because a dotted name blanks the operator.s Audit Log view entirely (§12 B-6), and per-condition gate counters. **And the FR-034 removal issue exists and is referenced by number in the config key's doc comment.**

### Seeded policy table (FR-021, FR-024, FR-030) — the exact target state

| Tool | Global | Jim | Ray | Explorer | Researcher | Mia | Ava | Worker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `browser_select_option` | allow | allow | allow | allow | allow | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global² |
| `browser_press_key` | allow | allow | allow | allow | allow | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global² |
| `browser_hover` | allow | allow | allow | allow | allow | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global² |
| `browser_handle_dialog` | allow⁴ | allow⁴ | allow⁴ | allow⁴ | allow⁴ | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global²˒⁴ |
| `browser_snapshot` | allow | allow | allow | allow | allow | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global² |
| `browser_upload_file` | **ask** | **ask** | **ask** | **ask** | **ask** | deny¹˒³ | deny¹˒³ | inherits global² (**ask**) |
| `serve_web` (**FR-030, new grants in bold**) | allow | allow | **allow** | **allow** | **allow** | deny¹ | deny¹ | inherits global² (allow) |

¹ **Automatic, no per-agent edit** — `denyAllThenOverride` starts every `allStaticToolNames` member at `deny`; Mia and Ava list no browser override. The ADR's D2.9 table reads as four hand-edits; two of them cost nothing.
² `IDWorker` uses `tightenGlobalCeiling` — a **sparse** map. Absent names inherit the **global** ceiling. Today Worker inherits `allow` for all eleven browser tools and for `serve_web` by exactly this route; the six follow, so `browser_upload_file` lands at **`ask`** for Worker. Recorded as intended, not discovered.

⁴ **`allow` on the tool, with an argument-level guard — FR-041, new in revision 4.** The policy value is `allow` for every browser-capable agent, exactly as the other four verbs. What FR-041 adds is inside the tool: `{accept:true}` is refused on a turn with `AutoDenyAsk` set — no human to ask — while `{accept:false}` is never refused. **This is not expressible in tool policy**, which is per-tool and cannot see an argument, which is why it is a requirement rather than a seed value. Worker matters most here: it inherits the global `allow` through `tightenGlobalCeiling`, and it is the delegation-tier agent the guard exists for. Full argument at §11(b); the limit that FR-041's control does not engage for a delegated turn until **#659** lands is stated there too.

³ **CORRECTED IN REVISION 3, and RULED as of revision 4. Mia and Ava resolve `deny`, not `ask` — and revision 2's footnote here confused two different mechanisms (round-2 C1).**

  Revision 2's footnote claimed the global `ask` reaches Mia and Ava because *"the coverage validator's OR-semantics mean the global entry covers them"*. **That is a coverage rule being used as a resolution rule.** They are different functions with different jobs, and the difference decides this cell:

  | Mechanism | Function | What it answers |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Coverage** — OR-based | `config.ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` | *Is there an entry for this (agent, tool) on **either** side?* A global entry satisfies it for every agent. This is a boot-abort guard and it never chooses a value. |
  | **Resolution** — most-restrictive-wins | `pkg/tools/compositor.go::resolveEffectivePolicyWith`, fed by `pkg/agent/instance.go::agentToolsCfgToPolicy` | *Which value applies?* `deny > ask > allow` when both sides match. `agentToolsCfgToPolicy`'s own comment: *"global × agent most-restrictive-wins … a global deny always blocks."* |

  **Re-derived from source rather than asserted:** `pkg/coreagent/core.go::denyAllThenOverride` iterates `allStaticToolNames` and writes `config.ToolPolicyDeny` for **every** name, then applies the overrides — so once `browser_upload_file` is in `allStaticToolNames` (edit site 1), Mia and Ava carry an **explicit agent-level `deny`** for it. Mia's seed (`case IDMia:`) and Ava's (`case IDAva:`) both call `denyAllThenOverride` and neither names a browser tool. Agent `deny` + global `ask` → **`deny`**.

  **So the state revision 2 asserted was not producible from its own edit-site list** (which contains no Mia/Ava edit), and `TestCoreAgentSeed_UploadIsAskForEveryAgent` (order 4) and `TestCoreAgentSeed_BrowserD2Posture` (order 3) could not both pass. **"`ask` for every agent" is therefore read as "`ask` for every agent that holds the browser surface"** — Jim, Ray, Explorer, Researcher, Worker — which is exactly the population the ruling's own reasoning is about ("not `deny` for delegation-tier workers"). The global `ask` entry is seeded exactly as the ruling directs; it is the two zero-browser agents' own least-privilege posture that overrides it downward.

  **This is now RULED, not merely decided (consolidated grill m4).** Revision 3 recorded it as DECIDED-overrulable and asked for an ADR erratum, because the ADR's D2.9 table read `ask` for Mia and Ava. **That erratum has landed.** The consolidated ADR's D2.9 table reads **`deny`¹** for both, and its footnote ¹ states the mechanism in full — *"`denyAllThenOverride` writes an explicit agent-level `deny` for every catalog name an agent does not override, and `compositor.go::resolveEffectivePolicyWith` merges deny > ask > allow"* — reaching this spec's conclusion by this spec's reasoning. The two documents now agree, so there is nothing left to overrule and nothing left to ask the operator. Full record at **§12 A-16 (RULED)**; **§15 item 3 is closed.** The reversal cost is retained at edit site 6a only because a *future* operator may still want it, not because a decision is pending.

**Three posture decisions this table encodes, each argued rather than inherited from a group label:**

**(a) `browser_upload_file` = `ask` for every agent that holds the browser surface, delegation-tier workers included (round-1 grill C3; scope corrected by round-2 C1).** Revision 1 proposed `deny` for Explorer and Researcher, reasoning that unattended workers have no operator to answer an `ask`. **That is verbatim the argument the operator overrode** (ADR D2.9, 2026-08-31). The ruling stands and the concern it overrode is answered by FR-029, not by a per-agent `deny`: #659 lands, `AutoDenyAsk` is inherited by delegated sub-turns, and an unattended `ask` becomes a clean refusal instead of a hang. **Until #659 lands the tool is not registered at all**, so the failure mode the `deny` proposal existed to prevent cannot occur.

  **The population is the five browser-capable agents, and the two that hold no browser tool resolve `deny` by their own least-privilege default** — see footnote ³ and §12 A-16. The ruling's concern was delegation-tier workers, and every one of them (Explorer, Researcher, Worker) is `ask`; nothing the ruling argued for is lost.

**(b) `browser_handle_dialog` gets its own row, not a group grant (grill M8).** The concern is real and specific: `browser_handle_dialog{accept:true}` on `confirm("Delete this account?")` or an `onbeforeunload` is an affirmative, consequential action — Stream C's own argument is that accepting one "is indistinguishable from a click the agent did not make". Ray's seed says he "researches and reports, he doesn't build or run", and already denies `browser_evaluate` for exactly this class of reason, with the reason inline. Granting `allow` by folding the verb into "the five action/read tools" would be inheriting a decision from a label.

  **Decision: `allow`, with `accept` defaulting to `false`.** Three reasons, in order of weight. (i) **A denied recovery verb is a wedged session.** The only agent that can unwedge a tab is the agent that wedged it; `deny` reproduces exactly the failure ADR D2.3 forbids, and Ray, Explorer and Researcher are among the agents most likely to hit a dialog, because they browse arbitrary public sites. (ii) **The dangerous half is `accept:true`, and the default is now `accept:false`** — dismissal unwedges every dialog type, and dismissal of a destructive `confirm()` is the *safe* answer, so an agent that reflexively calls the tool with no argument does the conservative thing. (iii) **`accept:true` is recorded**: it appears in the tool call the operator sees in the chat thread. **This leg now rests on a guard rather than on an unpinned property (round-2 m3).** Revision 2 leant it on the same zero-`browser`-in-`toolVisibility.ts` fact that FR-028 needed a regression test for, and gave the dialog tool no equivalent assertion — an argument leaning on a property the spec elsewhere treats as needing a guard. **S-43 now asserts `shouldRenderToolCall` returns `true` for all six names, `browser_handle_dialog` among them**, so (iii) cites a test. *(Reasons (i) and (ii) carry the decision on their own regardless.)* **Overrulable:** an operator who disagrees can seed `browser_handle_dialog: ask` for the research agents; the tool works identically, it just prompts. Recorded here so the decision is visible rather than implied.

  **⚠️ The argument above was made about attended research agents, and it does not answer the delegated unattended case (consolidated grill, unasked question). Revision 4 answers it.** The question nobody had asked in either document: `browser_handle_dialog` is exempt from **both** gates (FR-035), the ADR permits it *"even while a human holds control"* (D1.8), and **`IDWorker` inherits the global `allow` for it** through `tightenGlobalCeiling`'s sparse map — so **what stops a delegated, unattended `Worker` accepting `confirm("Delete this account?")` on the operator's signed-in session?** Under **D1.10** that session is real and shared, so the question is not hypothetical. §11(b)'s three reasons do not cover it: (i) the wedge argument justifies *dismissal*, never *acceptance*; (ii) "`accept` defaults to `false`" protects against a reflexive call, not a deliberate one; and (iii) "the operator sees `accept:true` in the chat thread" is **exactly the claim FR-039 has just disproved for delegated work** — a delegated `browser_handle_dialog` renders nowhere in the parent thread by default, so reason (iii) evaporates precisely where the risk is highest.

  **Decision: `allow` stands, and `accept:true` is refused when there is nobody to ask — FR-041.** The signal is the **existing** `AutoDenyAsk` field (`pkg/agent/loop.go:599`), which already means "no human can answer an approval on this turn"; nothing new is invented and no second attended/unattended discriminator is introduced (§5 states why that matters against D1.10). `{accept:false}` is **never** refused, so the recovery guarantee that carries §11(b) reason (i) is untouched — an unattended agent can always unwedge itself, it just cannot press "OK".

  **Two limits, stated rather than glossed.** *(i)* **`AutoDenyAsk` is set only on headless scheduled runs today** (`loop.go:6958`) and is **not inherited by delegated sub-turns** — that is **#659**, the same prerequisite FR-029 rides on. So until #659 lands, FR-041's control does not engage for a delegated `Worker`, which is exactly the population it is for. This is an honest gap of the same shape as FR-029's, and it is one more reason #659 is a prerequisite for this work rather than adjacent to it. *(ii)* An agent that genuinely needs to accept a dialog on an unattended turn cannot; the named error tells it so, and the operator's remedy is to run that work attended. **Overrulable** — §12 A-21 names the alternative (drop FR-041 and accept that a delegated Worker can confirm anything on the operator's sessions).

**(c) `serve_web: allow` for Ray, Explorer and Researcher is a real posture change, argued (grill M4).** `serve_web` scaffolds and serves a web app from the sandbox. Granting it to three research-tier agents widens what they can do beyond reading. **Why it is nonetheless right:** (i) FR-019's whole purpose is to end #242's dead end, and a pointer to a tool the recipient resolves `deny` for is the same dead end one failed tool call further away; (ii) all three already hold `write_file` or its equivalent within their confinement, so the marginal capability is *serving* an already-writable file over the existing preview listener, not new write reach; (iii) the preview route is already token-authenticated and confined by `gateway.preview_enabled`. **The alternatives considered and rejected:** making the error message conditional on the caller's resolved policy (the `ValidateURL` seam has no access to the calling agent's policy map, so this needs plumbing that D2 does not otherwise justify) and saying "this may not be granted to you" in the message (honest, but leaves the capability gap the fix exists to close). **Note the existing ten-allow/one-deny shape of these agents' grants is the precedent this reasons from** — their browser grant is *not* uniform, it embeds a least-privilege judgement excluding `browser_evaluate`, and this decision makes the same kind of judgement explicitly rather than by group.

  > **⚠️ The precedent is real; its premise moved, and it has since been RULED — this argument survives, and the carve-out it names has changed shape.** The ten-allow/one-deny carve-out was set when a browser was **per agent**. Under **D1.10** it is per workspace and carries the operator's live logins for every site the workspace has visited; the ADR's §6 recorded that **Jim's `browser_evaluate` grant *"predates the sharing model and has not been re-examined against it."*** **ADR D1.9b ruling 2 (2026-09-01) examined it and kept the grant, seeding `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` `true` so the tool is live (FR-042).**
  > **This section's argument is unchanged by that, and the reason is the point:** it argues from *"none of the five new verbs is arbitrary-code-adjacent"* — a property of the verbs, not of Jim — so it would have survived a revocation and it survives the enablement. **What does change is the sentence describing the precedent.** Explorer's and Researcher's grant is still literally ten allow / one deliberate deny for `browser_evaluate` (`pkg/coreagent/core.go`, *"(NOT browser_evaluate)"*), but **Jim's eleventh is now allow-and-live rather than allow-and-inert**, so "the roster's embedded judgement about arbitrary-code-adjacent verbs" is a judgement with real consequences on a stock install, not a paper one. Read it that way. **§12 A-19**; §15 item 8 CLOSED.

### Required edit sites, exhaustive (`file::symbol` form — grill m2)

| # | Site | Change | Order |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `pkg/coreagent/core.go::allStaticToolNames`, browser block | six names | **First**, or `validateOverrideKeys` panics |
| 2 | `pkg/config/defaults.go`, global `sandbox.tool_policies` browser block | six entries: five `allow`, `browser_upload_file: ask` | **This one edit closes the boot-abort risk for every agent** (OR-semantics) |
| **2a** | `pkg/config/defaults.go`, the `Sandbox: OmnipusSandboxConfig{…}` literal (`:166`) | **NEW in revision 6 — `BrowserEvaluateEnabled: true` (FR-042).** Not a tool-policy entry: it is the separate `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` key that gates `EvaluateTool.Execute` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:869-872`). Seeded as install-time **data** an operator may edit, exactly like `FilesystemModel` and `AllowedExecPaths` in the same literal — never a fallback branch. **Reaches upgrading installs too**, because `loadConfig` (`pkg/config/migration.go:19`) unmarshals the operator's JSON over `DefaultConfig()`; that is the same intended posture change `FilesystemModel`'s own comment documents, and it is why AC5's release note is mandatory | with 2 |
| **2b** | `pkg/config/sandbox.go:235` `BrowserEvaluateEnabled` | **NEW in revision 6 — `bool` → `*bool` (FR-042/AC4).** Required *by* 2a, not merely alongside it: with `omitempty` on a plain `bool`, `SaveConfig`'s `json.MarshalIndent` (`pkg/config/config.go:4413`) omits an operator's explicit `false`, so the next boot re-applies the seeded `true` and the only off-switch silently reverts. Harmless while the seed was absent; a real regression once it is `true`. Matches `PathGuardAuditFailClosed *bool` seven lines above (`:228`) — **the same pattern, and DELIBERATELY NOT the same default: see 2c, which is where round-4 C2 lands.** The pointer is borrowed for its unset-vs-explicitly-false shape; `PathGuardAuditFailClosed` resolves nil → `true` and this field must resolve nil → **`false`**. **S-68 must be RED against the plain `bool` before this lands** | **with 2a and 2c — not after, and not without 2c.** Landing 2a alone ships the regression; landing 2b without 2c does not compile |
| **2c** | `pkg/agent/loop.go`, the browser-registration block — `evaluateEnabled := cfg.Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled` (`:2431`) | **NEW in revision 7 — round-4 C2, and it is the site that makes 2b compile.** *(Revision 6 called its edit-site list "exhaustive" and omitted the field's **only consumer**.)* Becomes `config.ResolveBool(cfg.Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled, false)`. **The default MUST be `false`, and this is the whole of C2:** 2b cites `PathGuardAuditFailClosed` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:228`) as the precedent for the `*bool`, and that precedent resolves **nil → `true`** (`pkg/config/resolve.go:17`, asserted by `config_b1_test.go:368`). **Copied literally, every `OmnipusSandboxConfig` not built through `DefaultConfig()` — including every test construction and every zero-value struct — would get arbitrary JavaScript ON.** The pointer is borrowed for its *unset-vs-explicitly-false* shape only; the default it resolves to is this spec's own choice, and it is `false`, because the seed (2a) is what turns the capability on and it lives in `DefaultConfig()` where an operator can see and edit it. **Pinned by an assertion, not by this paragraph:** order 4a asserts `ResolveBool(nil, false) == false` against a bare `OmnipusSandboxConfig{}` — a test that fails on a copy-pasted `true` | **with 2a and 2b — all three or none.** 2b alone does not compile |
| **2d** | `pkg/config/config_b1_test.go:383` and `:413` | **NEW in revision 7 (round-4 C2 fallout, and round-4 m3).** `cfg.Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled = true` and `if !loaded.Sandbox.BrowserEvaluateEnabled` are plain-`bool` uses inside `TestBootConfigRoundTrip_NewFields`; both **fail to compile** against 2b's `*bool`. **And the round trip they assert is not the one that matters:** `omitempty` drops only the **zero** value, so a `true` round-trips before and after the change — this test passes either way and covers nothing S-68 is about. Worse after 2a: with the seed at `true`, `:383`'s explicit assignment becomes redundant and `:413`'s assertion passes even if the assignment is deleted. **Convert both to the pointer form and add the `false` case, or leave the `true` case and cite S-68 next to it** — what must not happen is a reader finding `:413` and concluding the round trip is covered. Named in §10's regression list for that reason | with 2b |

Defaults to false (deny-by-default per SEC-04/SEC-06). It is not a *reader* of the value (the ruling's survey is right about that, which is why the deletion is still safe), but after 2e it names a key that does not exist. **Re-point it at the surviving control or drop the clause** — it must not keep citing a deleted field as the reason a subtree is blocked | with 2e |
| **2h** | `pkg/sysagent/tools/config_blocklist_test.go:117` and `pkg/sysagent/tools/config_privilege_test.go:75` | **NEW in revision 7 — the decision, argued, because both tests stay GREEN either way and that is the trap.** Both name `tools.browser.evaluate_enabled`; both are blocked through the **`tools.browser` subtree** entry by `configKeyCovers` (`pkg/sysagent/tools/config.go:787-797`), never through the field, so deleting the field leaves two green tests asserting an escalation against a key that does not exist. **Decision: re-point both rows at `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled`** — the same escalation ("enable arbitrary in-page JavaScript"), a key that exists, blocked through the `sandbox` subtree entry. **Why this is a net gain and not bookkeeping: `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` has ZERO test occurrences in `pkg/` today** (verified), so the one control that works is untested while the one that does nothing has two tests. **Alternative if overruled:** delete both rows — cheaper, and it leaves the live key uncovered, which is how this state arose | with 2e |
| **2i** | **The eight deny-by-default doc comments** — `pkg/config/config.go:3679-3681` (deleted with 2e), `pkg/config/sandbox.go:230-234` (drop *"Mirrors Tools.Browser.EvaluateEnabled"* **and** *"Defaults to false"*), `pkg/config/defaults.go:629` (*"browser_evaluate stays opt-in (EvaluateEnabled=false) per SEC-04/SEC-06"* — names the deleted field **and** is now false), `pkg/tools/browser/register.go:15-16`, `pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:821` and `:826-828`, `pkg/agent/loop.go:2298-2299` and `:2425-2427`, plus `pkg/tools/browser/wave6b_evaluate_gate_test.go:41` (*"=false (the default)"*) | **NEW in revision 7 — in scope per D1.9d**, which puts them there *"or the code's own record will contradict the shipped default"*. **The ruling counts seven; re-derived here as eight comment sites plus one test-file comment**, because it does not name `tools.go:821` (*"Denied by default in deny-by-default policy mode (SEC-04/SEC-06)"*) or `loop.go:2298-2299`. **Reviewed, not tested** — US-21/AC6 says why a grep-for-a-sentence assertion is worse than a review item. **The SEC-04/SEC-06 citations are the load-bearing part:** they attribute the deny-by-default posture to a BRD requirement, and a comment that cites a requirement id for a posture the product no longer ships is the most convincing kind of wrong | with 2a and 2e |

A policy of allow does not mean this tool is usable; check the tool result for the runtime-disabled error."* After 2a that is false on a stock install. **This is a returned string, not a comment, so unlike 2i it IS assertable** — FR-042/AC6 and order 4a's third test. **Do not simply delete the paragraph:** the *runtime gate still exists* and an operator can still set the flag `false`, so the description must state that the tool is on by default **and** that a runtime disable is possible and reports itself distinctly from a policy denial — which is exactly the distinction S-67 turns on | with 2a |
