## 12. Ambiguity self-audit

**Status legend:** **RULED** = decided by the operator, recorded, not re-litigable here. **DECIDED** = decided by this spec on the evidence; overrulable, and the alternative is named. **OPEN** = genuinely needs a ruling before the affected stream opens.

| # | Ambiguity | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **A-1** | **`serve_web` vs `web_serve`.** The registered name is **`serve_web`** (`pkg/tools/web_serve.go::ToolNameWebServe`), corroborated by `previewedLazyToolNames`. | **RESOLVED BY CODE.** FR-019 specifies the literal `serve_web`; its test asserts the literal so the wrong name cannot leak back in. **The ADR has since corrected itself** (D2.5's inline note, 2026-08-31). Root `CLAUDE.md`'s ADR-044 paragraph still says `web_serve` and is a separate doc fix. *(Grill O2: revision 1 presented this as a live contradiction with the ADR; it is now a stale-elsewhere issue, and this row is downgraded accordingly.)* |
| **A-2** | **The snapshot's disclosure posture.** | **RULED, 2026-08-31 (Daniel Piatkowski): the snapshot returns field values by default.** Revision 1 proposed omit-by-default for `textbox`/`searchbox`/`combobox`/`spinbutton`/`slider` roles plus an `include_values: true` opt-in. **That was offered and declined** — an agent cannot verify a form is correctly filled before submitting it without seeing what is in the fields. The proposal is **deleted**, not deferred: FR-018 emits values unconditionally, and the tool schema has **no** `include_values` property (asserted by `TestSnapshot_SchemaHasNoIncludeValues`). The accepted risk — a card number or a partially typed password reaching the conversation and the 90-day transcript — is stated in §2.3 and in §4. The two non-optional mitigations are **FR-027** (sensitive-value replacer, defence in depth) and **FR-028** (operator-inspectable capture), each with a scenario, a test and a dataset row. **One correction the ruling's own text needs:** the ADR names the ActivityPanel as the visibility surface; verified against `src/hooks/useRunningActivity.ts`, that panel renders only subagent spans, background bash sessions and judge verdicts, never an arbitrary tool call. FR-028 therefore specs chat-thread rendering plus an audit event, and §11 edit site 21 files the ADR erratum. |
| **A-3** | **`browser_snapshot`'s manifest tier.** | **RULED 2026-09-01 — ADR D1.9b ruling 3: Tier 3, searchable only. Option A. This spec's recommendation (Option B) was NOT taken, and the reasoning behind the recommendation is not thereby wrong — it is outweighed.** The snapshot is treated exactly like the other eleven browser tools, and **ADR-071's previewed set is left intact at 7**, so no other accepted ADR is amended and the governance question this row could not settle **dissolves rather than being answered**. **Cost, re-derived at revision 6 from the fixture rather than carried from this row:** zero production edits (Tier 3 is the residual, so `manifest.go::previewedLazyToolNames` and `TestVisibility_PreviewedSetIsExactlySeven` are untouched); the Tier-3 fixture goes **62 → 68** (`pkg/tools/manifest_test.go:667-681` — `var …= []string{` at 667, `}` at 681, **62 quoted names counted, not assumed**, plus this spec's six); the union literal goes **87 → 93** (`TestVisibility_TierArithmetic`, `:694-744`: 17 full + 7 previewed + 62 search-only + 1 infra = 87 today → 17 + 7 + **68** + 1 = **93**). *A collision worth flagging to whoever edits that test: **`17` appears in it as the Tier-1 `FullManifestToolNames` count and separately as this spec's browser-tool surface count. They are unrelated numbers that happen to match. Do not "fix" one by touching the other.*** **The consequence the ruling states plainly, and this document now carries rather than softens:** *a tool an agent must search for is not a default*, so **D2.4's "the default way an agent reads a page" is false — and only PARTLY corrected in the ADR.** `356e587d3` is eight added lines and no deletions: a banner above D2.4, with the Decision sentence, D2.8's *"open question — §6"* tier row and ADR §6's open-question bullet all left standing (**§0c AC-5**, round-4 M1). **Agents will keep reaching for `browser_screenshot`, because that is what is in front of them.** The better mechanism exists and is not the path of least resistance. **§15 item 1 is CLOSED; Stream E2 is UNBLOCKED**; edit sites 9-12 collapse to one literal set each; and **the second-order effect this row identified is untouched by the ruling and is now the live risk**: `browser_screenshot` and `browser_get_text` are *also* Tier 3, so an agent that never searches reads the page by none of the three routes. That is ADR-071's tiering working as designed for a rarely-used tool, applied to a tool this spec expects to be used constantly — recorded at **B-9**, not disputed here. |
| **B-9** | **The snapshot is the tool this design is built around and the tier makes it the least reachable of the three ways to read a page** (new in revision 6; the residue of A-3's ruling, not a re-litigation of it). | **ACCEPTED, and the acceptance is the ruling's, not this document's.** D2.4's whole argument for `browser_snapshot` is that `browser_screenshot` needs vision and `browser_get_text` needs a selector the agent does not have. **Tier 3 puts all three behind a `ToolSearch`, so the ranking an agent actually experiences is set by its priors, not by the manifest** — and its priors, trained on every other tool surface in existence, say *screenshot*. **What follows for this document, concretely:** SC-001's headline demonstration and holdout 1 both require a role/name-only trace, and **both now begin with a `ToolSearch` the agent will not perform unprompted** — stated in each, so a failed demonstration is read as the tier and not as a defect in `resolveTarget`. **What would close it and is deliberately not proposed here:** Tier 2 for the snapshot, which is precisely what the ruling declined; or a description-level nudge in the *other* browser tools pointing at it, which is a prompt change with no test and belongs to whoever owns the tool descriptions. **What this document must not do is quietly assume the snapshot is reached.** Re-checked at revision 6, and stated precisely rather than as a blanket: **the only places that name a tier at all are the ones whose subject IS tiering** — FR-020, US-13/AC1-AC2, S-50, §6's manifest bullet and edit sites 9-10. **No behavioural FR, no dataset row and no success criterion presumes the agent has the tool in hand**; the two places that come closest — SC-001 and holdout 1, both hand-run — now say so out loud. That is the property revision 3 promised, re-verified rather than inherited, and it is what made this ruling free to apply. |
| **A-4** | **Explorer's and Researcher's posture on the six.** | **RULED for `browser_upload_file`; DECIDED for the other five.** The ADR's D2.9 table originally omitted both agents; its 2026-08-31 correction adds them. **`browser_upload_file` = `ask`** for both, per the operator ruling — revision 1's `deny` proposal is **deleted**, and its reasoning ("unattended delegation-tier workers with no operator to answer an `ask`") is verbatim the argument the ruling overrode; FR-029 is the correct answer to that concern. **The other five = `allow`**, decided on the corrected premise: **their existing grant is TEN browser tools, not eleven.** `browser_evaluate` is deliberately excluded from both, and Explorer's block says so inline (*"(NOT browser_evaluate)"*), as does Ray's (*"NOT browser_evaluate — arbitrary JS"*). So "parity with their existing grant" does not mean "the whole surface" — it means *ten allow plus one deliberate deny*, an embedded least-privilege judgement about arbitrary-code-adjacent verbs. Re-derived from that shape: none of the five is arbitrary-code-adjacent (`select_option`, `press_key`, `hover`, `snapshot` are all bounded verbs; `handle_dialog` is argued separately in §11(b)), so `allow` is consistent with the judgement already made rather than an extension of it. **Revision 4 addendum (consolidated grill M11): the carve-out this row reasons from had an open question upstream — see A-19. RULED at revision 6 (ADR D1.9b ruling 2): the grant stands and `browser_evaluate` is now live.** The judgement holds, as revision 4 predicted it would either way, because it argues from a property of the five verbs rather than from Jim's grant. **One word in the row's own reasoning is now sharper, not softer:** the carve-out these two agents embed excludes a tool that **actually executes** on a stock install, so "an embedded least-privilege judgement about arbitrary-code-adjacent verbs" describes a live control rather than a dormant one. **`handle_dialog` additionally gains an argument-level guard in revision 4 — FR-041, A-21** — which does not change this row's `allow`. |
| **A-5** | **`Page` domain enablement per tab.** | **CLOSED BY EVIDENCE, not a spike.** `chromedp@v0.15.1/chromedp.go::Context.attachTarget` executes `page.Enable()` for every non-worker target during its own bring-up, and every tab in this package reaches that path. No per-tab enable is needed, no extra round trip lands on FR-007's neighbours, and M6's "no" branch — which would have required a CDP call inside a function documented as "must be called with `m.mu` held" — does not arise. Additionally, `chromedp@v0.15.1/target.go` lists `EventJavascriptDialogOpening` among its **explicitly ignored** page events, confirming chromedp installs no browser dialog handler of its own. Pinned by §10 order 0c against a dependency bump. |
| **A-6** | **Measuring the gate's cost.** | **RESTRUCTURED (was: an unpinned-host assumption).** Split in two. The **assertable** part is FR-007's CDP round-trip count, host-independent and CI-runnable. The **wall-clock** part is SC-004, a recorded measurement with a defined statistic (paired per-call delta), a defined harness (≥1000 iterations, alternating arms, one Chrome, `benchstat`), no threshold, and the host recorded by name. Revision 1's ≤150 ms p95 is withdrawn in full — see SC-004 for the four reasons. |
| **A-7** | **`ErrNotActionable.Failed` reports the FIRST unmet condition, not all of them.** A disabled element under an overlay is both. | **DECIDED, recorded.** First-in-evaluation-order (`visible → stable → enabled → hit-testable`), because a later condition is meaningless while an earlier one is false. Deterministic and testable; a set-valued report would make the table test order-dependent for no agent benefit. |
| **A-8** | **`browser_snapshot`'s handle format.** D2.4 says "the handles needed to act on them" without naming a form. | **DECIDED, recorded.** A 0-based `index` into the snapshot's own ordering, which is the **same** document ordering Stream A's multi-match uses — so a handle read from a snapshot resolves identically in the next call. An opaque node id would be a second identity scheme that goes stale on the next DOM mutation with no way for the agent to tell. **And it is deliberately not a wire type** — §6's `contracts/` note. |
| **A-9** | **`browser_upload_file`'s `FSOp` and confinement.** | **DECIDED — `FSOpWrite`, reversing revision 1.** Revision 1 chose `FSOpSend` plus a hand-rolled `policy.AllowedRoots` check. Verified: **`FSOpSend` + `AllowedRoots` IS `FSOpWrite`'s path rule**, so that design re-implemented a chokepoint rule outside the chokepoint — a second string check, therefore a second TOCTOU window on top of the accepted `RealPath()` one. And `FSOpSend`'s own doc records that a path-based gate for that op was *"explicitly rejected"* by the operator, "so the real gate is tool policy"; revision 1 cited the first half of that reasoning and built the gate the second half rejects. **`FSOpWrite` gives the identical practical rule, enforced once, at the chokepoint.** Why an upload is a write: the operation hands a host path to a process outside the gateway's confinement, and the set of paths safe to hand out that way is exactly the set `FSOpWrite`/`FSOpServe` already bound. **Named alternative if overruled:** `FSOpSend` **alone**, tool policy as the sole gate, exactly as its doc instructs. Shipping **both** is the one option ruled out. **Consequence:** US-8/AC1's fixture narrows to a work-dir-or-mount path. **The `RealPath()` TOCTOU window remains, is unavoidable (`SetUploadFiles` has Chrome perform the read), and is stated rather than hidden.** |
| **A-10** | **Whether `browser_press_key` without a locator is an "action" for lease/deferral purposes.** | **DECIDED, recorded.** Yes — it calls `controlledResult` and takes the write lease, because it fights for the cursor exactly as `browser_type` does. It skips `waitActionable` when no locator is supplied (there is nothing to gate on), and the result reports `focused_element: null` when nothing had focus. **Symmetrically (grill O3): it rejects a `Text` locator by name**, the same collision `browser_type` has, because `key` is a value and not a locator. |
| **A-11** | **The tool count in ADR §4 ("11 → 17").** | **No action. The count is 17 and it is now settled on both sides.** Re-derived at revision 6 rather than carried forward: `pkg/tools/browser/register.go::RegisterTools` issues eleven `RegisterReplacing` calls (`:65-81`), and this spec adds six, so **11 + 6 = 17**. **The one dependency that could have moved it is discharged:** revision 4 flagged that a Shape-A take-control tool would make it 18; **A-20 is RULED implicit (D1.9b ruling 1)**, so there is no seventh tool and no consequential move in SC-002, §11's seeded table, edit sites 1-7 or the Tier-3 fixture literals. Note SC-002's caveat about what "registered" means for `browser_upload_file` — and that **`browser_evaluate`'s caveat is retired by D1.9b ruling 2**, which seeds `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` true (FR-042), so the tool is no longer registered-and-inert. |
| **A-12** | **`browser_handle_dialog`'s policy posture** (new; grill M8). | **DECIDED — `allow` with `accept` defaulting to `false`.** Full argument in §11(b): a denied recovery verb is a wedged session; the dangerous half is `accept:true` and the default is now the conservative one; and the call is visible to the operator in the chat thread. **Overrulable to `ask` for the research agents** without changing any code. |
| **A-13** | **Whether the actionability gate needs a revert switch** (new; grill M13). | **DECIDED — yes, FR-034, and time-boxed.** The change is unconditional on every click and keystroke on every page, `CondHitTestable` fails on shadow-DOM and iframe-hosted targets and `CondStable` on any permanently animating page — both common. The overcomplexity objection (a permanent flag on a hot path has its own cost) is real, which is why the switch copies ADR-071 `previewAllLazy`'s **time-boxed** shape: one atomic read inside one chokepoint, deleted in the same change that acts on the FR-032 counters, with the removal issue referenced by number in the key's doc comment (SC-009). **Who owns pulling the trigger, and on what (round-2 unasked-question 3):** "deleted in the change that acts on the data" needs someone to decide the data says act, and revision 2 named nobody and no threshold. **The removal issue carries both**, and this spec states what it must contain: an **owner** (the operator, or a named maintainer the operator designates — this spec cannot assign a person), a **review date** no later than **60 days after the feature merges**, and a **decision rule over the FR-032 counters**: if `gate_failure_total{condition="stable"|"hit-testable"}` over the window is a **negligible fraction of total gated actions** (the operator sets the number when the first real data exists — this spec deliberately does not invent a threshold with no baseline behind it), the switch is deleted; if it is not, the *gate* is the thing to fix and the switch stays only until that fix lands. **A switch with no review date is a permanent flag on a hot path**, which is the overcomplexity objection this design conceded was real. |
| **A-14** | **The ADR's `accessibility.Node.Value` citation** (new). | **Erratum, no design impact.** ADR D2.11 cites `cdproto/accessibility/types.go:206`; verified, that line is `AXPropertySource.Value`. The field the snapshot reads is `Node.Value` at `types.go:461`. Recorded so it is not re-derived; filed with the D2.11 ActivityPanel erratum (§11 edit site 21). |
| **A-15** | **Whether the `Locator` abstraction earns its keep** (new; grill m8). | **DECIDED — yes, on the strength of the matrix, not the struct.** The objection was fair: `resolveTarget` "supersedes" two functions that both survive as internal branches, to add one locator kind whose output is the same marker selector the text path already produces. What justifies it is the **per-tool locator matrix** in §3, which revision 1 had one entry of and now has five — two tools reject `Text` for the same value-vs-locator reason, two tools accept no locator at all, and one tool has a legal no-locator mode. That table has to live somewhere and be enforced somewhere; a struct with a validation function is the cheapest place. **If the matrix is ever reduced back to one row, this decision should be revisited.** |
| **B-1** | **Snapshot fetch cost is unbounded** (assumption, recorded). | `GetFullAXTree` runs in full on a 5,000-node page before any truncation, bounded only by the tool's page timeout. **Not** bounded by a requirement in v1. Measured post-hoc by §13 holdout 6. Recorded rather than implied away. |
| **B-2** | **Snapshot vs the ADR-066 turn budget** (assumption, recorded). | The 64,000-byte cap is per-tool; the turn budget is ADR-066's `windowTrim` and is out of scope. A snapshot plus a `get_text` in one turn is 128,000 bytes from two tools that each obey their own cap. Stated, not solved. |
| **B-3** | **What the `ask` prompt for `browser_upload_file` shows the operator** (grill unasked-question 3). | **Out of this spec's control, and flagged.** The approval prompt is rendered by the agent-loop/SPA approval flow, not by the tool. An approval that names only the tool is not a decision — the operator needs **the resolved path and the target origin**. Both are in FR-031's audit fields, so the data exists; **whether the prompt renders them is an approval-flow change this spec does not own.** **Promoted from a note to a stated exit condition on #659 (round-2 unasked-question 5).** Revision 2 filed it as "a note against #659's work", which the round-2 grill correctly called the weakest of the three dispositions in §15 — it is the entire value of the `ask` the operator ruled for, and a note is not a commitment. **FR-029 already makes #659 a hard prerequisite for registering `browser_upload_file`; this spec therefore states the prerequisite in full: #659 must deliver both (a) `AutoDenyAsk` inheritance by delegated sub-turns, and (b) an approval prompt that renders the resolved path and the target origin.** Both are the same flow and the same change. **This spec cannot bind another issue's scope** — that is the operator's or #659's owner's call — so if (b) is declined there, it must be declined **explicitly** and this row updated to record that the `ask` ships as a yes/no with no facts. What it must not do is stay a note nobody acts on. Listed in §15 item 5. |
| **B-4** | **`gateway.preview_enabled = false` makes the `file://` route dead for everyone** (round-1 unasked-question 9). | **Handled in the message, not in code.** FR-019's string ends `(requires the serve_web tool and gateway.preview_enabled)`. One clause, no extra round trip, honest when the flag is off. A conditional message would need `ValidateURL` to read live gateway config, which it does not do today. |
| **A-16** | **Whether "`ask` for every agent" reaches the two agents that hold no browser tool** (round-2 C1). | **RULED as of revision 4 — no, they resolve `deny`.** *(Revision 3 recorded this as DECIDED-overrulable and asked for an ADR erratum. **The erratum has landed**: the consolidated ADR's D2.9 table reads `deny` for Mia and Ava with footnote ¹ spelling out `denyAllThenOverride` plus most-restrictive-wins — this spec's conclusion by this spec's reasoning. The two documents agree; nothing is pending. Consolidated grill **m4**. §15 item 3 is closed. The argument below is retained as the record of how it was reached.)* **The original DECIDED text follows.** The mechanism is settled and re-derived from source (footnote ³ under §11's table): `denyAllThenOverride` writes an explicit agent-level `deny` for every `allStaticToolNames` member, and `resolveEffectivePolicyWith` merges deny-wins, so Mia and Ava resolve **`deny`** unless someone adds a literal override. Revision 2 asserted `ask` for them via a footnote that mistook the **coverage** validator's OR-semantics for a **resolution** rule; the two scenarios that followed (S-25, S-48) contradicted each other and neither state was producible. **Why `deny` is the right reading of the ruling, not merely the convenient one:** the ruling's words are *"`ask` in the GLOBAL tool policy, for every agent — not per-agent, and not `deny` for delegation-tier workers"*, and its stated reasoning is entirely about **unattended delegated work**. Every delegation-tier agent (Explorer, Researcher, Worker) is `ask` under this reading. Mia and Ava are least-privilege chat agents that hold **zero** browser tools and whose seeds route browser work away by design; giving them `ask` would be a real, if small, capability grant — policy is per-tool, so an `ask` there means Mia *can* invoke the tool subject to an approval, which is not something the ruling contemplated. **The ADR erratum this raises, and it needs the ADR's owner:** ADR D2.9's table lists Mia = Ava = `ask` for `browser_upload_file` **while its own next paragraph says "Mia and Ava need no edit at all — `denyAllThenOverride` gives them deny for free"**. Those cannot both hold. Recommended amendment: the table's Mia/Ava cell reads `deny`, with a footnote that the global `ask` is overridden downward by their agent-level default. **The alternative if overruled:** add `"browser_upload_file": ask` to Mia's and Ava's override maps (§11 edit site 6a) and flip S-25, S-48, US-8/AC3, US-12/AC3 and order 4 back. Two lines of Go; no code change beyond seed data. |
| **A-17** | **Whether the gate still needs `CondVisible`, given `chromedp.Click` re-checks visibility right after it** (new; round-2 unasked-question 1). | **DECIDED — keep it, and the duplication is the accepted cost.** Verified: `Click()` appends `NodeVisible`, which issues `dom.GetBoxModel` plus a `Runtime.callFunctionOn` visibility probe **after** the gate returns. Dropping the gate's `CondVisible` would take the fast path from two `Runtime.evaluate` round trips to one and remove a genuinely duplicated check on the hottest path in the change — a real saving. **It is still wrong, because the two checks do different things.** When chromedp's own wait fails, `Selector.Do`'s `s.wait` returns `(false, nil)`, `retryWithSleep` polls to the context deadline, and `sleepContext` returns `ctx.Err()` — a bare `context deadline exceeded` with no condition named. The gate's `CondVisible` is the **only** producer of a named `visible` failure in the system, and naming the unmet condition is ADR criterion 7, the entire point of the gate. **Consequence stated rather than buried:** the gate's cost is **purely additive**, so US-4's "not a tax" is a claim about magnitude (two in-page evaluates), never about the gate replacing work chromedp would otherwise do. Revision 2's framing implied the latter. |
| **A-18** | **What errors an operator sees in `visible_only` mode** (new; round-2 unasked-question 2). | **DECIDED, recorded.** With FR-034's switch at `visible_only` the gate is the pre-change `chromedp.WaitVisible` verbatim, so **`ErrNotActionable` never fires** and SC-003, S-09's oracle and the FR-032 per-condition counters describe the `full` mode only. In `visible_only` the operator sees exactly what they saw before this change: a bare `context deadline exceeded` on a failed wait. **That is not a regression — it is the mode's whole purpose** (restore prior behaviour byte-for-byte when the gate false-fails on a real site), and it is why the switch is **time-boxed**: it trades the named-condition diagnostic away, so leaving it on indefinitely silently gives back ADR criterion 7. **FR-037 is likewise `full`-mode only.** The FR-032 counters keep working in both modes for `indeterminate`, but `gate_failure_total` is necessarily zero in `visible_only`, which the runbook must not read as "no failures". Stated here because an operator flipping the switch during an incident should know the diagnostic goes with it. |
| **B-5** | **`resolveTarget`'s CDP cost is unbudgeted** (new assumption, recorded; round-2 M10). | **Recorded, not measured, in v1 — deliberately, with the reason.** FR-007 budgets `waitActionable` at two round trips and order 14 counts them. The **resolution** seam issues its own traffic and none of it is budgeted: the AX branch runs `QueryAXTree` + `DOM.setAttributeValue` + a cleanup `removeAttribute` per call, and the snapshot path runs `GetFullAXTree`, which B-1 already concedes is unbounded. §2.4 rates `resolveActionSelector`/`resolvePseudoOnlySelector` **HIGH** with a **wider** dependent set than the gate ("click, type, get_text, wait + all six new tools"), so v1 measures the cheaper half of the change and not the more widely used half. **Why not fix it now:** the useful number is not a round-trip count — the AX branch's cost is dominated by tree size, so a fixed count would be satisfied by a call that takes 400 ms on a real page, and a *useful* budget needs the same paired-measurement machinery SC-004 defines and deliberately leaves non-gating. **What would close it:** extend order 14's counting seam over `resolveTarget` as a second, non-gating recorded count (the seam already exists, so the marginal cost is small) and record CSS-branch versus AX-branch numbers side by side in the PR, exactly as SC-004 records the gate's. Filed as v1-deferred; it must not be discovered as a surprise in holdout 10. |
| **B-6** | **The audit-log viewer already breaks on dotted event names** (pre-existing; found while resolving round-2 M6). **Filed as [#667](https://github.com/elicify-ai/omnipus/issues/667), OPEN.** | **Out of scope to FIX, and no longer load-bearing for FR-028 (consolidated grill M6).** *Revision 3 filed this row and, four sections earlier, designated the same viewer as FR-028's operator surface — a mitigation resting on a surface this document documents as blank on most real installs. Revision 4 separates the two: FR-028's **primary** surface is `GET /api/v1/audit-log` and `$OMNIPUS_HOME/system/audit.jsonl`, which #667 does not affect (the failure is client-side, in `performRequest`'s zod validation); the **viewer** is named as the operator-friendly surface **conditional on #667**. #667 is deliberately **not** a blocking prerequisite the way #659 is — a correct, readable audit record ships without it — but the founder-facing half of the ruled mitigation is not delivered until it lands, and §15 item 9 says so.* **The original scope text follows.** `contracts/components/schemas/AuditEntry.yaml` pins `event` to `pattern: '^[a-z_]+$'`; the generated `src/lib/api/generated/schemas.ts::AuditEntry` carries `z.string().regex(/^[a-z_]+$/)`; `AuditLogResponse.entries` is `z.array(AuditEntry)`; and `src/lib/api.ts::performRequest` **throws `ApiSchemaError`** on a `safeParse` failure rather than dropping the offending row. **So one dotted entry blanks the entire Audit Log viewer.** Two shipped emitters already write dotted names into the same `audit.jsonl`: `audit.EventChannelPairing = "channel.pairing"` (`pkg/agent/loop.go:4357`) and `audit.EventCliValidate = "cli.validate"` (`pkg/gateway/rest_clivalidate.go:370`) — so on any install where a channel has been paired or a CLI validated, Settings → Security → Audit Log is already broken. **D2's obligation is narrow and absolute: use underscore-form names** (`browser_snapshot`, `browser_upload_file`), asserted by `TestSnapshot_AuditEventNameMatchesContractPattern` (§10 order 23) and forbidden in §5. **Fixing the pre-existing pair is a separate change** — it is either a contract widening (allow `.` in the pattern, regenerate, re-verify) or two event renames with a log-format consideration, and both need an owner this spec does not have. **Filed as #667; not folded in.** |
| **A-19** | **Whether Jim keeps `browser_evaluate` under the sharing ruling** (new in revision 4; consolidated grill M11, ADR §6). | **RULED 2026-09-01 — ADR D1.9b ruling 2: `browser_evaluate` is ENABLED. `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` is seeded `true`, and Jim's grant stands.** The ruling did not answer the question as posed; **it closed the split this row surfaced, and closed it by making the capability real rather than by hiding it.** After it, the policy surface an operator inspects (`pkg/config/defaults.go:282`, `pkg/coreagent/core.go:1058` — both `allow`) and the runtime gate at `pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:869-872` **agree**, which is the defect this row said was sharper than the grant. **The consequence, stated once and not re-argued:** arbitrary JavaScript becomes **live** for the agent holding that grant, inside a browser that under **D1.10** carries the operator's logins for every site the workspace has visited. A genuine widening; raised before the ruling; the ruling stands. **Release note is mandatory — FR-042/AC5.** **What this document must now carry, and did not before:** the seed is a real edit in Stream E1's own file, so it is numbered (**FR-042**, US-20, S-66/S-67/S-68, §10 order 4a) rather than left as prose; and **every place that described the tool as inert is corrected** (SC-002, §2.1's `EvaluateTool` and Jim rows, §5's non-replication rule, §6's boundary bullet, §11(c), A-4). **The one thing revision 5's "latent, not live" analysis was right about survives inverted:** the flag is still a *different key in a different section* from the policy surface, and there is **still no SPA control for it** — verified, zero occurrences of `browser_evaluate_enabled` under `src/`, and `pkg/sysagent/tools/config.go:322` blocks the whole `sandbox` namespace to agents for writes. So the only off-switch is a hand edit of `config.json`, and **FR-042/AC4 exists because that off-switch does not currently survive a save** (see the FR). §15 item 8 is CLOSED. |
| **A-19a** *(tombstoned split of A-19, retained so the pre-ruling reasoning resolves)* | The pre-ruling analysis, kept verbatim below because §11(c) and A-4 cite it and because the facts it established are what the ruling acted on. | **SUPERSEDED by A-19 above.** ADR §6 asked it: under **D1.10** Jim's arbitrary JS runs against a browser carrying the operator's live logins for every site the workspace has visited, and *"the grant predates the sharing model and has not been re-examined against it."* **Why it is on this spec's map at all:** §11(c) and §12 A-4 both reason *from* the ten-allow/one-deny carve-out as settled precedent, so a reader is entitled to know the premise is under review. **One fact this spec adds that the ADR's question does not carry, and an operator needs before ruling:** the control that actually disables arbitrary JS is **not on the tool-policy surface**. `browser_evaluate` is registered unconditionally and gated at `EvaluateTool.Execute` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:869-872`, *"This is the SOLE live gate"*) by `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` — default `false` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:230-235`; `pkg/config/defaults.go` seeds no value), while both the global policy seed (`defaults.go:282`) and Jim's seed (`core.go:1058`) say `allow`. **So an operator inspecting tool policy sees `allow` and no sign of the flag holding it shut; and an operator who enables the flag for any reason enables it for Jim on every workspace at once.** **D2 changes nothing here** — no new tool replicates the pattern (§5), and this spec neither widens nor narrows the grant. **And the distinction that matters to whoever rules, added in revision 5 after re-verification:** because the flag is unseeded and defaults `false`, **the exposure is LATENT, not live** — on a stock install `browser_evaluate` returns *"disabled — set sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled=true in config to enable"* (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:869-872`) and Jim's `allow` buys him nothing. **So the sharper defect is not the grant; it is the split.** The policy surface an operator inspects says `allow` (`pkg/config/defaults.go:282`, `pkg/coreagent/core.go:1058`), and the control that actually holds the tool shut is a **different key in a different section** (`sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled`, `pkg/config/sandbox.go:230-235`) that is off and invisible from there. Ruling on the grant alone would leave the split intact; ruling on the split — surface the flag's state wherever the policy is shown, or move the gate onto the policy surface — fixes the thing an operator can actually be wrong about. **Whose call:** the ADR's, as its own open question. Routed at §15 item 8. — **RULED: the split was closed the third way, by seeding the flag true so both surfaces say the same thing. See A-19 above. The paragraph is kept unedited because it is the analysis the ruling acted on, and because a reader tracing §11(c) or A-4 back to "latent, not live" needs to find it and then find that it no longer holds.** |
| **A-20** | **What *"take control on request"* looks like to an agent** (new in revision 4; ADR **D1.9a**, left open there). | **RULED 2026-09-01 — ADR D1.9b ruling 1: acquisition is IMPLICIT, and there is no take-control tool.** An agent acquires the operator's shared tab **by acting on it**; the action *is* the request. **Shape A — the explicit `browser_take_tab` this row previously specced and priced — is not built.** **It resolved in the cheap direction, and every figure this row used to move stays where it was:** the surface is **11 → 17**, not 18 (`pkg/tools/browser/register.go::RegisterTools` registers eleven today, and there are six new tools, not seven); **no seventh policy entry in any agent's block**, so no new row in §11's seeded table or in edit sites 1-7, and **no `ValidateToolPolicyCoverage` boot-abort risk** (Hard Constraint #6); the Tier-3 fixture moves **62 → 68** and the arithmetic literal **87 → 93** — and those are **ruling 3's** numbers for the six tools, not this row's, which now contributes nothing to either. No new FR, US, scenario or test. **Stream E1 does not need redoing, and this row's "rule it first" ordering advice is discharged.** **What the ruling rests on, stated because it IS the whole mitigation:** implicit acquisition can only occur while **no human holds the live view** — `controlledResult` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:962`) returns the `{"deferred": true, …}` non-error to every gated tool whenever `LiveViewRegistry.IsControlled` is true (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:963` → `pkg/tools/browser/live.go:1313`; ADR-038 D6). Without that gate this would be a silent takeover. **The cost the ruling knowingly accepts is exactly what this row recorded against Shape B:** there is no moment an operator can deny and no policy entry to inspect, so "the agent took the wheel" and "the agent did its job" are the same event. **The mitigation also has two holes that are this document's own making — recorded at A-22**, which is a consequence of the ruling, not a re-litigation of it. §15 item 7 is CLOSED. |
| **A-22** | **The D1.9b mitigation does not reach the two tools this spec exempts** (new in revision 6; derived from ruling 1, not a dispute with it). | **RECORDED — an honest narrowing of a mitigation stated elsewhere, routed to the ADR.** ADR D1.9b ruling 1 bounds implicit tab acquisition with *"`controlledResult` already defers an agent while a human drives"*. **That holds for eleven tools and fails for two — and both of the two are this document's.** `browser_snapshot` (**FR-038**) and `browser_handle_dialog` (**FR-035**) are deliberately exempt from `controlledResult`: the first because it is read-only, the second because it is the wedge-recovery verb and must work *from* a wedged state. **So while a human is actively driving the operator's shared tab, an agent may still read that tab's full accessible tree — field values included, by ruling (FR-018) — and may still answer a dialog on it**, `{accept:true}` included, because FR-041 refuses `accept:true` only on a turn with `AutoDenyAsk` set and an attended turn has an approver. **Neither exemption should be withdrawn to close this:** removing FR-038's would make a read wait on a human, and removing FR-035's would reintroduce the wedge the whole of Stream C exists to end. **A third fact anyone reasoning from that gate needs:** `controlledResult` tests `mgr.Live().IsControlled(defaultSessionID)` — a **browser-global** flag on a single session id (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:69`, `:963`), not a per-tab one. It cannot tell "a human is driving the operator's tab" from "a human is driving some other tab", so it is coarser in the agent's favour on the one tab the ruling is about and coarser against the agent on every other. **Whose call:** the ADR's, because the mitigation is asserted there. **Nothing in this document changes on the answer** — both exemptions are already numbered, tested (§10 orders 15 and 21) and cross-cited by D1. §15 item 11. **⚠️ Revision 7 correction, round-4 M3: revision 6 left this row as the ONLY home for the finding, in a document whose own rule is that prose is not citable.** The exposure now has **FR-044**, US-22, S-72/S-73, four dataset rows and three tests — so narrowing it later is a deliberate edit to a named test rather than a drift, and a reviewer who disputes it has an assertion to argue with. **One more consequence this row had recorded and not costed (round-4 m1):** the same browser-global flag means the eleven gated tools are **over-deferred** — a human on any one tab defers every agent write on every tab in that browser. Under **D1.9c** (session-owned tabs) that is the common case, not an edge, because a scheduled run and a live chat now hold different tabs in the same browser. **FR-044/AC4 asserts the coarseness; the availability cost of it is measured by holdout 14, not by a requirement.** |
| **A-21** | **Whether an unattended agent may ACCEPT a dialog** (new in revision 4; consolidated grill, unasked question). | **DECIDED — no, and it may always dismiss one. FR-041.** The gap: `browser_handle_dialog` is exempt from both gates (FR-035), the ADR permits it *"even while a human holds control"* (D1.8), Worker inherits the global `allow`, and under **D1.10** the session is the operator's real signed-in one — so nothing stated what stopped a delegated unattended agent accepting `confirm("Delete this account?")`. §11(b)'s three reasons do not cover it, and its reason (iii) — "the operator sees `accept:true` in the chat thread" — is the claim **FR-039 has just disproved for delegated work**. **The control reuses `AutoDenyAsk`** (`pkg/agent/loop.go:599`), the existing "no human can answer on this turn" signal; no second discriminator is introduced, and §5 states why that matters against D1.10. `{accept:false}` is never refused, so the unwedge guarantee is untouched. **Limit, stated:** `AutoDenyAsk` is not inherited by delegated sub-turns today (**#659**), so the control does not engage for exactly the population it is for until #659 lands — the same shape of gap as FR-029's, and one more reason #659 is a prerequisite. **Named alternative if overruled:** drop FR-041 and accept that a delegated Worker can confirm anything on the operator's sessions; the cost of reversing is one guard and three tests. |
| **B-7** | **The preview token now crosses a boundary this spec creates** (new in revision 4; round-3 M11, ADR §6). | **Out of this spec's control, and flagged.** `serve_web` mints `/preview/<agent>/<token>/` per **agent** while the browsing context is per **workspace**. FR-019 points an agent at `serve_web` from the `file://` error and FR-030 grants `serve_web` to three more agents, so D2 makes it materially more likely that a preview minted by one agent is opened in a tab another agent drives. **The token is the credential** (`ADR-044-preview-on-main-listener.md`, FR-023), so whether that is acceptable is a security question, not a routing one. **Not decidable here:** the preview route is ADR-044's and the browsing key is D1's. ADR §6 carries it open. Recorded so it is not discovered after both halves ship. |
| **B-8** | **The `max_tabs` refusal message is D2's, and D1.5a deletes what it says** (new in revision 5; ADR **D1.5a**). | **Recorded, and deliberately not made an FR.** `browser_click` adopts a `target="_blank"` tab through `mgr.ReconcileTabs`, and **`applyReconcileOutcome` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go::applyReconcileOutcome`) renders the outcome** — a D2-owned function. Today its switch turns `tabAdoptReasonMaxTabs` (`manager.go:2105-2108`, set at `manager.go:2216` and `:2286`) into *"the maximum concurrent tabs limit was reached… Close a tab with `browser_close_tab` and retry"* (`tools.go:347-350`). D1.5a deletes the cap, so that arm goes, and every memory-pressure refusal falls to the `default:` arm — *"it could not be adopted"* (`tools.go:354-355`), a message with no reason and no remedy. **Why no FR:** the refusal is D1's requirement and §1 keeps the pool out of scope, so a D2 FR here would put a D2 test on a D1 behaviour. **What it needs instead:** D1 supplies a reason code that names *memory*, and whoever lands it extends this switch rather than merely deleting the `max_tabs` arm. §6's D1.5a bullet, §15 item 2(e). |

---

## 13. Holdout evaluation scenarios (post-implementation, NOT in the TDD plan or the traceability matrix)

1. **(happy)** On a real public site with generated class names, an agent fills a multi-field form end to end using **only** `browser_snapshot` + role/name locators + `browser_select_option` + `browser_press_key{Enter}` — no CSS selector in the whole trace. *(SC-001's first part.)* **Record separately whether the agent found `browser_snapshot` on its own.** It is **Tier 3 — searchable only** (§12 A-3 RULED, ADR D1.9b ruling 3), so the trace must open with a `ToolSearch` the agent has no manifest prompt to perform. **Run it twice: once unprompted, once told to search first.** The unprompted run is the honest measurement of §12 B-9 — if the agent screenshots instead, that is the tier, not a defect in `resolveTarget`, and it is the finding this holdout is for.
2. **(happy)** An agent attaches a real file to a real upload form; the operator sees exactly one `ask` prompt, approves, and the upload completes. **Blocked on #659 (FR-029)** — record it as blocked, not as passing. *(SC-001's second part.)*
3. **(error)** A site fires `confirm()` on navigate-away. The agent hits it, does **not** call `browser_handle_dialog`, and the next browser tool's error tells it what happened and what to call. The agent recovers unaided.
4. **(error)** A cookie banner overlays the page. `browser_click` on the underlying CTA fails naming `hit-testable` **and** the banner element; the agent dismisses the banner and retries successfully — without the operator intervening.
5. **(edge)** A single-page app re-renders between the snapshot and the action. The handle is stale. Assert the failure is a *named* resolution error, not a click on the wrong element.
6. **(edge)** A page with 5,000 AX nodes. Snapshot returns within the page timeout, capped at ≤64,000 bytes on a node boundary, top retained, marker naming the byte cap and omitted-node count. **Record the fetch time separately** — B-1 states the fetch is unbounded, and this is where that gets a number.
7. **~~(edge)~~ WITHDRAWN — this is a D1 holdout (round-2 M4c).** "Two agents on one browsing context both drive a form; the loser's `{"deferred": true}` is visible in the transcript and it waits and retries" exercises the **write lease**, which revision 2 relocated to `browser-workspace-ownership-spec.md` §14. D2 defines no lease and cannot evaluate one. Number retained so 8-11 keep their identifiers. **What D2 *does* hold out on contention is new item 12 below** — the two exemptions, which are D2's.
8. **(edge)** A dialog opens on tab 2 while the operator is watching tab 0 in the live panel. The agent recovers tab 2. **Record what the human sees** — this is the accepted gap in §6, and the holdout exists to measure how bad it is, not to pass.
9. **(edge)** `linux/arm64`, where no Chromium is present (ADR D2.7 / #665). All new tools are registered and fail. Confirm the failure names the missing browser and does not read as a tool bug. **Promoted to FR-033/US-16 with a real test (§10 order 25)** — this holdout is now the *field* confirmation of an automated assertion, not the only coverage (grill m7).
10. **(regression)** With the actionability gate live, run a 10-minute mixed browsing session on real sites and compare per-click latency against the same build with `actionability_gate: visible_only`. **Also record the FR-032 counter values** — this is the run that tells the operator whether the gate is rejecting real clicks, and the data FR-034's time-box is measured against.
11. **(security)** Snapshot a real signed-in page with a partially filled payment form, **from the agent the operator is chatting to**. Confirm the values **are** present (the ruling), the audit event carries only metadata, and the operator can see the tool call in the chat thread. **Then read the audit record two ways:** `GET /api/v1/audit-log` (or the JSONL file) — which must work — **and** Settings → Security → Audit Log, which works only if this install carries no dotted legacy event. **Record which install you were on**, because a blank viewer here is **#667**, not this work. **This is the holdout that shows the accepted risk to a human before it ships.**
13. **(security, new in revision 4 — the case holdout 11 does not cover)** Run the same snapshot **from a delegated sub-agent**, with chat settings left at their defaults. **Confirm the operator sees nothing in the chat thread** — that is FR-039's stated behaviour, so it is a pass, not a bug — then open the Activity panel, expand the span, and confirm the call is there. **Repeat with an external-CLI sub-agent and confirm it is not there either.** *This is the holdout that puts a human in front of the gap the ruling's mitigation does not cover. Its value is the reaction: if an operator watching a delegated agent read a signed-in payment form finds no trace of it in the conversation, that is the input ADR §6's open UI question needs and no code-level test can produce.*
14. **(edge, new in revision 7 — round-4 m1: the cost of the same coarseness A-22 records, measured from the other side)** A human takes the wheel on **one** tab and keeps it for ten minutes while agents work on **other** tabs in the same workspace browser. **Record how many agent writes deferred that had nothing to do with the tab the human was on**, and what the agents did about it. *`controlledResult` reads `IsControlled(defaultSessionID)` — a **browser-global** flag (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:69`, `:963`), so every gated tool on every tab defers. **Under ADR D1.9c this is the ordinary case rather than an edge:** tabs are owned by sessions, so a scheduled run, a delegated sub-turn and a live chat routinely hold different tabs in one browser, and a human touching any of them stops all of them. §12 A-22 records the coarseness as an exposure on the two exempt tools; this holdout measures it as an **availability** cost on the other eleven, which no FR, scenario or criterion measures. **Holdout 12 does not cover it** — it exercises the exempt pair on the tab the human is on. **A fix, if the number is bad, is D1's or ADR-038's, not this spec's** (a per-tab control flag), which is why this is a measurement and not a requirement.*

12. **(edge, new — replaces the withdrawn holdout 7; rescoped in revision 4 by ADR D1.9a, re-scoped again in revision 7 by **D1.9c**)** **On a tab the operator opened** — the only tab the lease reaches now that tabs are owned by the session rather than by the agent — with another action tool genuinely blocked on it, confirm that **`browser_snapshot` and `browser_handle_dialog` still return** (FR-038, FR-035) while the four leased verbs defer. Record what the transcript shows the agent, because "deferred, wait and retry" is only useful behaviour if the agent can tell it apart from a failure. *The deferral half belongs to D1; the two exemptions are D2's, and this is where they are seen working together on a real page rather than on a fixture.*

## 14.3 Disposition of the consolidated three-document grill (2026-09-01)

**The review exists only as chat output** — its own instructions forbade writing files — so there is no round-4 review file in the repo to cite. It returned **4 CRITICAL / 11 MAJOR / 5 MINOR / 4 OBSERVATION** across ADR-072 and both implementation specs. **The rows below are the findings assigned to THIS document**; the rest belong to the ADR and to `browser-workspace-ownership-spec.md` and are not dispositioned here.

**Eight rows: 5 FIXED · 1 RECORDED (not fixable here — it is an ADR edit) · 1 DISCHARGED · 1 ANSWERED.** *One of the five is part fix, part verification — the M1/M8/M9 row: the lease half was a real correction, the pool half was a check that found no dependence, and the row says which is which rather than claiming both as edits.*

> **Revision 6 note on M11, and it is a point about disposition discipline rather than about `browser_evaluate`.** M11 was closed as a **surfacing** — this document recorded the question and the one fact the ADR's version of it lacked, and deliberately declined to decide it. **Within the same day, that surfacing produced a ruling** (ADR D1.9b ruling 2), and the ruling turned on exactly the fact the surfacing added: the split between the policy an operator inspects and the flag that actually gated the tool. **Recorded because "FIXED as a surfacing" reads like a soft disposition and was not one.** The counter-example is item 1 of §15, where this document's *recommendation* was overruled — surfacing the facts and recommending an answer are different acts with different success conditions, and both happened here.

| ID | Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M2** | ADR citations dangle after the renumber — `D1.1a` ×3, `D1.0a` ×2, and two old numbers now resolve to the **wrong** section | **FIXED, by re-derivation not replacement.** Every D1 citation re-read against the current ADR; the old→new map, including the two that mis-resolve silently (`D1.2`, `D1.5`), is **§0d**. D2.0–D2.11 spot-checked and unchanged | §0d, §0b row 13, §1, §6 |
| **M4** | ADR §3.3 criterion 22 and §4 still assert the ≤150 ms p95 budget SC-004 withdrew in full — **and ADR §9 has no section for this spec at all**, so every conflict this document carries is invisible to an ADR reader | **RECORDED, not fixable here — this spec does not edit the ADR.** All four withdrawal defects restated, the conflict given its own top-of-document section, and the structural gap (no ADR §9.5) named as the reason the rest is invisible | **§0c** (AC-1 and its preamble), SC-004's leading warning, edit site 21, §15 item 10 |
| **M5** | The snapshot's chat-render mitigation is asserted unconditionally and pinned by a test that is silent on the span gate — so it is untested exactly where D1.10 widened the risk | **FIXED, and one ADR claim narrowed on evidence.** New **FR-039** / **US-10 AC6** / **S-63** / order **23a** state and test both populations. The parent-thread claim is scoped everywhere it appears; S-43 and its test are renamed and their limit written into the row. **Verified rather than inherited:** a delegated snapshot *is* reachable in the ActivityPanel (native spans only, capped, expand-required), which the ADR says it is not — §0c AC-2 | §2.1, §2.3, §4, §5, §7, §8, §9, §10, §13 holdout 13 |
| **M6** | FR-028's named reading surface is documented by this spec as already blank on real installs | **FIXED.** #667 cited by number throughout; FR-028's **primary** surface is now the endpoint and the JSONL file, which #667 does not affect (the failure is client-side zod validation); the viewer is named as the operator-friendly surface **conditional on #667**. New **US-18 AC4** and a test that seeds a dotted legacy event | §2.1, §2.3, §6.1, §9 FR-028, §12 B-6, §15 item 9 |
| **M11** | Jim's `browser_evaluate` is open in the ADR and invisible here, while this spec reasons *from* the carve-out it belongs to | **FIXED as a surfacing, not a decision** — the ADR owns the question. New **§12 A-19**, a §6 boundary bullet, a §2.1 row, and an explicit note in §11(c) and A-4 that the premise moved. **Adds the fact the ADR lacks:** the disabling control is `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` at `Execute`, **outside the tool-policy surface**, default off, while both seeds say `allow`. **→ CLOSED at revision 6: the surfacing produced a decision.** ADR **D1.9b ruling 2** seeds the flag `true` (**FR-042**), so the two surfaces agree and the tool is live. The surfacing was the right disposition and it worked — this row is the one place in the sheet where "fixed as a surfacing" later became a ruling | §2.1, §6, §11(c), §12 A-4, A-19, **FR-042/US-20/S-66-68**, §15 item 8 (CLOSED) |
| **M1 / M8 / M9 (context)** | Do not depend on D1's old shape: its lease membership rule, its exemption set, and its refusal-vs-eviction model | **FIXED for the lease; CHECKED, no dependence found, for the pool.** *(The pool half is a verification, not an edit: this document has no `errBrowserPoolFull`, no refusal FR and no pool acceptance criterion — the pool is out of scope at §1 and stays there. **One consequence of the eviction model does land on D2's own state and is now named:** a whole-Chrome teardown — idle close, eviction, crash recovery — is a teardown site for the `sessionEntry` dialog state D2 introduces, and edit site 15's "exhaustive" list omitted it. Added there; round-3 **M7**.)* **Re-run in revision 5 against ADR D1.5a, and narrowed: the check was right about the requirements and wrong about the prose.** With every counter deleted the pool has no cap at all, and this document still has no `errBrowserPoolFull`, no refusal FR and no pool acceptance criterion — that half stands. **What it missed is a rendered message:** `applyReconcileOutcome` (`tools.go::applyReconcileOutcome`) is D2-owned and turns the adoption path's `max_tabs` refusal into agent-facing text, so the deletion does reach D2's code — as a sentence, not a requirement. Recorded at §12 **B-8**, §6's D1.5a bullet and §15 item 2(e). **The lease half:** §3 no longer restates the *"mutates page or tab state, enforced against the registry"* derivation D1 discarded; it quotes D1's biconditional (*lease iff `controlledResult`-gated*) and counts the gated set **from source** — seven call sites, listed with `file:line` — rather than from either spec's prose. **And the inclusions get a number:** new **FR-040** / **US-19** / **S-64** / order **24a**, because D1's test 18 depends on D2's wiring and revision 3 carried it as prose only | §3, §4, §5, §7, §9, §10, §15 item 2 |
| **m4** | One "outstanding ADR edit" is already discharged | **DISCHARGED.** The ADR's D2.9 table reads `deny` for Mia and Ava with footnote ¹. **§12 A-16 is RULED**, §15 item 3 is closed, edit site 21 splits into 2 discharged / 2 outstanding | §11 fn³, §12 A-16, edit site 6a, edit site 21, §15 item 3 |
| **Unasked question** | With `browser_handle_dialog` exempt from both gates and permitted *"even while a human holds control"*, what stops a delegated unattended agent accepting `confirm("Delete this account?")` on the operator's signed-in session? | **ANSWERED — nothing did, and now FR-041 does.** `{accept:true}` is refused on a turn with `AutoDenyAsk` set; `{accept:false}` is never refused, so the unwedge guarantee is untouched. Uses the **existing** signal, so no second attended/unattended discriminator is introduced against D1.10. **Limit stated:** it does not engage for a delegated turn until **#659** lands | §11(b), §12 A-21, US-9 AC10, S-65, order 15, §9 FR-041 |

**Operator ruling 2 of the brief (audit is per action for write-class tools, ADR D2.11) — checked, no change needed.** This spec's two audit events fire **per invocation**: FR-031 *"on **every** invocation including denials"* (§6.1) and FR-028 *"every invocation"*. Neither is first-use-only, and nothing in §9, §10 or §11 assumes the weaker form. Recorded so the check is not re-run.
