## 15. What still needs an operator ruling before implementation

**Zero genuinely open rulings, down from three.** Items **1**, **7** and **8** were all ruled on 2026-09-01 by ADR-072 **D1.9b** and are retained as closed rows rather than deleted, each recording **which shape was chosen and which was not** — item 1 in particular went *against* this document's recommendation, and a deleted row would hide that. Item 3 was already closed at revision 4. **One new item, 11, is added and it is not a request for a ruling from the operator: it is a narrowing of a mitigation the ADR asserts, routed to the ADR's owner.** Numbered 1–11 across all categories so nothing is lost between them.

### Closed by ADR-072 D1.9b (2026-09-01) — retained, not deleted

**~~1. §12 A-3 — `browser_snapshot`'s manifest tier.~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-01 by ADR D1.9b ruling 3. Nothing is asked of the operator here.** The ruling is **Tier 3 — searchable only**, i.e. this document's **Option A**, not the Option B it recommended. **The recommendation was not adopted, and this row says so rather than quietly reading as though it was.**
   - **The governance question dissolved rather than being answered.** Option B would have amended **ADR-071's** previewed set and therefore needed ADR-071's ratifier, whom this document could not name. Option A leaves that set intact at seven, so **no other accepted ADR is amended and no second ratifier is needed.**
   - **What it costs: nothing in production.** Tier 3 is the residual. The edits are the fixture (**62 → 68**), the arithmetic literals (**union 87 → 93**; `17`/`7`/`1` unchanged) and FR-036's new partition test. **`previewedLazyToolNames` and `TestVisibility_PreviewedSetIsExactlySeven` are untouched — and the latter passing unedited is the assertion that the ruling was applied.** Edit sites 11 and 12 are struck. **Stream E2 is unblocked**, and no stream now waits on any ruling.
   - **What it costs in the product, stated and not softened.** *A tool an agent must search for is not a default.* **ADR D2.4's "the default way an agent reads a page" was false, and the ADR's correction is a banner rather than a retraction** — `356e587d3` adds eight lines and deletes none, so the Decision sentence, D2.8's *"open question"* tier row and ADR §6's bullet all still read the old way (**§0c AC-5**, and three ADR edits added to item 10). **Agents will keep reaching for `browser_screenshot`, because that is what is in front of them** — the better mechanism exists and is not the path of least resistance. Recorded as **§12 B-9**, carried into SC-001 and holdout 1 so a failed role/name-only demonstration is read as the tier rather than as a defect in `resolveTarget`.
   - **Revision 3's promise held and was re-checked, not assumed.** No FR, scenario, dataset row or success criterion named a tier for the snapshot, so applying the ruling required no requirement to change — only literals, edit sites and the honest consequence.

**~~7. §12 A-20 — what *"take control on request"* is, in the tool surface~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-01 by ADR D1.9b ruling 1. Nothing is asked of the operator here.** The ruling is **IMPLICIT acquisition: there is no take-control tool.** An agent takes the operator's shared tab by acting on it. **The expensive shape was not chosen, so none of the costs this item was raised to avoid are incurred:** the surface stays **11 → 17**, no agent gains a seventh policy entry (so Hard Constraint #6's boot-abort risk never arises), §11's seeded table and edit sites 1-7 are unchanged, and the Tier-3 fixture and arithmetic literals move only by ruling 3's six tools. **Stream E1 was never actually blocked and does not need redoing** — the ordering advice this item carried is withdrawn, not merely satisfied. **The mitigation the ruling names is `controlledResult`** (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:962`), which defers a gated agent while a human holds the live view. **One derived finding is left open and it is the ADR's, not the operator's to rule here: §15 item 11 / §12 A-22** — that gate does not reach `browser_snapshot` or `browser_handle_dialog`, both exempt by this spec's own FR-038 and FR-035.

**~~8. §12 A-19 — does Jim keep `browser_evaluate` under the sharing ruling?~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-01 by ADR D1.9b ruling 2. Not the answer this item anticipated.** The question was whether the grant survives; **the ruling instead closed the split this document surfaced, and did it by making the capability real.** `sandbox.browser_evaluate_enabled` is seeded **true** (**FR-042**, new in revision 6), so the policy surface that reads `allow` (`pkg/config/defaults.go:282`, `pkg/coreagent/core.go:1058`) and the runtime gate that held the tool shut (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:869-872`) now agree. Jim's grant stands.
   - **The consequence, stated once here and not re-argued anywhere in this document:** arbitrary JavaScript becomes **live** for the agent holding that grant, inside a browser that under **D1.10** carries the operator's logins for every site the workspace has visited. That is a genuine widening. It was raised before the ruling — this document raised it — and the ruling stands. **It ships with a release note (FR-042/AC5).**
   - **What it does change in this spec, and it is more than revision 5 predicted.** Revision 5 said "no D2 FR, seed value or test changes on either answer". That was true of a *revocation*; it is **false of an enablement**. The seed is a real edit in Stream E1's own file (`pkg/config/defaults.go`), so it gets a number: **FR-042**, US-20, S-66/S-67/S-68, §10 order 4a. §11(c)'s and §12 A-4's reasoning is re-checked in place: both argue from a property of the five new verbs, not from Jim's grant, so **both survive intact** — but the carve-out they cite is now **ten allow / one allow-and-live**, not ten allow / one deliberately-inert, and the text says so.

### Cross-spec — D1 must act, and D2 cannot

2. **D1 §14.2 rule 3's exemption set is D1's to settle, and it is not settled.** *(Rewritten in revision 3 — round-2 **M1** was right that revision 2's version was stale, and the correct disposition is "downgraded", not "closed".)*
   **What changed:** commit `335d56fe` — whose own subject is *"widen D1's lease exemption to five"* — amended `browser-workspace-ownership-spec.md` §14.2 rule 3 to *"a closed, named set of **five**"*, adding `browser_snapshot` and `browser_handle_dialog` with rows citing D2. Verified at HEAD. Revision 2 quoted the superseded "three" text and declared D2's Streams C and D blocked on it, which would have cost a round trip and invited a duplicate amendment.
   **Re-verified against D1 at HEAD for revision 4: two of the three defects are FIXED, one survives, and a new obligation is added by D1.9a.**
   - **(a) The exemption set — FIXED at HEAD.** D1's `FR-019a` and its US-9/AC4 now read *"the exempt set is **six** … four read-only tools shipped today — `browser_screenshot`, `browser_get_text`, `browser_wait`, **`browser_list_tabs`** — plus `browser_snapshot` … and `browser_handle_dialog`"*, and AC5 states the biconditional. Revision 3 recorded this as in-flight and uncommitted; it has landed.
   - **(b) D1's disposition table — FIXED at HEAD.** Its MAJ-008 row now reads *"a closed set of SIX, not three"* and its MAJ-101 row records the four-different-figures defect and its correction. The number no longer disagrees with itself.
   - **(c) D1 cites the wrong D2 requirement for the snapshot — STILL OPEN.** D1's US-9/AC4 still reads *"`browser_snapshot` (read-only, **D2 FR-018**)"* — which is the *values-by-default* requirement and says nothing about the lease. **Revision 3 created FR-038 precisely so there is a correct citation, and D1 has not taken it.**
     > **⚠️ Disambiguation, because these two documents have collided on a number.** **D1 has its own FR-038** — a `max_browsers` cap on the browser pool, unrelated to anything here. *(**ADR D1.5a deletes `max_browsers` outright**, so D1's FR-038 is expected to be tombstoned or rewritten. **That does not remove the need for the prefix**, and it is worth saying why: a tombstoned number is still a number, and a rewritten one is a different requirement under the same label — either way a bare `FR-038` read inside D1 resolves to D1's, not to this document's. This spec does not predict which D1 will do.)* The citation D1 needs is therefore **"D2 FR-038"**, written with the document prefix, exactly as it already writes "D2 FR-035". A bare `FR-038` inside D1 means D1's own cap requirement and would be actively misleading. **This spec does not renumber to dodge the collision** — FR numbers here are cited by both grills and by D1, and the no-renumbering rule is what keeps every cross-reference resolving; the prefix is the fix.
   - **(d) NEW — D1 §14 must be rescoped to what D1.9c left of the lease.** *(Written against D1.9a in revision 4; **D1.9c supersedes it** and the obligation is unchanged in shape.)* The operator ruled on 2026-09-02 that **tabs are owned by the session, not the agent**, and only the **operator's** tab is shared, so two sessions on their own tabs do not contend and the lease engages **only on that one shared tab**. D1's §14 is written for the general "two writers, one browsing context" case that ruling removes. **D2 has already narrowed its own wording** (§3, §4, FR-035, FR-038, US-9/AC7, US-10/AC5, holdout 12) and must not run ahead of D1's revision. The ADR itself says the rescope is *"a much smaller change than either answer implied"* — the retry-vs-defer question D1 was answering now applies to one tab, not to every action tool.

   - **(e) NEW — D1.5a puts the pressure check in the tab-open path, and D2 renders its refusal.** The operator ruled (2026-09-01) that memory is the only admission control and that the check **must** sit in the tab-open path, not only the browser-launch path. `browser_open_tab` is D1's. **`browser_click` is not** — a `target="_blank"` click adopts a new tab through `mgr.ReconcileTabs`, and the outcome reaches the agent through **`applyReconcileOutcome` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go::applyReconcileOutcome`), a D2-owned function**. Its `tabAdoptReasonMaxTabs` arm (`tools.go:347-350`, fed by `manager.go:2216` / `:2286` via the code at `manager.go:2105-2108`) dies with `max_tabs`, and its `default:` arm — *"it could not be adopted"* (`tools.go:354-355`) — is where every pressure refusal will otherwise land. **What D1 must supply:** a refusal that carries a reason code naming *memory*, so the message the agent reads can say which constraint bound and what to do about it. **What D2 will do with it:** extend that switch, in the same change. **Neither half works alone**, and this is not covered by (a)-(d). §12 B-8, §6's D1.5a bullet.

   - **(f) NEW in revision 7 — D1.9c's residual: can two concurrent turns share one session?** The ruling moves tab ownership from the agent to the **session**, which closes the two-concurrent-turns-of-one-agent hole by construction (a heartbeat during a live chat, and parallel delegation of the same agent, are different sessions). **The ADR states the residual rather than assuming it away, and assigns it: *"whether that can occur is a session-serialisation question, not a browser one — the spec must establish the answer from code rather than inherit this ADR's assumption, and if it can occur, §14's lease applies to the session's tab set."*** **That spec is D1's, not this one.** D2 owns no tab-ownership key, no lease and no session-serialisation behaviour, so it states no requirement here and writes no test — **and deliberately does not assert that the case is impossible**, which is the assumption the ADR asks someone to check. Recorded so that a reader who finds this document silent on it can tell silence-by-scope from silence-by-oversight.

   **Required of D1, then:** re-point the snapshot citation at **D2 FR-038**, rescope §14 to the operator's shared tab per D1.9c (**and settle (f)**), **and — new in revision 5 — give the tab-open pressure refusal a reason code D2's message switch can branch on (e)**. **D2's obligation is now complete on both sides of the boundary** — it states both exemptions (FR-035, FR-038) and, new in revision 4, **wires and tests the four inclusions (FR-040)**, which is what D1's own registry-driven test 18 reads to decide membership. *(Round-2 M7's ordering constraint is separate and lives in §3's Parallelization: D1's `leaseWrite` must exist before S-31b can be written, and S-31a covers the wedge-recovery half meanwhile.)*

### Decided here, and cheap to reverse — flagged so the operator can overrule

3. **~~§12 A-16 — `browser_upload_file` resolves `deny` for Mia and Ava~~ — CLOSED in revision 4. Nothing is asked of the operator here.** Revision 3 raised this because the ADR's D2.9 table said `ask` for both agents while this spec derived `deny`, and asked for an ADR erratum either way. **The erratum has landed:** the consolidated ADR's D2.9 table reads `deny`¹ for Mia and Ava, and its footnote ¹ states the mechanism — `denyAllThenOverride`'s explicit agent-level `deny` plus `resolveEffectivePolicyWith`'s most-restrictive-wins — reaching this spec's conclusion by this spec's reasoning. **§12 A-16 is RULED.** Consolidated grill **m4**. The reversal cost is still recorded at edit site 6a for a future operator who wants it; it is not a pending decision.
4. **§12 A-9 — `FSOpWrite` for `browser_upload_file`.** The named alternative is `FSOpSend` alone with tool policy as the sole gate. Reversing is a one-line change plus a test-fixture path.
5. **§12 A-12 — `browser_handle_dialog: allow` with `accept` defaulting to `false`.** Reversing to `ask` for the research agents is a seed-map edit with no code change.

### Outside this spec's control, and must not be forgotten

6. **Issue #659** (`AutoDenyAsk` not inherited by delegated subagents; OPEN, `priority:P1-high`, `area:agent-loop`). FR-029 holds `browser_upload_file`'s registration until it lands. **Two things ride on it, and revision 3 promotes the second from a note to a stated prerequisite (round-2 unasked-question 5):**
   - **(a) the inheritance itself** — without it a delegated worker hitting an `ask` blocks on an approval nobody can answer. §10 order 0b is the oracle, skipped until #659 closes.
   - **(b) the approval prompt's *content*** — the resolved path and the target origin. Both are already in FR-031's audit fields, so the data exists; whether the prompt renders them is an approval-flow change this spec does not own. **An `ask` that names only the tool is a yes/no with no facts, which is not the control the operator ruled for.** This spec cannot bind #659's scope: if (b) is declined there, it must be declined **explicitly**, and §12 B-3 updated to record that the `ask` ships without facts.
   - **(c) NEW in revision 4 — a third thing now rides on #659: FR-041's unattended-`accept` guard.** It reads `AutoDenyAsk` to decide there is nobody to ask, and `AutoDenyAsk` is not inherited by delegated sub-turns. So until #659 lands, the guard does not engage for a delegated `Worker`, which is exactly the agent it exists for. Recorded as an honest gap of the same shape as (a)'s, and one more reason #659 is a prerequisite for this work rather than adjacent to it.

**9. Issue [#667](https://github.com/elicify-ai/omnipus/issues/667)** (`Audit Log screen blanks entirely when history contains channel.pairing or cli.validate`; OPEN). **Not a blocking prerequisite, and stated so deliberately.** FR-028's audit record is correct and readable without it, via `GET /api/v1/audit-log` and `$OMNIPUS_HOME/system/audit.jsonl` — #667 is a client-side validation failure in `performRequest`, not a server-side or storage one. **But the founder-facing half of a mitigation the operator ruled non-optional is not delivered until #667 lands**, because a JSONL file is a surface for an on-call operator and a script, not for the person the ruling was made for. D2's own obligation is narrow and met: underscore-form names, asserted against the contract regex (S-42), so **D2 does not add a third dotted emitter**. Holdout 11 records which kind of install it was run on, so a blank viewer is read as #667 rather than filed against this work.

**11. §12 A-22 — the D1.9b mitigation does not reach `browser_snapshot` or `browser_handle_dialog`** (new in revision 6; **given a requirement of its own in revision 7 — FR-044, US-22, S-72/S-73, round-4 M3**). **Not a request for a ruling this spec is blocked on; a correction owed to a mitigation stated in the ADR.** D1.9b ruling 1 makes tab acquisition implicit and bounds the exposure with *"`controlledResult` (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:962`) already defers an agent while a human drives (ADR-038 D6)"*. **That holds for eleven tools and fails for two, and both are this document's own exemptions:** `browser_snapshot` (**FR-038**, read-only) and `browser_handle_dialog` (**FR-035**, the wedge-recovery verb). So while a human is actively driving the operator's shared tab, an agent may still read that tab's full accessible tree — **field values included, by the operator's own ruling (FR-018)** — and may still answer a dialog on it, `{accept:true}` included on an attended turn, since **FR-041** refuses `accept:true` only when `AutoDenyAsk` is set.
   - **Neither exemption should be withdrawn to close this**, and that is the reason it needs an owner rather than a fix here: removing FR-038's makes a read wait on a human, and removing FR-035's reintroduces the wedge the whole of Stream C exists to end. The remedy, if one is wanted, is at the ADR's level — narrow the mitigation's wording, or accept the two tools as a stated exception.
   - **A third fact anyone reasoning from that gate needs.** `controlledResult` tests `mgr.Live().IsControlled(defaultSessionID)` — a **browser-global** flag on a single session id (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:69`, `:963`), not a per-tab one. It cannot distinguish "a human is driving the operator's tab" from "a human is driving some other tab": coarser in the agent's favour on the one tab the ruling is about, coarser against it everywhere else.
   - **What it blocks in this spec: nothing** — and as of revision 7 it is no longer carried in prose alone. Both exemptions were already numbered and tested (§10 orders 15 and 21) and cross-cited by D1; **FR-044 now additionally asserts the exposure itself** — a snapshot with values while a human drives (S-72), and an accepted `{accept:true}` on an attended turn (S-73) — so if the ADR narrows the mitigation, the tests that must change are named. **A second consequence of the same coarse flag is recorded rather than fixed (round-4 m1):** `IsControlled` being browser-global also **over-defers** the eleven gated tools — one human on one tab defers every agent write in that browser, which under **D1.9c**'s session-owned tabs is now the ordinary case. FR-044/AC4 asserts it; **holdout 14** measures what it costs.

**10. ADR-072 needs SIX edits this spec cannot make** (§0c, edit site 21) — **up from two outstanding, because revision 7 found that one "discharged" correction was a banner rather than a retraction.** Two remain **discharged** (the `types.go` line number; the D2.9 Mia/Ava cell). **Six are outstanding:** *(a)* **§3.3 criterion 22 and the §4 bullet still assert the ≤150 ms p95 budget SC-004 withdrew in full**, so an implementer reading the ADR will build a gate this spec has no test for; *(b)* **D2.11's "a `browser_snapshot` never appears there" is right for a parent-turn snapshot and wrong for a delegated one**; *(c)* **D2.4's Decision sentence still reads "as the default way an agent reads a page"** — `356e587d3` added a correction banner above it and deleted nothing (`8 insertions(+)`, 0 deletions, re-derived), so the section now asserts both things and the false one is the one an implementer reaches first; *(d)* **D2.8's tier table still lists `browser_snapshot` as "open question — §6"**, offering the Tier-2 route that D1.9b ruling 3 declined and that this spec's struck edit sites 11-12 depend on being closed; *(e)* **ADR §6's open-question list still asks "Is `browser_snapshot` Tier 3?"** — a ruled question presented as open. **And one structural edit that makes the others trackable: an ADR §9.5 for this document.** ADR §9 lists the D1 spec, #509, ADR-048 and root `CLAUDE.md` — **not this spec** — so every conflict recorded in §0c is currently invisible to an ADR reader. **All ADR owner's calls.** *(c)-(e) are §0c **AC-5**, round-4 **M1**.*

---

**Next:** re-grill this revision, then implement — **there is nothing left to rule.** A-3, A-19 and A-20 were all closed by ADR-072 **D1.9b** on 2026-09-01 (§0f), so the ruling-ordering advice earlier revisions carried is retired rather than merely satisfied. Sequence: **Stream E1 first** (names + policy + the FR-042 seed and its `*bool` fix, no behaviour), then A as the critical path, then B/C/D/F in parallel, **E2 alongside** (no longer gated), then the 7-reviewer gate, then SC-001 as the headline demonstration (part 2 blocked on **#659**). **The one prerequisite that still binds is #659**, and it now carries three things, not two — FR-029's registration hold, FR-041's unattended-`accept` guard, and order 0b's oracle.

**Two items are routed outward rather than closed, and neither blocks implementation:** **§15 item 11 / §12 A-22** (the D1.9b mitigation does not reach `browser_snapshot` or `browser_handle_dialog` — the ADR's wording to narrow) and **§15 item 10** (now **six** outstanding ADR edits, not two: the ≤150 ms budget, the D2.11 absolute, and — new in revision 7 — D2.4's uncorrected Decision sentence, D2.8's "open question" tier row and ADR §6's stale bullet, plus the §9.5 that makes any of it trackable. **`356e587d3` corrected D2.4 with a banner and no deletion**, which this document had read as a retraction — §0c AC-5).

**Seven things a re-grill should check first**, because they are where judgement was spent rather than merely edits. *(Items 1-4 are revision 4's; item 5 is revision 5's; items 6-7 are revision 6's. Item 3 is retained as a **discharged** row rather than deleted — the question it posed was ruled, and the row records what the ruling avoided.)*

1. **§2.1's ActivityPanel row and FR-039** — revision 4 **narrows a claim the ADR makes** (that a `browser_snapshot` never reaches the ActivityPanel) on the grounds that a *delegated* one does, inside its span's step list. The evidence is three files and five line references and anyone can repeat it. **If that reading is wrong, FR-039's third and fourth assertions, §0c AC-2 and holdout 13 all move together** — and this document will have added a row to the ADR's §8 pattern rather than avoided one.
2. **§3's lease-membership block and FR-040** — the count of `controlledResult` call sites (**seven**) is derived from source here rather than from either spec's prose, and it disagrees with a casual reading of the brief that produced this revision. It agrees with D1 at HEAD. Check the seven `file:line` citations before trusting either document's number.
3. **~~§12 A-20~~ — DISCHARGED by ADR D1.9b ruling 1: acquisition is implicit, there is no seventh tool.** Nothing is left to check on the cost side, because the cost is not incurred. *Retained because the row is what makes the ruling's cheapness legible: the shape that was avoided would have moved the surface to 18, the fixture to 69, the union to 94, and would have added a policy entry to every seeded agent block on pain of a boot abort.* **What a reviewer should check instead is item 6.**
4. **§12 A-21 / FR-041** — the unattended-`accept` guard reuses `AutoDenyAsk` specifically to avoid introducing a second attended/unattended discriminator against ADR D1.10. Confirm that reading of D1.10 (it deletes the split for *browsing-session isolation*, not for *approvals*, where D2.9 relies on `AutoDenyAsk` by name). If D1.10 is meant more broadly, FR-041 needs a different signal, not a smaller scope.
5. **§12 B-8 / §6's D1.5a bullet — the decision NOT to write an FR** (revision 5's one judgement call). D1.5a's counter deletion reaches exactly one thing this document owns: the sentence `applyReconcileOutcome` shows an agent when an adopted tab is refused. This revision records it as an assumption and routes the requirement to D1 (§15 item 2(e)) rather than numbering it here, on the grounds that §1 puts the pool out of scope and a D2 FR would put a D2 test on a D1 behaviour. **Check the boundary, not the prose:** if the reviewer thinks the *message* is independently testable against D2 code alone — it plausibly is, `applyReconcileOutcome` is already unit-tested without Chrome (`tabs_test.go`'s `TestApplyReconcileOutcome_*`) — then B-8 should become an FR with a scenario and a test, and this revision was one step too cautious. That is the single call worth overturning here.
6. **§12 A-22 / §15 item 11 — the claim that D1.9b ruling 1's mitigation has two holes** (revision 6's first judgement call, and the one that contradicts an ADR sentence). The ADR says `controlledResult` bounds implicit tab acquisition; this document says it does not reach `browser_snapshot` (FR-038) or `browser_handle_dialog` (FR-035), because **this spec exempts them by number**. **Check the exemptions, not the prose:** if either tool is in fact `controlledResult`-gated in the design as written, A-22 is wrong and §15 item 11 should be withdrawn. Then check the narrower half — `IsControlled(defaultSessionID)` being **browser-global** rather than per-tab (`pkg/tools/browser/tools.go:69`, `:963`) — because if it is per-tab in some path this document has not read, the mitigation is tighter than A-22 claims.
7. **FR-042/AC4 and S-68 — the claim that seeding `browser_evaluate_enabled` true creates a persistence defect** (revision 6's second judgement call). The chain is four hops and every one is checkable: `DefaultConfig()` seeds `true`; `loadConfig` (`pkg/config/migration.go:19`) unmarshals the operator's JSON over it, so an explicit `false` wins **on load**; `SaveConfig` (`pkg/config/config.go:4388`) marshals the struct at `:4413`; and the field is `BrowserEvaluateEnabled bool \`json:"…,omitempty"\`` (`pkg/config/sandbox.go:235`), so a `false` is **omitted** and the next load re-applies the seed. **If any hop is wrong, S-68 should not ship** — and if all four hold, note that the defect did not exist before the ruling, because `false` and absent meant the same thing while the seed was absent. The proposed `*bool` matches `PathGuardAuditFailClosed` seven lines above it (`sandbox.go:228`), which is evidence the pattern is already the house style for exactly this problem, not an invention.

---

## Relationship to issue #509 / ADR-048 Option B — MUST be closed when this lands

**This work implements what #509 describes.** That issue — *"Per-agent browser
isolation compatible with WebRTC capture (ADR-048 Option B)"* — specifies
"one Chrome instance (own user-data-dir) per browser-capable agent, so each
agent's tabs sit in that instance's own default context (capturable) while
remaining isolated". That is this design, with **workspace** where #509 says
**agent**.

**#509 was closed `NOT_PLANNED` on 2026-08-19.** This work revives a
deliberately declined option, which is a reversal that must be visible rather
than implicit.

**Required actions, both of them:**

1. **Reopen #509 now** (or file a successor referencing it) so the trail is
   honest while this is built. A design that silently revives a not-planned
   issue leaves the next reader unable to tell whether the decline still holds.
2. **Close #509 when this lands**, citing the implementing PR. It is the
   issue this work completes, and leaving it closed-as-not-planned after
   shipping the thing it asked for makes the tracker lie in the other
   direction.

**One piece of evidence from #509 that strengthens this design and was not
previously recorded here:** CDP-created browser contexts fail capture for
**two** independent reasons, not one — `chrome.tabCapture` returns "Invalid tab
specified", **and** `chrome-extension://` pages will not load in them at all
(`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`). Both verified on real Chrome 150 (commit
`687c7c6e`). The second reason is the more final of the two.

**And one existing constraint #509 records that this design must reconcile
with:** today's v1 is "fenced to effectively single-browser-agent use — capture
start is denied when another agent has live tabs". A pool of N browsers changes
that fence's meaning. **Not yet verified against code in this document** — the
adjacent comment is `pkg/tools/browser/capture_session.go:839`; the precise
enforcement path must be found and reconciled before implementation.
